# Tesbo ASER — Independent assurance rerun and existing-spec reconciliation

**User-facing date:** 18 November 2026. **Run:** ASER-RERUN-2026-11-18. **Scope:** the existing R-ASE-01–48 integration, its governed-skills and human-facing delivery contracts, the original QA task/report/retest loop, and the same eight canonical files. This updates existing specifications and review records; it implements no product capability.

**Final White verdict: NOT FROZEN. The bounded v3 proposed-contract remediation is accepted; no further material document correction is required by this closeout.** The authorized documentation delivery may proceed with its proposed/NOT FROZEN status. Graph is CLEAN only for the bounded manual proposed-contract ownership, traceability and evidence-accounting scope. Implementation/full-journey claims remain NOT CLEAN/BLOCKED on retained source failures and missing runtime, authority and authentic UI evidence. The exact sealed receipts below control their own scope; no analytical approval is a human operational decision.

## Outcome and product meaning

Tesbo remains a shared platform for QA, BA, developers, PM, policy/security, operations/support and people coding with agents. Its purpose is to make the application's expected behavior, actual changes, attributable agent activity, verification and remaining decisions understandable, while supporting faster evidence-backed release. Coding tools are complementary clients; Qase is a closer QA-category benchmark. Neither competitor features nor generated documentation prove that Tesbo improves outcomes.

The intended workflow is explicit in canonical 02 ASER-UX01: developer or agent changes behavior → Tesbo MCP obtains the shared project/change context → assess applicable Requirements, Test Cases, User Manual, Policies/Decisions, Tasks/Issues and Files/Evidence → versioned traceability, actual verification and authorized human approval. Skills are cross-cutting authoring governance; a change does not have to fabricate six artifact mutations or a skill update.

The original QA path is independently retained: ChatGPT prompt → Tesbo task → assigned OpenCode/other executor using its own authorized Chrome/Playwright/Appium/API driver → immutable original report/evidence → Tesbo/ChatGPT analysis → linked retest → exact human review and selected atomic apply. I3-Q / ASE-INT-12 / QA-135–138 protect the case-free, no-native-model-key path. A code-change demo cannot substitute for it.

Actual machine/human principal, semantic definition/dependency revisions, exact decision and run eligibility remain separate. | Existing source writer/run-gate concerns need implementation and allowed/denied native tests. |

## Review execution and evidence limits

The unchanged methodology is `NesberryEnterprise/Nesberry@d83549c85a4dcd047fb40dc4fba4ec62737ab0fc`, applied with Tesbo's own policies, source and product scope. Blue, Red, Grey/SUJT, White, Green, Security, Purple, QA and Elephant-Gold are the nine specialist roles; Graph Assurance is the separate closeout function, not a substitute specialist or a new product subsystem. Simulation is Grey's declared activity.

Initial invocations used separate cold agent contexts (`fork_turns=none`); later deltas are explicitly informed reviews. All agents use the same model family. Shared primary files and some source-cache selection limit independence; this is not cross-model assurance or independent human operational approval. A separate cold-prepared additional member participates under the existing Blue/Red/Green membership rules, with actual final dispositions recorded separately. No quorum is inferred from naming two roles.

Sequence: actual Blue/Red receipts → Grey Gate C → White pre-gate → bounded author corrections → applicable specialist/independent-member deltas → Graph closeout → White final. White's original pre-gate was BLOCKED—DUPLICATE/UNCLEAR OWNERSHIP and expressly permitted bounded in-place specification remediation while withholding freeze/implementation admission. It is preserved unchanged, not rewritten as a historical pass.

Initial Grey inventory: 49 journeys (43 high-risk, 6 medium), 37 G0 / 12 G1; 24 HOLDS, 21 INCONCLUSIVE, 4 FAILS. High-risk subset: 33 G0 / 10 G1. These are bounded design/source simulation outcomes, with zero product runtime tests. QA independently challenged the three Major mechanism families; their original and later contract dispositions remain separate. Grey’s later frozen-v3 delta reconsidered all 49 obligations at bounded G0 level: 31 HOLDS, 18 INCONCLUSIVE, 0 contract FAILS, with the design verdict JOURNEYS HOLD WITH FRICTION. This is a different, explicitly scoped post-correction analysis, not a conversion of old failures into executed test passes. Grey's initial bounded partial-retry HOLDS and Elephant-Gold's precise lifecycle objection are both preserved rather than erasing the disagreement.

## Findings still open beyond specification correction

1. **Current native enforcement:** all case/status/dependency writers, preserved machine identity, exact run eligibility, P-18 on both MCP aliases/discovery/cached clients, and Bug-link side effects. Documented mitigations are not applied code.
2. **Storage and historical truth:** immutable governed submission/acceptance, binding-generation migration, complete relevant reader/cache/projection inventory, historical unknowns, retention, restore and allowed/denied readback.
3. **Actual authority and operational choices:** root bootstrap/recovery/quorum, real role-to-capability policy, emergency exceptions, first-release package/provider capabilities and measured resource/SLO/retry budgets. Reviewer agents do not appoint themselves operational approvers.
4. **End-to-end execution:** original QA loop, source-triggered synchronization, exact tested/deployed build, denied paths, concurrency, uncertain writes, queues, recovery, file bytes and client interoperability in an authorized disposable fixture.
5. **Authentic UI and user outcomes:** real current baseline and after-state, keyboard/AT/responsive behavior, stale/denied/unavailable recovery, human understanding and empirical delivery benefit.
6. **Adjacent instruction reference drift:** `AGENTS.md:57` associates five future AI directions with FR-106–110/T-448–453, while canonical 1.4.27 assigns those IDs to guidance/pack work. The canonical 06 amendment expressly prevents adopting older branch shorthand as a new definition. The AGENTS instruction file is not silently rewritten in this scope; reconcile its intended mapping in an explicit owner-reviewed instruction update. This is recorded, not used to change policy.
7. **Optional adapter detail:** the earlier market review's translation/publication-channel and bidirectional external-provider claims are not proved by generic ACL or versioning language. Their capability-specific source/revision/freshness/approval contracts remain admission gates; unsupported required work cannot be labelled complete.

The archive retains historical source/preview implementation claims with their original scope and dates. Neither an older CI run, a source finding, a mechanical document check, a simulation nor agent agreement is a fresh runtime pass. No merge, deployment, live tenant mutation, Guide/skill/policy publication or new visual approval was performed by this run.

## 18 November 2026 — native implementation verification follow-up

This follow-up implements the user's subsequent request to address the identified next steps. It preserves every earlier specification receipt and its original authority limits. PR #153 is merged; the implementation base is `development` `cf38515f7433afad618cc757fd9b9d93593bc534`, including PR #154's from-cases compatibility fix. No earlier specification sign-off is presented as approval of this code.

The bounded changes reuse native testcases/run snapshots, Bug associations, `ai_generation_requests` task proposals, project MCP settings/token grants, Requirements coverage and KB document versions. They implement separate machine/human authority, exact semantic review, selected publication with immutable receipts and replay, current-only run admission, unchanged execution evidence when a Bug is linked, named MCP consent, truthful unavailable coverage, and exact governed document history/readers. New external proposal tools remain default off pending S0; isolated preview testing does not grant operational authority.

Independent implementation corroboration is a read-only review by an agent that did not author these changes but retains prior specification context; it is not a fresh cold review or a full White/team quorum. Its concrete findings include the migrated AI-memory approval badge/re-review gap and stale cross-project MCP settings responses. Both receive bounded product corrections and browser regression obligations. This is stronger than implementer agreement but does not replace actual migrated/runtime/UI evidence.

**Verification state before CI:** local backend/frontend/e2e TypeScript checks pass, and the migration inventory validates all 156 migrations. Focused contract/unit checks pass; migrated DB and browser scenarios have not yet executed for the implementation head. Exact executed totals, remaining failures and the CI commit will be added after the isolated run. Tests explicitly collect genuine human approval, no-key proposal staging/selection/replay, direct machine refusals, stale snapshot/no-orphan rollback, named consent/revocation, scoped discovery fingerprints, requirement-read faults and legacy KB re-review. Historical test totals are not combined with these to invent coverage.

**Open scope remains explicit:** I3-Q's external executor assignment and attempt fence, case-free report bytes/provenance and independent retest, the full mixed case/result/Bug/issue-text apply loop, provider/CI source receipts and missed-hook reconciliation, durable externally observed agent activity, multi-repository release scope and Skill/root assignment/resolution are not implemented by this increment. The implementation plan retains native owners, sequence, proposed operational decisions and their acceptance gates. No production activation, shared target test, release approval or guarantee of defect-free software follows from this work.

## 18 November 2026 — bounded KB implementation follow-up

This source implementation follow-up does not rewrite the earlier independent specification receipts or their gates. V155 extends the existing KB document/version owner with exact submitted/reviewed snapshots and a current review binding. The existing AI-memory owner/manager decision now requires a trusted human-session principal, expected document revision, fresh membership under the document lock, and a snapshot plus mandatory audit in the same transaction. Ordinary autosave remains separate. Later governing content/source/type/folder changes invalidate current reliance while preserving the historical snapshot.

**Explicit legacy disposition:** authorized humans retain access to legacy document content and history. An old approved flag without an exact human receipt remains `legacy_unverified`, never a backfilled approval. The shared native/RAG AI-context predicate excludes legacy/unbound, candidate, foreign-bound and stale AI-memory versions, and ANN additionally rejects chunks whose existing content hash differs from the currently reviewed text. Current keyword/native retrieval remains possible only under the same exact-review condition. Delayed reindexing therefore reduces semantic availability instead of returning stale governed content. Operational embedding/search bookkeeping is excluded from the edit revision; governing meaning remains included.

The source inventory covers twelve native context entrances and both RAG retrieval modes, including direct memory and requirement-update lookup paths that previously lacked the AI-memory condition. Ordinary document retrieval, human list/get/history and independent raw evidence intake are preserved. Existing general-type rolling Zyra notes are not converted into governed Skills. A current source audit, an immutable record and a semantic retrieval match are distinct evidence, and none proves live target execution.

New migrated-PostgreSQL regressions exercise legacy/candidate/foreign/stale exclusion with exact-human and ordinary-document positive controls, stale ANN chunk rejection/readmission after matching bytes, and stable human review across worker indexing updates. Existing HTTP/browser regressions cover exact review and visible content/save races. The local synthetic authority and existing RAG unit suites passed **30 tests across two suites** after this reader follow-up; backend TypeScript and focused lint checks also passed. Its SQL/vector/browser scenarios remain **NOT RUN locally** and require the isolated migrated CI stack. No shared/live fixtures or migrations were run by this implementation agent.

The existing implementation plan now records the minimum reuse proposal and concrete operational choices: project-scoped Skill identity/version references, one versioned assignment relation, actual human content/assignment authority, exact root/meta-skill bootstrap/recovery and conflict/exception policy. These are proposals awaiting the accountable human owner, not newly active permissions. Skill assignment/resolution remains `not_implemented`; `authority_unconfigured` is the required future refusal when implementation exists without ratified authority. No parallel specification, Skill publication, external executor/report system, independent team reapproval or deployment acceptance is created by this follow-up.

The separate P07 inlet work is a **bounded publication-protocol preview** using the existing native proposal owner. Its server switch `TESBO_PROPOSAL_REVIEW_PREVIEW_ENABLED` defaults off, and its new MCP tools are not advertised while off; isolated CI enabling it is not an operational release decision. It does not satisfy I3 or the original assigned-executor → raw report → retest → exact human selection QA roundtrip. Skill/root authority and the remaining original-loop owners still need their specified implementation and acceptance. Existing authorized human workflows and the current source-change workflow retain their normal entry paths.

The read-only owner inventory found reusable `ai_generation_requests` tasks/proposals/save events, agent identities, native attachment/storage byte services and case-backed automation runs. It did not find the original-loop contracts for assigned external executor instance/attempt/fence, a case-free raw report with reserve/finalize/hash/byte retrieval, report supplements and manual provenance, independent retest reports, or the complete mixed result/Bug/issue-text selected-apply lifecycle. Existing source binding, pinned local Git capture, graph history and run links are real source owners, while provider event receipt/replay/order handling, missed-hook reconciliation, binding-generation history and multi-repository release scope remain missing from the inspected path. Current graph-notification recovery redelivers committed notifications; it does not poll for missed source changes. This inventory is source evidence only and does not certify deployment or close those gates.

## Verification and rollback scope

The bounded verifier checks the same eight filenames, preserved historical bodies/IDs, exactly 48 requirement definitions and trace rows, all 75 defined scenarios with direct proof mapping, consistent vocabulary markers, local amendment links and exact ZIP member bytes. It returns 0 on candidate v3 and detects the missing-requirement and undefined-family negative controls. Version 1.2 returns 1 on the actual v2 mapping defect and 0 after the four links are added. Semantic conclusions remain separate reviewer judgments.

Only the six existing repository integration views and the two existing review records are written in the follow-up draft PR. The canonical export replaces the existing package with revision 1.4.28; the living application baseline receives a dated source/spec entry without changing live outcomes. Reverting the documentation commit or restoring the prior package version rolls back this change without product/schema/runtime effects.

# Integration base reconciliation — evidence supplement

PR149 was observed merged at 2026-11-19T02:58:13Z, and its source branch was deleted. This was not a mutation by this review run. The documentation follow-up therefore targets a new draft branch from development commit 6a14cdd66ccd67d9f44d65dd4867f1f7a015c921.

The GitHub compare from reviewed source 0c54b8ed28c9dec51ed0ca3bbb3cd3b7b8ab12d8 to that integration base lists 11 changed files. None of the six ASER views or the existing challenge review changed. Of 47 cached source-manifest paths, only Tesbo-Frontend/lib/api.ts changed: a seven-line runTestRunSchedule wrapper was added beside schedule APIs. No reviewed core case, KB, auth, source-binding, Requirements or Zyra mechanism changed. Schedule execution/UI/tests and related feature/coverage documents changed outside this documentation increment and are preserved in the new base; their runtime is not certified here. The added POST /api/cycles/schedules/:scheduleId/run entry delegates to SchedulesService.runNowForUser and the existing LegacyService.createCycleFromScheduledRun. Record it in the future all-entrance run-admission inventory alongside due schedules; it cannot be omitted because the old reviewed source lacked that manual route. This bounded diff observation does not establish its full authorization, concurrency or eligibility behavior.

A previously written market challenge was added at docs/reviews/aser-architecture-market-challenge-2026-11-18.md. Its full 232-line added-file patch was materialized, and the resulting Git blob SHA equals the server's 50add1b219797a7e69657b924f8eef28357ba3ff. It is a preliminary author research record, not an additional independent team. Its historical canonical-access block and not-run team status are superseded for this new review by the actual canonical download and actual receipts, without rewriting its historical claims. Its ARC01–20 and HUMAN-E2E01–14 are crosswalk inputs, not 34 newly executed tests or another spec authority.

Exact changed-path/patch data is retained in integration-base-delta.json. This supplement does not transfer source/runtime claims to unexamined changes. Later delivery-link changes require final hash validation; frozen v1/v2 remain unchanged.

# Final delivery-base supplement

The final follow-up branch was created from development commit `017c51b59ea22176b94523d48f77d5e8dabc055b`, tree `73a6b0fc20c8e73bfcdb061e864986ddaa083c1d`, after a second read showed two further commits beyond `6a14cdd66ccd67d9f44d65dd4867f1f7a015c921`. The exact compare is retained in `integration-base-delta-2.json`. This later supplement preserves the earlier base-reconciliation record and every reviewer receipt unchanged.

All six ASER views, the existing challenge record and the earlier market research file remain unchanged across this second base delta. The v3 specification hashes and proposed contracts therefore remain the same. Incoming implementation and test changes are inherited, not overwritten or attributed to this assurance run.

Root read the complete changed-path list and the complete patches for ModelStudioRepository, the KB document editor, the shared frontend API types and the feature-documentation update. Model Studio adds a project/revision-scoped query and response for generated cases, while retaining its explicit note that observed case evidence is not model verification. It does not alter the reviewed case publication, KB snapshot, P-18 or source-binding writers. The shared API adds the corresponding generated-case response shape. The KB editor adds blank-title protection and last-valid-title recovery; this does not implement the server-side immutable governed submission/acceptance boundary specified by ASER-C03. Schedule/model UI and related E2E/workflow changes are preserved as incoming work; their runtime and authentic visual behavior are not certified by this bounded reconciliation.

The independent review source pin remains `0c54b8ed28c9dec51ed0ca3bbb3cd3b7b8ab12d8`; it is not silently relabelled as an audit of the entire newer build. The 47 cached source files remain exact copies of that source pin. Their hash integrity is distinct from the later integration-base changes. Current source consumers, actual deployment/schema and authentic UI still need the already named implementation/admission checks. Model Studio's new generated-case list must retain its weaker provenance semantics; a current case title/status is not a substitute for exact historical assertion or tested-build evidence under C02.

The new documentation branch is `docs/aser-canonical-team-reconciliation-20261118`. Its base is now pinned for a reviewable draft; future unrelated development activity does not turn these source-bounded receipts into claims about unreviewed work. No merge or deployment is authorized or performed by creating that branch.

| ARC-15 — resource/scale budgets | R-ASE-36/45; C06; S/F1/E | Workload measurements, bounded resolver/queue/dependency/tenant budgets, visible degradation and recovery owner are admission gates. Numeric SLOs are deliberately not invented or declared passed. |
| ARC-16 — noisy low-value proposals | R-ASE-02/32/41/47/48; C05/UX; F5/F6 | Evidence-backed applicability, grouped exact review, independent evaluation and comprehension/coordination metrics. Valid N/A does not create fake records; actual usability/benefit is unmeasured. |
| ARC-17 — revoked shared guidance / missing history | R-ASE-27/31/36; C02/C06; S2 | Narrow authorized shared-guidance references, independent endpoint/payload ACL, protected history and explicit unavailability; no copied private content. Retention/restore/migration require real proof and accountable policy. |
| ARC-18 — emergency outage policy | R-ASE-24/36; C06; S0/E | No agent-created exception or fabricated root approval. Actual human authority/quorum, recovery and predefined bounded emergency policy remain open owner gates. |
| ARC-19 — lost notifications | R-ASE-19/20/45; C04/C05/C06; F1 | Durable impact/work obligation independent of inbox state; outbox, delivery receipts, dead-letter/readback and measured freshness gate. No assumed delivery from enqueue. |
| ARC-20 — skill quality regressions | R-ASE-32/47; T-453 / F6 | Versioned held-out evaluations, independent oracle, false positives/omissions and cost/latency/quality evidence; SK-E2E-20/27 planned. Format validation and self-authored fixtures do not prove quality. |

## Human outcome aliases

These 14 labels are earlier outcome lenses over the existing 75 planned scenarios and 49 journey inventory; they are not an additional executed suite.

| Historical outcome | Canonical requirement / existing proof obligations |
| --- | --- |
| HUMAN-E2E-01 — spontaneous code-first change | R-ASE-01/02/37/48; ASE-FLOW-01/13; ASE-INT-16 |
| HUMAN-E2E-02 — BA/QA/PM same change | R-ASE-41/46; ASE-FLOW-07; ASE-INT-13 |
| HUMAN-E2E-03 — approved policy conflict | R-ASE-08/40; ASE-FLOW-05; SK-E2E-26; ASE-INT-10 |
| HUMAN-E2E-04 — actual target verification | R-ASE-06/14/42/43; ASE-FLOW-08/10/14; ASE-INT-08 |
| HUMAN-E2E-05 — exact human skill activation | R-ASE-22/23/33; SK-E2E-03/04/18; ASE-INT-01/03 |
| HUMAN-E2E-06 — absent agent hook | R-ASE-37/45; ASE-FLOW-02/16; ASE-INT-07 |
| HUMAN-E2E-07 — bounded delegation | R-ASE-12/23/38; ASE-FLOW-03/04/12; ASE-INT-03/09 |
| HUMAN-E2E-08 — partial failure/recovery | R-ASE-13/39; ASE-FLOW-06; SK-E2E-24; ASE-INT-04/05 |
| HUMAN-E2E-09 — lightweight/stronger governance | R-ASE-02/48; ASE-FLOW-13; ASE-INT-16; P-07 stays binding |
| HUMAN-E2E-10 — observed agent activity | R-ASE-38/46; ASE-FLOW-04/07/15; ASE-INT-09/13 |
| HUMAN-E2E-11 — regression after release | R-ASE-43/44; ASE-FLOW-11/14; ASE-INT-08/15 |
| HUMAN-E2E-12 — exact risk decision | R-ASE-42; ASE-FLOW-08/10; ASE-INT-08/16 |
| HUMAN-E2E-13 — empirical comparative benefit | R-ASE-44/47; F6 comparative acceptance and design17; ASE-FLOW-01/07; ASE-INT-13/15. No benchmark has run. |
| HUMAN-E2E-14 — skill change during work | R-ASE-25/27/35; SK-E2E-05/11/15; ASE-INT-05/09 |

All open implementation, authority, migration, provider/client, operational, UI/accessibility and live target gates remain visible. A mapped concern is accounted for; it is not automatically fixed in software or accepted by a human policy owner.

This is a document-integrity checker, not a product test, a semantic judge, the Nesberry Graph harness or an authorization verifier. It needs the retained `input/canonical` v1.4.27 originals and the candidate eight-file folder. The source below is the exact version1.2 used. Commands: `python3 verify_spec_package.py --negative-control --archive deliverables/Tesbo_Specifications_v1.4.28.zip`; the known-defect check used `--folder review-snapshots/v2/canonical` and returned1 for the four missing direct proof mappings. Earlier1.1 receipts retain their original results and source digest; the independent1.2 delta does not rewrite them.

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Bounded document-integrity checks; not a semantic or runtime verifier."""
import argparse, hashlib, json, re, sys, zipfile
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
NAMES = [
    '00_README.md', '01_PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md', '02_WORKFLOW_AND_UX_SPEC.md',
    '03_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md', '04_MCP_API_CONTRACTS.md',
    '05_IMPLEMENTATION_BACKLOG_AND_TEST_PLAN.md', '06_IMPLEMENTATION_AGENT_BRIEF.md',
    '07_UI_DESIGN_TASKS.md',
]
CONSISTENCY = ['confirmed_conflict','unresolved_conflict','missing_link','stale_reference',
               'unknown_source','not_applicable','no_detected_issue']

def check(folder, archive=None):
    errors = []
    if sorted(p.name for p in folder.glob('*.md')) != sorted(NAMES):
        errors.append('canonical filename set differs from the same eight originals')
    texts = {}
    for name in NAMES:
        p = folder / name
        if not p.exists():
            errors.append(f'missing {name}')
            continue
        text = p.read_text()
        texts[name] = text
        old = (ROOT/'input/canonical'/name).read_text()
        old_body = old[old.index('\n## '):] if '\n## ' in old else old
        if old_body not in text:
            errors.append(f'historical body not preserved verbatim: {name}')
        before = set(re.findall(r'\b(?:FR|NFR|AC|T|QA|MBT-[A-Z]+)-\d+\b', old))
        after = set(re.findall(r'\b(?:FR|NFR|AC|T|QA|MBT-[A-Z]+)-\d+\b', text))
        if before-after:
            errors.append(f'historical IDs lost: {name}: {sorted(before-after)}')
        if '## Revision 1.4.28' not in text:
            errors.append(f'current amendment missing: {name}')
    prd = texts.get(NAMES[1], '')
    definitions = re.findall(r'^### R-ASE-(\d{2}) —', prd, re.M)
    if sorted(definitions) != [f'{n:02d}' for n in range(1,49)]:
        errors.append('R-ASE definitions must be unique and exactly 01..48')
    plan = texts.get(NAMES[5], '')
    trace = re.findall(r'^\| R-ASE-(\d{2}) \|', plan, re.M)
    if sorted(trace) != [f'{n:02d}' for n in range(1,49)]:
        errors.append('R-ASE owner/design/task/proof trace must contain exactly 48 rows')
    trace_text='\n'.join(line for line in plan.splitlines() if re.match(r'^\| R-ASE-\d{2} \|',line))
    for prefix, total in [('ASE-E2E',15),('ASE-FLOW',16),('SK-E2E',28),('ASE-INT',16)]:
        ids = set(re.findall(r'\b'+prefix+r'-(\d{2})\b', '\n'.join(texts.values())))
        expected = {f'{n:02d}' for n in range(1,total+1)}
        if not expected.issubset(ids):
            errors.append(f'{prefix} missing: {sorted(expected-ids)}')
        linked=set()
        for group in re.findall(r'\b'+prefix+r'-(\d{2}(?:/\d{2})*)',trace_text):
            linked.update(group.split('/'))
        if not expected.issubset(linked):
            errors.append(f'{prefix} defined scenario absent from direct requirement proof trace: {sorted(expected-linked)}')
    api = texts.get(NAMES[4], '')
    marker = 'ASER consistency enum: ' + ', '.join(CONSISTENCY)
    if marker not in api:
        errors.append('exact canonical consistency enum missing')
    for name, text in texts.items():
        if '## Revision 1.4.28' not in text:
            continue
        amendment=text.split('## Revision 1.4.28',1)[1]
        if re.search(r'\bASER-INT-\d', amendment):
            errors.append(f'undefined integration scenario family ASER-INT in {name}')
        if 'SK-E2E-01..22' in amendment:
            errors.append(f'stale Skills acceptance range in {name}')
        for target in re.findall(r'\]\(([^)]+)\)', amendment):
            if target.startswith(('https://','http://','#')):
                continue
            basename=target.split('#',1)[0]
            if basename and not (folder/basename).exists():
                errors.append(f'unresolved amendment local link {name}: {target}')
    if archive:
        with zipfile.ZipFile(archive) as z:
            if sorted(z.namelist()) != sorted(NAMES):
                errors.append('ZIP member set differs from canonical eight')
            for name in NAMES:
                if name in z.namelist() and z.read(name)!=(folder/name).read_bytes():
                    errors.append(f'ZIP bytes differ: {name}')
    return errors

def main():
    ap=argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument('--folder', type=Path, default=ROOT/'working/canonical')
    ap.add_argument('--archive', type=Path)
    ap.add_argument('--negative-control', action='store_true')
    args=ap.parse_args()
    errors=check(args.folder,args.archive)
    result={'verifier':'tesbo-document-integrity/1.2','mode':'mechanical document integrity only',
            'errors':errors,'runtime_tests':'NOT RUN','semantic_assurance':'separate independent review'}
    if args.negative_control:
        import tempfile, shutil
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
            test=Path(td)
            for name in NAMES: shutil.copyfile(args.folder/name,test/name)
            p=test/NAMES[1]
            p.write_text(p.read_text().replace('### R-ASE-48 —','### REMOVED-REQUIREMENT-48 —'))
            caught=any('definitions' in e for e in check(test))
            result['negative_control']={'mutation':'remove R-ASE-48 defining heading','detected':caught}
            if not caught: errors.append('negative control did not fail')
            shutil.copyfile(args.folder/NAMES[1],p)
            p=test/NAMES[5]
            p.write_text(p.read_text().replace('ASE-INT-02/03','ASER-INT-02/03'))
            caught_reference=any('undefined integration scenario family' in e for e in check(test))
            result['reference_negative_control']={'mutation':'replace ASE-INT-02/03 with undefined ASER-INT-02/03','detected':caught_reference}
            if not caught_reference: errors.append('reference negative control did not fail')
    result['exit_code']=int(bool(errors))
    result['files']=[{'name':n,'sha256':hashlib.sha256((args.folder/n).read_bytes()).hexdigest()}
                     for n in NAMES if (args.folder/n).exists()]
    print(json.dumps(result,indent=2))
    return result['exit_code']

if __name__=='__main__': sys.exit(main())
```

```python
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
NAMES = [
    '00_README.md', '01_PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md', '02_WORKFLOW_AND_UX_SPEC.md',
    '03_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md', '04_MCP_API_CONTRACTS.md',
    '05_IMPLEMENTATION_BACKLOG_AND_TEST_PLAN.md', '06_IMPLEMENTATION_AGENT_BRIEF.md',
    '07_UI_DESIGN_TASKS.md',
]

               'unknown_source','not_applicable','no_detected_issue']

        after = set(re.findall(r'\b(?:FR|NFR|AC|T|QA|MBT-[A-Z]+)-\d+\b', text))

    if sorted(definitions) != [f'{n:02d}' for n in range(1,49)]:

    plan = texts.get(NAMES[5], '')
```

No other new team receipt or author discussion was read. White's expressly handed-off pre-gate is the permitted stage dependency. Links to historical review documents embedded in the amended specifications were not followed.

## Original-finding reconciliation

### ASER-B01 — Canonical semantic owners

**Design status: Addressed. Implementation/source closure: Open.**

**Actual contract change:** canonical 01 R-ASE-01 and 04 ASER-C02 define `changeId` as the existing FR-115/T-455 `changesetId`, prohibiting a second packet identity. ASER-C05 makes coordination a derived view, not independently writable completion or approval. Canonical 03 §18.1 allocates candidates to FR-027–037/T-301–306; impacts to FR-119; links to FR-118/T-426; guides to T-422/D-14; native cases/runs/Bugs and files to their existing owners. Native requirement content extends KB identity rather than a second wiki. General tasks have independent acceptance and do not inherit generation-task completion or copy impact state. Canonical 02/07 places Skills and Assignments within D-14 and Change Workspace within existing Zyra change review.

**Original falsifier applied conceptually:** a spontaneous change with no requirement/model and a prepared change now use the same changeset owner. A guide approved but not assigned remains separate from effective guidance. A rejected-only proposal may settle without publishing or closing independent QA/release obligations. Two releases using the same commit retain separate decision contexts. These answers follow explicit identity/cardinality/state clauses rather than a generic reuse claim.

**Limits:** “existing Tasks service/shell” still requires actual storage/lifecycle fit; no all-consumer proof, precise migration or built T-422 resolver is established. Requirement/policy/release authority and human root governance remain unratified named gates. These are now stated as implementation/authority dependencies, not competing alternate semantic owners. No new table/service is approved.

### ASER-B02 — Human case publication and current-definition eligibility

**Design status: Addressed. Enforcement closure: Open / NOT RUN.**

**Actual contract change:** R-ASE-06 now requires shared unpublished staging and says a candidate is not a native Draft case. ASER-C03 requires machine-versus-interactive-human identity, server-owned action-bound review evidence, exact operation/payload/dependency scope and reauthorization. Selected protected native apply must use one actual transaction/client, allocate official IDs at apply and commit mandatory audit/logical-operation commitments atomically. Its case rule explicitly rejects status-only approval inheritance through generic create/update/bulk/clone/import/restore and preserves old run snapshots. Custom fields, reference images and material relationships enter the reviewed semantic manifest.

**Original counterexamples applied:** machine creation with Approved status; modification of an Approved definition while omitting status; cloning Approved into a new identity; and bearer access to approval no longer conform to the proposed contract. ASE-INT-03 tests all these entrance families alongside a legitimate human transition and old-run preservation. ASE-INT-04 checks unapproved dependencies and audit failure produce no official partial writes. The old hash-versus-post-approval-row shortcut is avoided by requiring the reviewed semantic definition/dependency manifest.

**Pinned source remains different:** LegacyService still contains `body.status || "Draft"` at 5235, `status=COALESCE($20,status)` at 6011 and status-copy duplication; the run policy still tests status. The middleware/rest-guard/controller chain remains as initially inspected. These counterexamples have not been executed or repaired. The all-writer table in canonical 05 and exact interactive proof/root-recovery policy remain exposure gates.

### ASER-B03 — Governed immutable KB revisions

**Design status: Addressed. Persistence/concurrency closure: Open / NOT RUN.**

**Actual contract change:** ASER-C03.2 specifies an explicit immutable submitted snapshot separate from coalesced draft autosave, covering semantic body/metadata, references/attachment hashes, authority/applicability, base and effective-method manifest. CAS and locking reject stale submission; later repairs create a new candidate iteration. Policy effectivity, skill approval and assignment remain separate; metadata correction cannot mutate relied-on history. Canonical 03 §18.2 and the governed Skills contract acknowledge the real 15-minute edit window and protect ordinary draft editing.

**Original falsifier applied:** ASE-INT-02 now distinguishes draft A, submitted B and a subsequent C within 15 minutes. It requires B and its semantic/method hashes to survive concurrent draft and assignment changes, review/retrieval and restore. This is the missing reliance boundary, not a renamed current-history row.

**Pinned source remains different:** LegacyService 11057 and writeKnowledgeDocument 12070–12182 still implement coalesced edit snapshots and no caller expected-revision predicate in that writer. The per-mounted-document queue remains local ordering. Physical representation, CAS coverage, transaction participation, acceptance sequence and legacy UNKNOWN migration need implementation evidence. The design does not justify a second versioning service or invent past approvals.

### ASER-B04 — Knowledge retrieval and instruction authority

**Design status: Addressed. Reader/ACL/cache closure: Open / NOT RUN.**

**Actual contract change:** R-ASE-31/34, ASER-C06 and Skills §14 cover direct/list/history/search, recent/folder context, ANN/FTS RAG, native Zyra fallbacks, graph, notifications, exports/sharing, summaries and caches. Authorized candidate readability is explicitly distinct from effective instruction authority. Only the exact approved active resolved manifest is governing. Scope-aware cache keys, reauthorization/invalidation and permission-safe incomplete/unknown outcomes are required. Missing access cannot become a successful empty dataset or N/A.

**Original falsifier applied:** ASE-INT-06 includes approved assigned content, an authorized candidate and restricted evidence across two projects, then revocation. It requires candidate inspection as data, truthful authority classification, no cross-project bytes/identity/count leak and no cached privilege renewal. This preserves the legitimate neighboring use case of agent-assisted review rather than banning all candidate reads.

**Pinned source remains different:** RAG 275/321 and repeated LegacyService recent/folder/fallback predicates still accept ordinary non-ai_memory documents without that future authority classification. No full-reader inventory, migration of stored chunks, cache invalidation execution or post-revocation proof was provided. The amended spec properly calls these required before exposing the new capability; it does not assert that a resolver alone fixes them or that semantic prompt injection is universally solved.

### ASER-B05 — Historical and multiple source bindings

**Design status: Addressed. Migration/event closure: Open / NOT RUN.**

**Actual contract change:** R-ASE-37 and ASER-C02 require stable binding identity, immutable configuration revision, source/ref/scope/activation history and exact historical event association. The current row may remain a pointer; unavailable historic configurations remain UNKNOWN. Rebind cannot relabel history. Delayed events resolve their original configuration and receive a permitted retained/quarantined/rejected disposition without advancing an unrelated active source. Changeset/release relationships are many-to-many; single-source pilots cannot claim multi-source completeness. Existing server-approved checkout constraints are preserved.

**Original falsifier applied:** ASE-INT-07 now explicitly configures A/configuration1, rebinds B/configuration2, delivers old/duplicate/out-of-order A events and exercises two repos in one release plus one source in two releases. The requested forbidden effect and independent release decisions are stated.

**Pinned source remains different:** V146 still keys a single configuration by project, and ProjectSourceBindingManagement 119 still upserts on `project_id`. Exact migration/index/cardinality fit, all readers/capture paths and delayed-event receipt behavior remain unimplemented proof obligations. A bounded single-source slice is still permitted with truthful capability limits.

## Material consumers and regression challenge

| Boundary checked against v1 | Result and retained consequence |
| --- | --- |
| Case write → approval → run admission → old result | Corrected contracts carry exact semantic dependencies and preserve old snapshots; neither a flag nor an accepted proposal is conflated with actual execution. Current run selectors/counts/exports still require migration review. |
| KB draft → submission → human decision → resolver → retrieval | Explicit reliance snapshot and authoritativeness are now defined. Readable candidate data and raw evidence remain usable without becoming active instructions. Source RAG/history behavior has not changed. |
| Change set → proposal → impact/task → coordination | One changeset identity and independent native decisions; mixed/rejected/deferral outcomes stay visible. Cancellation cannot erase independent residual obligations. No additional writable packet lifecycle. |
| Duplicate request → receipt → uncertain effect → resume | ASER-C04 distinguishes exact duplicate receipt lookup, outcome readback, new resume-attempt ID/expected journal revision and new candidate revision for changed intent. Committed logical operations cannot replay under a new key. Native protected apply remains atomic. |
| Source/release/readiness | Stable source configuration and independent release contexts; candidate evidence before deployment is distinct from observed-build proof afterward. No circular demand for future production evidence to approve initial deployment. |
| Revoked skill / disabled MCP / raw failure receipt | A stale authored promotion is blocked, while independently authorized raw observation intake remains possible. P-18 still covers both external aliases, initialization, discovery, calls and cached sessions; native access/provider intake is not equated with MCP enablement. |
| New workflow → original QA workflow | Canonical 02/06 and I3-Q preserve task→assigned executor/own driver→original report→analysis/retest→exact human selection/apply, including zero cases/no Tesbo model key. This is not proven by a cycle-linked verification bundle or source-sync demo. |
| New views → current UI owners | D-14, existing Zyra review and native domain destinations retained. Authentic build/role/fixture/render, focus/accessibility/reflow and branch evidence remain blocked. No mockup becomes a certified Before or approved design. |

I found **no new Must-Fix ownership or authority regression** in the reviewed v1 contracts. This is a bounded negative result, not proof of semantic completeness.

Two small wording watchpoints remain, neither overriding the explicit ASER-C contracts:

1. R-ASE-13's older acceptance sentence still says “failure after case creation but before manual update.” Under C03/C04 and R-ASE-06 this must mean candidate staging or separately scoped effects, never half of a selected official native apply. Before executable test authoring, label the staging boundary explicitly; the companion ASE-INT-04/05 already has the discriminating oracles.
2. The illustrative source-event receipt in design §11 shows a sourceBindingId without spelling out configurationRevision, while C02 requires exact original configuration and is explicitly controlling. The eventual advertised wire schema must include or unambiguously resolve that immutable reference. The illustration must not become an excuse to resolve delayed events against current settings.

These are clarification recommendations, not a new blanket rejection or evidence that a forbidden implementation has occurred. The source-backed gaps in general task fit, file/report byte lifecycle, provisioning, Model Studio/native consumers, all-writer/read reconciliation, resource limits and retention remain as initial review limits. The new source-prerequisite table names a path to close them; it does not constitute the completed inventory.

## Handoff and stop boundary

The five original findings are now addressed **as design remediation** because the amendments change what must be owned, persisted, authorized and relied upon, and provide falsifiers that would reject the observed old shortcuts. Their operational effects and migrations remain unproved. Keep the initial receipt's maturity classifications and evidence tiers; do not relabel PARTIAL/SPECIFIED ONLY as BUILT+LIVE.

Before stronger claims, retain actual human authority/root/no-skill policy decisions, complete source writer/reader and legacy treatment, applicable positive and denied-effect implementation tests, original QA-loop execution, exact source/build/schema/catalog identity, client compatibility and authentic current UI evidence. This delta supplies neither a second Blue member nor another specialist's verdict. White/Graph and the prescribed independent reviewers must use their own actual receipts on the same or a separately reviewed later revision.

**Sealed disposition:** design-level remediation accepted within this bounded Blue scope; formal quorum, implementation admission, runtime/visual certification and final freeze withheld. No target or source modification performed. Further changes require a new snapshot and separate delta.

| Runtime/API/MCP/DB | None in this invocation | Tool catalog/deployment fingerprint, role denial and persistence unverified |
| Running UI/visual | None in this invocation | SOURCE-INSPECTED / VISUAL-BASELINE BLOCKED; no visual parity approval |

Package files establish a Nest/TypeScript/PostgreSQL backend and Next/React/Tiptap frontend with existing BullMQ/Redis infrastructure. They do not establish installed dependency versions or runtime readiness. No external web factual research was needed for this source-grounded gate.

## Independent problem reconstruction

The user job is to keep an application's accepted expectations, change work and verification understandable while humans and coding agents change implementation. This remains necessary when there was no requirement or model before coding, when an external provider owns a requirement, when the agent is offline, and when only some of the seven domains actually need amendment.

A coherent outcome needs: a stable change identity; independent accepted expectations; candidates that do not acquire authority from their author; exact reviewed artifact revisions; qualified relationships; reliable work ownership; source/build/deploy distinctions; and role-appropriate views of the same facts. A code change cannot prove its generated requirements are correct, a linkage cannot prove coverage, an agent report cannot prove target execution, and a completed task cannot establish release readiness.

The relevant actors are developers and external agents, QA, BA/product reviewers, policy/security reviewers, support/manual owners, project/workspace administrators, executors and source/CI/deployment providers. Tesbo's actual permission model, rather than these descriptive job names, must authorize actions. A solo user may perform multiple jobs without conflating their evidence or granting machine credentials human governance authority.

Customs/tags, bulk/import, automation and Zyra writers require complete follow-up inventory. Approval history must remain attributable to the exact reviewed definition; old run snapshots must not be rewritten. |
| Document draft and edit history / KB owner | Create/update, autosave queue, read/history; list/get, recent/folder snapshots, RAG ANN/FTS | Add reviewed immutable snapshot boundary without inventing past approvals. Migrate every reader's authority and visibility treatment, not just the new resolver. |
| External requirement / provider; native requirement / approved KB extension | Requirements provider tabs/sync/history, MCP provider-key links, source refs | Preserve provider identifiers and read-only mirror constraints. Native identities cannot be smuggled through external key strings. Split linkage from coverage and source citations from accepted obligations. |
| Source observations / graph capture; accepted behavior / native owners | Graph capture/review, current-cache projection, review-to-run links | Captures remain observations. General typed edges reference native revisions; source history is not authorization to change expected behavior. |
| Source configuration / existing binding owner | Owner-scoped get/set, single project upsert, review page settings consumer | Bind historical events to stable source configuration identity/revision. Do not reinterpret old events after source reassignment. |
| Approval operation / canonical proposal service | Canonical staged operation/review/apply lifecycle; current case approval is a narrower implementation | ASER packet coordinates domain operations, not a parallel generic approval state machine. Approved and applied remain separate. |
| Skill authority / T-422 planned resolver and human assignment | Canonical settings/index/section and ASER candidate contracts; implementation not proven | One resolver across native UI, MCP and background agent generation. Candidate/readability, publication and effective assignment are separate axes. |
| Execution/release outcome / native runs, attempts and provider deployment facts | Run admission policy and selected graph handoff; rest not inspected end-to-end | Compose release-readiness reasons; no automatic promotion of agent assertions, run labels or links into verified deployment proof. |

## Orthogonal axes and participation

**Brand Deletion Test:** remove “ASER,” “Zyra,” “Kiro,” and any agent vendor name. The surviving concepts are change identity, source observation, authored candidates, exact-revision review, scoped effective guidance, domain work, execution evidence and derived release view. Those belong to Tesbo's existing domain/service owners. A vendor hook, named workspace or proposed “library” does not justify another owner. Nesberry's actor, plan and manifest terminology is not required.

Keep these axes independent: tenant/workspace/project; artifact kind/subtype; internal/external audience; source authority; exposure/confidentiality; author/principal; mutable draft/immutable revision; approval/effective assignment; source branch/build/deploy; event evidence tier; applicability and completion; task classification; observation versus accepted expectation. A scalar “approved”, “external”, “complete” or “current” must not silently decide several axes.

| Participant / scope | Applies | Legitimate nonparticipation / boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Human UI author and reviewer | Same native identity, history and permission model | Human ownership of a bearer token does not make that request human approval |
| External coding agent | Project-bound context and permitted candidate operations | Optional hook absence must not hide provider-observed code changes; no automatic publication |
| Native Zyra/Copilot/background generation | Same effective guidance and protected mutation boundaries | A native surface is not an exemption from agent restrictions |
| Provider-owned requirement/manual | External identity and provenance, permitted mirror/link behavior | Do not mutate provider truth through native copies |
| Native requirements/cases/manuals/policies | Assessment and appropriate governed candidate workflow | Each domain can be unchanged or N/A with rationale; no forced seven-record update |
| Skill authoring | Protected meta-skill and candidate-only writes | No machine assignment/activation or self-authorized bootstrap |
| Raw execution evidence/file receipt | Provenance, authorization and byte/attempt association | No requirement to author a skill or policy just to ingest legitimate evidence |
| Project without applicable active skill | Deterministic explicit unresolved/no-active-skill policy | Do not invent assignment, silently fallback or block unrelated read-only source visibility |
| Multi-repo release | Source/build scope and per-repo missing-data accounting | A bounded single-binding first slice is valid if multi-repo acceptance remains explicitly blocked |
| Solo developer / mixed job roles | Same evidence distinctions and explicit decisions | Descriptive role names must not require fictitious departments or new permission roles |

### Semantic Consequence Map

| Material truth | Owner and scope | Authored / observed / derived | Reliance or mutation consequence | Required proof |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Approved test definition | Native case, project, exact semantic revision | Authored + human decision | Permits new run membership; editing must not carry approval onto changed semantics | All-writer and run-admission revision tests |
| Active skill binding | T-422 identity/version + authorized scope assignment | Authored + human governance | Governs future candidates; rollback/revocation affects resolver and pending work | Stale cache, in-flight, history and principal denial |
| Policy authority | Governed KB/policy revision and declared applicability | Authored, not inferred from code | Shapes expected oracle; audience does not grant authority or access | Exact review snapshot and role-filtered retrieval |
| Native requirement linkage | Native IDs/revisions + typed relation owner | Authored relationship | Changes coverage/readiness; a citation cannot imply tested acceptance | Stale link and approved/executed coverage separation |
| Source binding and event | Source owner, project/repo/ref/configuration revision | Observed/provider-attested | Determines which change/impact/release is affected | Rebind, delayed event, replay and multi-source tests |
| QA assessment/work closure | Canonical impact/proposal/work-item owner | Authored decision + evidence | Bell read must not close work; task done must not certify execution | Persistence, retry, permission and exact-revision closure |
| Release readiness | Projection of native/provider facts | Derived | Supports a separately authorized release decision | Missing scope/build/evidence stays unknown; actual deployment smoke required |

## Findings

### ASER-B01 — Canonical semantic owners must be reconciled before freezing ASER names

**Severity:** Must-Fix. **Category:** Consistency. **Status:** Open.

**Finding/evidence:** ASER correctly calls itself a proposal and requires I0 reconciliation, but its Change Packet, Skills Library and general work-item vocabulary does not yet supply a decisive alias/extension map to canonical T-422/FR-076–077, default pack FR-106–114, proposal FR-027–037, change-set FR-115 and impact FR-119. Canonical 03 already assigns logical change/event/edge/impact owners; 07 explicitly extends D-14 rather than introducing a new Skills page. Current graph capture and review handoff (S5) establish observation/review machinery, not all those semantic owners. ASER design §3 and work packages S/D/F are insufficient as a final source-to-canonical allocation.

**Consequence:** an implementer can satisfy the local nouns while creating another guide resolver, independent packet approval lifecycle, duplicate QA impact/task store or conflicting navigation. The existing change set, proposal, impact, generation task and general work item have different lifecycle meanings.

**Disposition/recommendation:** preserve the reuse direction, and map every affected ASER clause to existing FR/AC/T/QA/UI sections across all eight files. Declare whether Change Packet is a label/projection or extension of the canonical change set; retain per-domain staged operations and impact state. Put skills within the T-422 owner, separating project KB rationale from globally governed pack identity; reconcile the proposed UX explicitly. Record any genuinely new primitive and the specific existing owner limitation that requires it. Native requirement authority and no-active-skill decisions remain open decisions, not accidental approvals.

**Falsifiable closure:** two unlike project scenarios and one spontaneous change trace to exactly one authoritative identity and lifecycle per concept, and a reviewer can follow a clause-to-owner-to-writer-to-reader map without selecting between competing stores. A name difference alone is not a defect; an explicit one-owner mapping would close this finding.

### ASER-B02 — Existing case approval cannot yet be relied on as the human publication boundary

**Severity:** Must-Fix. **Category:** Implementation-Grounding. **Status:** Open.

**Finding/evidence:** MCP create/update accept client status; update explicitly permits omitted expectedRevision (S3, mcp.tools 516–635). Legacy insertion uses `body.status || "Draft"`; update directly retains/sets status, and duplicate copies the source status. Dedicated approve performs valuable exact-revision CAS and inserts V151 receipts, but the controller passes only req.userId. Middleware distinguishes bearer credentials, while the global bearer REST guard permits project write operations except its named project/credential control plane. The run gate checks the case's `Approved` status with transactional row locks, not a current-definition approval receipt.

**Static counterexamples, not executed:** create a case with Approved status; change an Approved case's steps while omitting status; duplicate an Approved case. Each inspected path can leave a newly written definition with Approved status that the inspected admission gate accepts. A project write bearer also reaches the approval route without that route retaining machine-principal context.

**Consequence:** reusing the current “approval flow” alone would permit candidates to acquire or retain official/run-eligible status without the proposal's required human review. Exact CAS on one route does not close other writers.

**Disposition/recommendation:** make the common protected-publication and all-writer migration a prerequisite to I3/S0 exposure. Preserve principal type into native domain authorization. Define which semantic definition is approved, how amendments lose current eligibility, and how legitimate explicit human review enables it. Inventory REST/MCP/Zyra/import/bulk/clone/custom-field/tag/background routes. Do not simply compare a stored pre-approval edit hash with a post-approval row hash: the current approval transaction itself changes status/timestamps. Preserve historical run snapshots.

**Falsifiable closure:** deny each counterexample's unauthorized publication and any machine approval across every entrance; verify no protected postimage/receipt is written on denial; permit a legitimate exact-revision human review and run admission; refuse stale reviewed content and altered approved semantics while keeping old run definitions intact.

### ASER-B03 — Coalesced KB edit history needs an explicit governed immutable revision boundary

**Severity:** Must-Fix. **Category:** Implementation-Grounding. **Status:** Open.

**Finding/evidence:** V45 and S4 provide native document identity and useful version history. LegacyService uses a **15-minute** snapshot window (11057); writeKnowledgeDocument (12070–12182) snapshots the old body only outside the window and updates latest version diff metadata inside it. The mutable current document is written without an expected-revision predicate in the inspected method. Ordinary non-ai_memory status can be supplied by the caller. The frontend DocumentWriteQueue explicitly provides per-mounted-document ordering, not server idempotency or cross-client CAS.

**Consequence:** an arbitrary autosaved state, concurrent candidate or accepted skill/policy text cannot reliably be identified forever merely by reusing the current edit-history version number. The exact reviewed body and its governing pins could disappear into a coalesced draft interval or differ from the mutable document.

**Disposition/recommendation:** extend the existing owner with an explicit submit/accept immutable snapshot contract, exact content/metadata digest and CAS; retain ordinary draft autosave/coalescing where appropriate. Scope must include title, body, type/applicability, source authority, effective skill/assignment refs and other semantic fields. Independently record human approval and active assignment; do not conflate “latest approved” with “effective”. Backfill unknown history honestly. This does not require a new versioning service or a snapshot per keystroke. ASER work package B already acknowledges the storage decision; this receipt identifies the concrete seam that must close it.

**Falsifiable closure:** edit twice within the snapshot window, review one exact candidate, concurrently edit the draft and change assignment, then retrieve the accepted version and its original pins unchanged after restore/rollback. A stale submit must not silently overwrite. Historic coalesced edits cannot be retroactively described as individually reviewed versions.

### ASER-B04 — Candidate inertness and role filtering must reach existing knowledge retrieval consumers

**Severity:** Must-Fix. **Category:** Completeness. **Status:** Open.

**Finding/evidence:** inspected KB list/get use project membership, recent/folder snapshots include every non-ai_memory document, and RAG ANN/FTS use the same `document_type != 'ai_memory' OR status = 'approved'` condition (S4; RAG 275,321). The special AI-memory approval treatment does not establish treatment for new skill/policy candidate types. ASER's resolver and candidate-only guarantees are sound intentions, but the reuse plan lacks a closed inventory/contract for these independent agent-context readers and their caches.

**Consequence:** a new KB-backed candidate could enter ordinary agent generation context through an existing retrieval path even while the effective-skill resolver correctly excludes it. Project-level membership alone also does not implement the proposed field/evidence visibility distinctions. This is a design integration risk, not a demonstrated confidential-data leak or proof that a retrieved sentence controls the model.

**Disposition/recommendation:** define shared publication/authority and visibility decisions at the reader boundary, then apply them to recent/folder context, full-text/vector search, direct get/list, embeddings, exports, graph/report projections and caches. Authorized agents may read candidates as clearly untrusted data; they must not receive them mislabeled as governing instructions. Keep context readable-data permission separate from instruction authority. Revoke and invalidate consistently.

**Falsifiable closure:** introduce a draft policy/skill with conflicting instructions and role-restricted source evidence. Test recent, folder, keyword, ANN, direct/history and export paths under permitted and denied principals. Confirm candidate data remains inert/attributed, approved effective content is selected correctly, metadata does not leak, and revocation/stale caches fail safely. This does not claim semantic prompt-injection elimination.

### ASER-B05 — Multi-source scope needs a migration from the existing single mutable binding

**Severity:** Must-Fix before multi-source acceptance. **Category:** Implementation-Grounding. **Status:** Open.

**Finding/evidence:** V146 uses `project_id` as primary key; the management service reads one configuration and upserts on that key (S6). It has repository/checkout/ref/selection values but no persistent sourceBindingId UUID or immutable configuration revision. ASER's packet source binding and R-ASE-37–48 multi-repository/branch/release scope require more than the inspected owner currently expresses.

**Consequence:** changing the project's configured repository/ref can make late events or historical captures appear attached to the new configuration; a single source object cannot establish a multi-repo release's completeness. Existing source-scope fingerprints help detect change, but do not by themselves define historical binding identity or authorize delayed events.

**Disposition/recommendation:** evolve the existing binding owner with stable identity/revision and explicit repo/ref lineage; preserve the current single-binding API as a compatibility projection if useful. Bind events and reviewed scope to the version used, and define rebind/archive/revocation/late-event treatment. Represent missing repositories as unknown in release scope. A single-binding first slice remains valid if multi-source claims are explicitly deferred behind this gate.

**Falsifiable closure:** configure source A, capture, rebind to B, deliver A's delayed/replayed event, and retrieve both histories. The event must neither mutate B nor disappear without an auditable disposition. Add two repositories/branches and demonstrate readiness cannot become complete while one required source is missing.

## Negative design proof, limits and handoff

The nearest reuse options are strong enough that a new top-level subsystem has not been justified: native case/KB identities, planned T-422 guide semantics, staged publication, one typed relationship owner, current graph/change-review surface, existing project gateway and source configuration. The genuine missing work is at their seams: immutable governed snapshots, exact publication authority, shared retrieval policy and historical source identity. Extraction/generalization is appropriate only where a source-backed native owner needs that capability.

Positive boundaries retained: ASER permits N/A instead of compulsory artifact churn; distinguishes expected from observed; treats hooks as optional; acknowledges unknown semantic conflicts; separates candidate/approval/assignment; keeps release readiness derived; and treats client/runtime catalog mismatch as open. These are valid neighboring designs, not findings to remove.

Outstanding evidence gates include complete legacy writer/reader enumeration; current native guide/workspace provisioning implementation; file storage/restore/download; task/Bug and report consumer paths; source attestor/event intake and retention; actual P-18 discovery denial; authenticated case/KB principal and persistence tests; exact dependency/runtime fingerprints; real current UI and role fixtures; external-agent interoperability; and source/build/deploy/attempt execution proof. No negative result is inferred merely from unavailable evidence.

Performance and operational budgets must follow canonical NFR-012 measurement: payload/fan-out bounds, query/queue measurements, cache invalidation and slow-provider behavior need owners and baselines before claims. This review invents no numeric SLO and does not claim a new worker is needed.

**Handoff package:** this receipt, `source/blue/source-manifest.json`, exact cached sources, canonical/input/method manifests, and the five open findings. Red should challenge both the necessity of the proposed seams and the counterexamples; Green can complete the consumer map; Grey/Purple/QA must close real journey and UI/runtime limits within their actual authority. White determines the prescribed later gate after actual role receipts. The incomplete Blue quorum and unexecuted runtime/visual proof remain explicit blockers to formal certification.

**Method:** unchanged canonical Elephant-Gold v1.3, runnable agent and orchestration from `NesberryEnterprise/Nesberry@d83549c85a4dcd047fb40dc4fba4ec62737ab0fc`, using the Tesbo application binding.  
**Evidence class:** specification inspection, pinned source inspection, current official-document inspection, and explicitly conceptual counterexamples. No application execution.

## Advisory result

The proposal is understandable as a direction for shared human-and-agent delivery assurance, but it does not yet establish an implementation-ready contract. This receipt raises **four Major candidates and one Nit**: unresolved integration with the canonical owners, an incomplete central lifecycle/resume contract, an insufficiently precise reuse baseline for protected case/version behavior, conflicting consistency-result vocabularies, and an out-of-date acceptance-suite range. These are leads for driver/owner triage, not a freeze verdict or an instruction to implement.

The draft repeatedly discloses that its features and tests are proposed. Absence of the proposed implementation is therefore **not** treated as a false completion claim. Several important prerequisites are already acknowledged by the author; this review makes their concrete source consequences and required exit evidence explicit. No Blocker severity is assigned merely because a design-stage decision is open. No runtime, security penetration, UI usability, external-client interoperability, deployed-schema or release certification is issued.

## 1. Invocation, independence and boundaries

No target edits, GitHub writes, deployed actions, or runtime claims from reasoning. Do not audit or modify charters.

The driver additionally supplied only invocation metadata, a cache-integrity correction notice, and the location/hash of the existing application baseline. This was one separately dispatched reviewer applying all six lenses, not six separately executed reviewers. It uses the inherited model family; **context isolation does not prove independence from shared model bias**.

I did not open prior standalone review reports, other-team receipts, the author thread, or `working/`. The baseline likewise contains historical fieldwork and decisions, which were kept separate from current source and current runtime truth. Corroboration against other teams has **not been assessed**. All findings are independently derived within this receipt; they are not represented as unique across the team.

Actions were limited to reading local input, read-only GitHub retrieval at the named pins, public primary-document retrieval, temporary source caching, and writing this receipt. No target specification, charter, GitHub object, application record, deployment or database was modified. No tests were run against Tesbo.

## 2. Plain-language paraphrase

A person or coding agent changes an application. Tesbo should help another person understand that change without having watched the coding session. It gathers the source-change identity, author and delegated actors, the approved expectations that govern the change, proposed updates to existing engineering records, review decisions, real test receipts and deployment observations. A Change Packet coordinates those links; the requirement, case, document, task, bug, execution and evidence records remain owned by their existing Tesbo services.

Every relevant domain receives an explicit assessment. A small change can legitimately leave manuals or policies unchanged or not applicable. Creating more records is not the completion criterion. A proposed case is distinct from an approved case, a run selection, an executed test and evidence about a particular deployed build. A source observation or an agent's assertion cannot become its own independent requirement or test oracle.

Governed skills supply versioned authoring guidance. A server resolves approved, applicable assignments and pins the exact guidance used. An agent may submit a new candidate or successor, but human governance controls approval and effective assignment. Existing versions are not overwritten, historical provenance is not fabricated, and changing an assignment while work is pending must produce an explicit stale/review outcome. Skill formatting never supersedes product policy or grants tool authority.

Optional agent hooks, provider/CI observations, task-oriented MCP adapters, read-only consistency analysis and role-sensitive views connect this work. Human release decisions bind a specific evidence snapshot and candidate build. Monitoring and incidents continue the same history after deployment. Delivery metrics and human review effort are descriptive evidence, not proof that generating records made the product correct or that Tesbo caused an improvement.

That problem is broader than synchronizing documents. Its irreducible obligations are trustworthy identity and authority, understandable next actions, recoverable partial work, independent observation, and a clear distinction between a missing observation and an observed absence.

## 4. Six-lens results

| Lens | Independent assessment |
|---|---|
| **1. Comprehension / self-containment** | The purpose, ownership intent and evidence distinctions can be paraphrased without the author. The main state model cannot yet be reconstructed unambiguously: packet status, per-domain disposition, native proposal/operation status, skill assignment status and release readiness are scattered and not bound by a transition/derivation contract. See EG-02. The discrepancy vocabulary also has two spellings with different meanings, EG-04. Acronyms ASER, MCP, KB, CI, E2E and DORA are recognizable or expanded by linked context; `Zyra`, P-/FR-/T-/QA- identifiers and the prior GAE proposal require the target binding. They should be linked in the eventual canonical integration, not assumed universal. |
| **2. Altitude / negative space** | The actual job is cross-role confidence about a changing application, not mandatory record generation. The proposal covers spontaneous coding, no-agent events, missing observations, post-deploy incidents, confidentiality and human release judgment. Remaining negative space includes changed or withdrawn source intent, rejected/abandoned domain work, partial approval followed by dependency/skill changes, and who can decide a packet is closed while a deferral remains. Existing acceptance scenarios identify many failures but do not settle the aggregate semantics. |
| **3. Decided ground and drift** | Existing canonical QA Standards & Playbooks, protected proposal/apply, typed links and quality-change/impact owners must be mapped explicitly. ASER acknowledges this gate but does not yet resolve it (EG-01). Pinned current source has 58 declared MCP tools and committed graph observation machinery, whereas older canonical source snapshots refer to 50 tools and absent graph routes. Preserve those historical pins and add a current delta; do not rewrite history or call newer source deployed. Existing approval and history are real but weaker than the full proposed guarantees (EG-03). |
| **4. Structure versus standard** | The six proposal documents contain purpose, requirements, source reuse, design, risks, acceptance scenarios, roadmap and operating template. P-14 permits their use as noncanonical research/proposal input; it does not make them a ninth approved product spec. Exact format-standard compliance is unverified because the target standard is unavailable. The concrete required structural work is the canonical owner/ID crosswalk and complete lifecycle contract, not imported Nesberry headings. |
| **5. Implementation readiness** | The proposal is intentionally not ready for full implementation admission. It leaves native requirement authority, storage/version compatibility, human-assent enforcement, bootstrap/recovery, deterministic composition, no-skill policy, writer inventory, resource bounds and rollout evidence open. The first slice may be designed and scoped, but the unresolved decisions cannot be silently supplied by an implementer. See the prerequisite table and EG-01–03. |
| **6. Claim verification** | Primary source supports the main reuse candidates and external patterns with qualifications. It does not prove deployment, client visibility, complete native requirement lifecycle, universal governance, general-task suitability, binary recovery, or empirical benefit. The ledger below explicitly separates VERIFIED, STALE, WRONG and UNVERIFIABLE claims. Broad claims are not promoted because a narrower symbol exists. |
