## 7. Findings for triage

| ID | Severity | Lens | Claim/gap and independent consequence | Relationship to other teams | Route |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EG-01 | Major | Decided ground; structure; readiness | ASER's named skills/change owners are not yet mapped to the canonical owner/ID contracts. Two implementations could create competing identities or UI/resolvers while both say “reuse.” | Independently derived; corroboration not assessed | Blue; Graph; Purple for placement |
| EG-02 | Major | Comprehension; negative space; readiness | Packet status and partial-operation resume are not defined as a complete transition/derivation contract. Rejected, deferred, superseded and partially committed work can have inconsistent closure/retry behavior. | Independently derived; corroboration not assessed | Grey; Graph; Red for lifecycle falsifier |
| EG-03 | Major | Claim verification; bidirectional drift | Existing case approval and KB history are real but do not establish all required exact-revision/immutable guarantees. The implementation baseline must explicitly classify the source paths needing extension before reuse is treated as sufficient. | Independently derived; corroboration not assessed; partly reinforces the draft's own open gate | Blue; Security; Graph |
| EG-04 | Major | Comprehension; claim verification | Requirement and design disagree on the consistency-result enum. API consumers and readiness rules can interpret unknown/unsupported/missing states differently. | Independently derived; corroboration not assessed | Graph; Grey |
| EG-05 | Nit | Structure; drift | Two cross-references stop at SK-E2E-22, omitting six listed scenarios from the referenced range. A mechanical checklist can silently lose those rows. | Independently derived; corroboration not assessed | Driver/QA test-plan owner |

### EG-01 — bind the proposal to the existing canonical owners

**Evidence:** ASER README reuse inventory, design §§3/4/7/10 and plan Step 0/I0/S/D; canonical PRD FR-076/077 and line 530, technical design 667–723 and 727–824, backlog T-422/T-448–453 and UI 441. The canonical UI contract specifically places this work in D-14 QA Standards & Playbooks; T-451 says extend the existing settings/context surface. Canonical source-change and impact proposals have their own identities and state contracts. ASER introduces names and conceptual `skills`, `skill_versions`, `skill_assignments`, `artifact_skill_usages` and `changeId` without an exact crosswalk.

The proposal's repeated “reconcile before build” statement prevents a false claim of completed integration. It does not complete that integration. A dedicated human governance view may be a legitimate extension of D-14; a new label does not automatically prove duplication. Similarly, a Change Packet may be a projection of a change set or a coordinator spanning several change sets. The missing decision is which, with stable identity/cardinality and one authority for each field.

**Required resolution:** map every ASER requirement/work package and acceptance family to the original FR/AC/T/QA owners; state alias versus extension versus genuinely new responsibility; resolve packet/change-set/graph-capture/proposal/impact cardinality; bind Skills Library/Assignments to T-422/D-14 or seek an explicit owner decision for a divergence. Keep v1.4.19/.20 recommended decisions visibly unratified until decided. Do not build a second resolver or a second review workflow while the map is unresolved.

**Conceptual falsifier:** show an existing guide upgrade and a source-change review using both old and ASER entry points. They must resolve the same authoritative content, assignment, proposal and impact identities, with the same permission and history behavior. If different IDs/heads appear without an explicit relation, the ownership claim fails. This walkthrough was reasoned about, not executed.

### EG-02 — define the whole packet/operation lifecycle

**Evidence:** R-ASE-02 and -13; design §4's `open|review|partially_applied|completed|blocked`; §§5/6/13 retry prose; canonical FR-030/035 and QA-050/053/063. The canonical proposal contract already preserves stable logical operation identity across revisions and permits terminal rejection without requiring publication. ASER has no explicit rule connecting its packet status and domain dispositions to those established semantics.

The problem is not a demand for a new state engine. A precise computed projection is preferable if an existing owner can supply it. The needed definition is what becomes terminal, what can remain deferred, who owns an accepted risk, what changes the revision, and what happens when a packet is abandoned, rejected, superseded or reopened. R-ASE-02 combines applicability outcomes with publication states; a domain can contain several artifacts with mixed outcomes, which the single example assessment does not explain.

**Conceptual trace:** the requirement draft persists; a case operation commits; a manual operation fails; the applicable skill assignment changes; the client retries with the same key. The design says a matching duplicate returns the previous receipt and also promises idempotent resume. It does not yet specify whether that retry only reads the receipt, retries failed items, requires a separate resume action, or how reauthorization/new dependency versions affect only the remaining operations. Different implementations could either never finish or reapply a committed operation under a new revision/key.

**Required resolution:** one transition/derivation table for packet and domain summaries; distinguish immutable receipt retrieval from controlled resume; use the existing logical operation identity across revisions; define per-item terminal/uncertain/deferred/rejected behavior, approval and source/skill staleness, and exact completion predicates. Include rejected-only completion, mixed success/deferral, changed source head, and interrupted resume examples with expected durable state. Preserve truthful partial results without weakening atomic selected native apply.

### EG-03 — make current reuse limitations explicit and gating

**Evidence:** S01/S03/S04/S05/S06 and KB snapshot code. The positive path exists: `approveTestCaseForUser` binds an expected row revision to a receipt in the same transaction. The broader paths differ:

- `create_testcase` and `update_testcase` forward the writable status field to native services.
- Native create uses `body.status || "Draft"`; the default is Draft but caller-supplied status is retained.
- Native update can change content while keeping the existing Approved value, and the revision precondition is optional in the current MCP contract.
- New run admission checks the current Approved status; the inspected gate does not match a current exact content receipt.
- The edit hash explicitly excludes independently stored custom fields, tags and relationship records.
- Ordinary KB edits can be coalesced within the 15-minute snapshot window; this is not one immutable skill candidate/version per edit.

These are **source observations and an assurance gap**, not an executed exploit or a complete security audit. Other migrations/triggers and every writer were not exhaustively traced. Nevertheless, the inspected path is sufficient to reject an assumption that “existing approval/history” alone already provides the full proposed invariant. R-ASE-34 and work package S already demand a universal writer sweep, and work package B already questions KB compatibility; those acknowledgments are retained as counterevidence against overstating the defect.

**Required resolution:** record these paths in the reuse matrix as EXTEND/UNVERIFIED with named producer/consumer dependencies; bind the pre-agent-write gate to protected status transitions, current exact reviewed content and relevant dependent versions, mandatory revision guards where required, and immutable candidate/version persistence. Preserve the existing service/transaction owners. Do not claim an approval receipt verifies relationships that its digest does not cover. If an independent guard already closes a path, cite its exact reachable enforcement before closing this candidate.

**Falsifier:** in an authorized disposable environment, exercise status supplied on create, editing an Approved case without the review path, custom-field/relationship changes, and subsequent new-run admission. The intended exact-approval invariant must hold through direct and composite adapters. For skills, rapid successive candidate edits must retain distinct immutable identities without modifying a previous candidate. These tests are proposed, **NOT RUN**.

### EG-04 — use one consistency-result vocabulary

**Evidence:** R-ASE-40 requires `confirmed_conflict`, `unresolved_conflict`, `missing_link`, `stale_reference`, `unknown_source`, `not_applicable`, `no_detected_issue`. Design §14 instead uses `candidate_conflict`, `missing_requirement_or_link`, `stale_dependency`, `unsupported_source` alongside the common values. No mapping or layer distinction is defined.

The differences are substantive: an unknown source may be supported but temporarily inaccessible; an unsupported source describes a capability limit; a missing requirement is not necessarily a missing link; a candidate conflict is not necessarily an unresolved established conflict. A readiness consumer could treat the same case differently.

**Required resolution:** select one canonical result contract or define a lossless distinction between analysis findings and readiness reasons; include definitions, evidence/confidence fields, and explicit unknown/inaccessible/unsupported semantics. Update the requirement, design, fixtures and eventual API together. A consumer given each documented enum must preserve its meaning and refuse unknown versions rather than downgrade it to `no_detected_issue`.

### EG-05 — correct the Skills scenario range

Requirements' Skills acceptance section and Work package S cite `SK-E2E-01..22`, while Skills governance includes 23–28 covering disabled skills at hooks, composite per-item governance, imported scripts, policy conflict, outcome regression and no-agent source events. Refer to the full 01–28 set or an explicit versioned suite manifest. The tests themselves are present; this is a reference-completeness defect, not an allegation they were executed or omitted from the source.

## 8. Named prerequisites and unresolved decisions

These are readiness gates, many already honestly declared by the proposal. They should not be counted as additional independent defects without further evidence.

| Required consumer guarantee | Producer / path evidence now | Remaining decision or proof |
|---|---|---|
| One effective governed authoring context | Planned T-422 resolver and ASER assignment model; current KB/MCP candidates | Exact platform/workspace/project identity, deterministic composition grammar, no-skill policy, revocation/cache protocol and provenance; distinguish mandatory machine-checkable controls from semantic review. |
| Human-only skill governance | S12 demonstrates a machine-token MCP surface; proposal explicitly rejects admin-owned machine tokens and forged assent | Action-bound independent interactive human decision, capability and session/delegation rules, root bootstrap/replacement/emergency recovery, audit and revocation. A token owner's human identity is insufficient. |
| Exact approved native requirement | External keys/UI and KB note capabilities exist | Canonical owner decision for native requirement/AC authority, immutable review versions, typed case relation and draft/approved history; do not promote a KB note automatically. |
| Independent source-change observation | S07–S10 server-controlled configured checkout capture | Provider/CI adapters, issuer attestation, source/event identity, dedupe, clock skew, deletion/rename, source cardinality, missing events and reconciliation. External-agent manifest is not already the current capture contract. |
| Durable cross-domain completion | Native transactions and planned journal; canonical proposal operation semantics | EG-02's complete lifecycle and exact receipt/resume protocol; delivery/outbox acknowledgment cannot substitute for QA assessment closure. |
| Privacy-respecting human comprehension | Existing project/UI/read services and role-view proposal | Current authentic UI baseline, recoverable reviewer journey, role/field filtering including unavailable evidence, accessibility and human comprehension observation. No pixel or usability sign-off here. |
| Immutable approved policy/skill histories | KB identity/history is a reuse candidate, with explicit snapshot limitations | Exact immutable version producer, policy authority, candidate repair, amendment/correction and assignment lineage; migration must retain UNKNOWN historical pins. |
| Bounded dependable service | R-ASE-36 and Skills §12.7 admit limits remain to decide | Explicit payload/cardinality/depth/retention/queue/time budgets, observable failure policy and workload/SLO validation. No numeric thresholds invented by this review. |
| Comparable delivery measures | Official definitions verified; ASER separates observed deployment from incident | Versioned event normalization, production environment and service scope, distinct attempts/deployed changes/incidents, duplicate/rollback/rework rules, censoring and period denominators. Successful deployment events may later cause production failures; do not erase them. |
| Interoperable and useful delivery workflow | Portable template and proposed two-client/comparison tests | Real authenticated disposable-project lifecycle, two authorized clients, target build/evidence readback and comparative user-job results. Source review supplies none of those outcomes. |

No required missing producer was conclusively shown absent across the entire repository. Therefore unknown reuse questions remain **UNVERIFIED/EXTEND pending discovery**, not automatically BUILD-NEW. The new ASER adapters are explicitly planned work; their absence is not reported as a regression.

## 9. Counterevidence, false-positive controls and scope limits

The proposal already distinguishes agent claims from observation, protects skill assignments from machine principals, requires all-writer enforcement, preserves unknown history, treats raw captured evidence differently from authored artifacts, and calls its fixtures unexecuted. Those are real requirements in the text, not proofs of implemented controls; this review did not raise their omission as findings.

The apparent DORA “successful-only” objection was checked against additional primary sources and was **not sustained as a categorical error**. GitLab and the historical Four Keys implementation explicitly count successful production-deployment events. The remaining obligation is to define success at the deployment-event layer and retain subsequent production-failure attribution, not to include every failed pre-production pipeline attempt in deployment frequency. No invented metric superiority or Tesbo causal benefit is asserted.

A Skills Library label alone is not proof of a duplicate store, and a human governance view alone is not a violation of D-14. EG-01 requires an explicit owner/capability mapping before a duplication verdict. Similarly, the new graph implementation does not prove the older quality-change/impact design is obsolete; the behaviors must be compared at their own scopes and dates.

**Not checked / still UNKNOWN:**

- Authenticated MCP catalogs, client cache behavior, real token permissions, actual deployment commit and migration state.
- Full target runtime, production records, database contents, replay experiments, authorization attacks or tenant-isolation tests.
- Complete writer/migration/trigger inventory, full task/file producer paths, all repository source or every canonical historical claim.
- Current graphical UI, supplied image pixels, assistive technology, real reviewer comprehension and role journeys.
- Full Model Studio implementation/conformance, third-party integration availability, load/cost/SLO performance, comparative benchmark or empirical skill improvement.
- Prior independent review outcomes and other-team findings, intentionally excluded to preserve the cold review.
- A separate Tesbo artifact-format standard; absence of a retrieved standard is not proof none exists.

## 10. Handoff and disposition

The driver should triage EG-01–05 against the canonical owners, preserve this receipt, and record any acceptance, rejection, scope adjustment or demonstrated false positive separately. Blue/Graph can settle owner reuse and current enforcement; Security can trace human/machine and protected-writer boundaries; Grey can challenge terminal/recovery journeys; Purple can validate the actual settings/workspace placement. These are routed questions, not claims that those teams have run or agreed.

A later correction should receive a delta review against the revised files. The original finding is not silently rewritten when the author supplies an answer. This receipt supplies no freeze verdict; the driver and designated closeout process retain that decision.

## 4. GD-V2-01 — four planned skill scenarios lack direct requirement-proof links

Severity: Medium, specification traceability. Discovered through a later QA observation relayed by root, then independently reproduced against the frozen v2 bytes. This is not claimed as a second cold independent discovery.

The v2 canonical 05 appendix defines seventy-five ASER scenarios, but the expanded proof references in its forty-eight R-ASE crosswalk rows name only seventy-one distinct scenarios. Directly unlinked definitions are SK-E2E-18, SK-E2E-23, SK-E2E-24 and SK-E2E-26. Each definition exists exactly once; no undefined proof reference was found in that bounded check. The same row text appears in the plan view.

| Unlinked defined scenario | Meaning already present | Relevant corrected trace destination to assess |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SK-E2E-18 | Modified payload after human review requires fresh approval | R-ASE-23 and/or the exact decision/publication requirement, with C03 |
| SK-E2E-23 | Disabled authoring skill does not prevent independently authorized raw observation or grant promotion | R-ASE-37, coupled with R-ASE-25/30 and C03.6 |
| SK-E2E-24 | Composite candidate operations preserve individual skill/authorization and partial receipts | R-ASE-39 with C03/C04 |
| SK-E2E-26 | A skill cannot override actual approved product policy | R-ASE-40 with C01 and independent source authority |

The behaviors are not absent from v2: they already occur in requirement/design clauses and scenario definitions. The defect is the supposedly complete direct trace failing to attach these definitions to accountable requirement work. Some related integration scenarios provide overlapping semantic proof, but overlap is not a direct trace disposition for the omitted named scenarios.

Required correction: add meaningful direct links without changing their definitions or replacing existing tests, then run a verifier that checks definition-to-crosswalk coverage as well as the reverse. Do not relabel a scenario as executed or treat the additional link as evidence that its mechanism works. This is a bounded documentation correction; no runtime-platform expansion is warranted.

The initial v1.1 verifier result remains true within its advertised scope. It checked scenario presence and trace-row count, not full direct linkage. A later stronger verifier can correctly fail v2 without invalidating the earlier execution receipt or authorizing alteration of it.

## 5. Second-order opportunity and opposite-sibling reassessment

The useful compression from preparation survives v2: exact versioned references; one effective-method resolver; shared operation identity and recovery receipts; typed impact with native ownership; and authorization-aware projections. The amendment incorporates these rather than creating new content, graph, task or runtime authorities.

| Hard case | Contract mechanism in v2 | Bounded reasoning result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Assignment revoked after a requirement stages, before manual work | C03/C04 revalidate at promotion/resume; prior commitment and authored-under history remain; changed remaining intent needs a new candidate revision | The earlier recipe cannot silently become permission for remaining writes. Actual concurrency/atomicity remains to test. |
| Raw provider event arrives without agent session or active skill | R-ASE-25/30, C03.6, Skills12.4/13.1, ASE-INT-09 and SK23/28 | Intake uses its own permission/provenance; neither fictional skill-use nor code-derived approved requirement is necessary. SK23's direct trace remains the v2 documentation gap. |
| Shared guidance read permission is revoked | C02 narrow scope exception, C06 read/cache rule, Skills12.6/14 | Retained references do not create payload access or a copied core; new effective reliance stops as policy requires. Real cache/reader sweep still gates implementation. |
| Older case passed on a different build | C02 relationship grain, C03.5, C01 phase-specific readiness | Current link/status cannot certify the new assertion or target. Old runs remain historical; new eligibility and exact proof are separate. |
| Binding changes A/config1 to B/config2 before delayed event | C02 original-configuration resolution and ASE-INT-07 | Event cannot advance unrelated current B. Single-source pilot cannot represent unsupported multi-source release completeness. |
| Documentation-only change and cosmetic source change | C01 seven assessments, C02 source-free reason, C05 settlement, R-ASE-48/ASE-INT-16 | Neither fabricated code nor unnecessary artifact changes are required. Independently unresolved mandatory work still blocks the relevant readiness claim. |
| Rejected-only proposal, later new evidence, and explicit cancel | C05 derived settlement/terminal precedence and independent obligation preservation | Coordination can settle without publication; it cannot thereby close QA or release. New evidence creates attributable recomputation, not rewritten history. |

These are conceptual contract challenges, not G2/G3 observations or executed tests. The strongest opposite sibling remains raw observation without authorship. It shares correlation, identity and permission checks; it does not acquire the authoring lifecycle. That distinction prevents the common envelope from becoming a universal approval platform.

The thirteen Green lenses remain covered as follows: sibling/common-substrate/compression and three-use evidence carry from preparation and now have explicit owners; adjacent-category patterns remain optional inputs; platformization and brand deletion leave native Tesbo invariants; option-value seams are specified before provider/storage expansion; prematurity leaves implementation-head fit and actual policy gated; source reconciliation is bounded as stated; business benefit remains a measured hypothesis; cross-role/channel continuity uses the same IDs below UI/Zyra/MCP/notifications; and participation scope separates primary authoring, supporting guidance, execution evidence and summary projections. No additional marketplace, agent-runtime, generic approval or universal attachment subsystem is justified.

## 1. Review boundary and independent reconstruction

The user's outcome is a connected Tesbo work cycle: a developer, coding agent, BA, QA practitioner, policy reviewer, or other authorized contributor can understand a change, evolve applicable existing artifacts, and show what was observed, proposed, approved, executed, and left unresolved. Requirements, cases, manuals, policies, tasks, files, and code need durable relationships at the versions actually relied on. They do not need to share one storage lifecycle or one approval authority.

The promising shared capability is a small set of native identities, typed references, resolution receipts, and honest derived views below Zyra, MCP, and human UI surfaces. The proposal already protects several important distinctions: optional ceremony, explicit unknowns, independent normative sources, human skill promotion, partial persistence, separate artifact owners, and evidence-backed readiness. The integration risk is that broad reuse language can still create parallel meanings for Change Packet, skills, graph, task, or release authority unless the eight canonical specifications name the exact extension points.

Binding policy comes from Tesbo. The Nesberry methods supply review discipline, not Tesbo domain entities, an operational governance role, migration names, scripts, deployment procedures, or permission rules. Preserve P05/P07/P14/P17/P18/P20 and current repository policy. In particular, an approved-only rule for adding cases to protected runs must not erase legitimate historic run associations, and project opt-in or interactive human authority cannot be inferred from an administrator-owned machine token.

No previous review report, other-team receipt, working document, execution registry, live application, or GitHub state was read or mutated. Source caches named for other agents were used only as pinned primary-source files and retrieval metadata, never as their findings.

Keep the shared approved core and scoped references; use existing settings and contextual authoring surfaces. |

These are integration proposals, not new binding entities. The canonical eight files must carry accepted mappings and dispositions so a side proposal cannot silently supersede P14.

## 4. Preparation findings

### GP-01 — Explicit aliases are needed to prevent parallel coordination and skill authority

Priority: high integration risk. ASER design §§1–4 and implementation plan introduce Change Packet, a Governed Skills Library, assignment UI, and work packages while promising reuse. Canonical FR076–077/T422, FR115–119/T455–457, and UI D14 already name guidance, change, impact, and typed-edge ownership. Generic reuse text does not explain identity equivalence or migration of terminology.

Consequence: two records could describe the same change with divergent completion, or two skill selectors could choose different authority for the same authored revision. A second top-level Skills product surface would also conflict with the existing integration direction.

Proposed disposition: represented elsewhere plus bounded extension. Add an explicit canonical crosswalk for owner, ID, lifecycle, revision, and projection. If Change Packet is only the view/envelope of quality_change_set, say so. Keep task, impact, approval and source-event states distinct. Effective-skill resolution should extend the T422 service contract rather than bootstrap an unrelated platform. Acceptance must show one change and one applicable guidance resolution across native and external paths.

### GP-02 — Reusing KB history needs an exact-revision extension, not an immutability assumption

Priority: high. ASER R-ASE-21–36 and design §§3–4 require immutable skill candidates and per-revision reliance. The sampled current KB writer creates snapshots only after its snapshot window is stale and otherwise updates the latest snapshot's diff metadata. It stores the current live document separately; content snapshots, current content, and per-edit provenance are not interchangeable. General document status handling is also different from the special ai_memory approval path. Evidence: legacy.service.ts 12040–12175 and V45; canonical P05 and FR112; ASER skills §§2–6.

Consequence: pinning the current document ID or nearest history row can misstate the exact content used for a skill decision or authored artifact. This is a semantic gap in the proposed reuse contract; it is not a conclusion that all existing KB history is unsafe or that a machine exploit has been proved.

Proposed disposition: extend existing owner. Specify when an immutable content revision/hash is materialized for reliance or approval, which native record owns it, how it differs from an autosave/coalesced history view, and how an accepted old snapshot remains addressable without granting current read access. Do not introduce a universal snapshot database. Test later with two edits inside the coalescing window and an intervening approval/proposal: the relied-on bytes must be recoverable or explicitly unavailable, never silently replaced.

### GP-03 — Generic relationship labels leave historical coverage and authority ambiguous

Priority: high. Design §4 lists useful labels, but endpoint type, cardinality, historical grain, authoritative owner, and invalidation are not defined per kind. FR081/118 already require typed historical semantics. Current cached requirement projection explicitly says cache-only, freshness unverified, origin attestation not recorded, and no coverage assertion. Current graph run links are review-to-run/case handoff links; timestamps, current run status, and storage availability are not per-condition or exact-build proof.

Consequence: a consumer can interpret verified_by as either a planned test, an approved mapping, or an executed passing assertion. A relation to current latest content can rewrite what a past decision relied on.

Proposed disposition: extend the existing edge contract with a small admissible-kind matrix and explicit relation state. Preserve independent artifact and link lifecycles. Candidate relationships are supplied in §6; they are not approved registry values. Missing links remain missing; current cache references must not be upgraded by renaming them.

### GP-04 — Scope and provenance rules need the opposite-sibling exceptions propagated

Priority: high for scope; medium for wording propagation. Design §4 says links are within one project. Canonical default-pack design and skills §12.6 allow explicitly authorized platform/workspace guidance references. R-ASE-30 and plan S2 broadly pin skills on files/evidence; skills §12.4 correctly exempts externally sourced raw data, and SK-E2E-28 permits a source event without an agent session.

Consequence: a blanket rule either rejects legitimate shared guidance or opens a general cross-project path; universal skill pinning can fabricate authoring provenance or block honest source observations when no authoring occurred.

Proposed disposition: distinguish project-owned artifact links from governed references to shared guidance. Scope resolution, endpoint existence and payload visibility remain independently authorized. Apply skills to the authored transformation and its revision, with explicit not_applicable for raw observations when appropriate. Propagate that distinction into R-ASE-30, plan S2, bundle contracts and the agent rule. A revoked parent read grant invalidates effective use while retaining a protected historical reference; it does not copy private content to a project.

### GP-05 — Source-binding history and multi-source scope must be honest at the integration seam

Priority: high. R-ASE-37 anticipates multiple repositories, branches and releases. Design §4 has a singular source object/sourceBindingId; §11 uses binding/event/revision receipts. Actual V146 makes project_id the primary key, and project-source-binding.management.ts upserts one mutable binding for a project. This is source evidence of that implementation shape, not proof that no other provider support exists anywhere.

Consequence: a delayed event from repository A could be evaluated against a changed binding for B, or a historical change could appear to have used the latest binding. A one-to-one packet/commit/release assumption also fails for multi-commit changes and rollbacks.

Proposed disposition: architecture-now requires immutable source identity or binding generation in each observation and an explicit source-set cardinality in the logical contract. Existing single-binding operation may remain the first implementation slice. Multiple-provider orchestration, indexing and deployment integrations remain later work. A new binding is not an attestation of prior source freshness; changed scope may require a new baseline with impact unknown.

### GP-06 — Reconcile dated canonical checkpoints with newer actual source without upgrading source to deployment truth

Priority: medium/high. The canonical v1.4.27 material preserves a dated preview/non-persistence checkpoint. At the pinned PR head, sampled Model Studio methods now create persisted revisions, record approval receipts, and generate native cases; one class header still describes a narrower earlier state. The read code's active-model guard means non-archived, not necessarily an approved active revision. The MCP service also advertises a build/catalog fingerprint and invalidates stale sessions, but no external-client exchange was observed.

Consequence: implementation planning can unnecessarily rebuild existing behavior or rely on capabilities beyond what the code actually guarantees. Stale historical prose must not be treated as a current absence claim; current methods must not imply permission, merge, deployed operation, or an exact canonical activation model.

Proposed disposition: maintain a dated reconciliation table per capability: historical statement, pinned source finding, semantic gap, implementation/deployment evidence still required. Preserve the MBT/behavior-family alias and approval-versus-activation distinction. Do not use a physical table name alone to conclude a second domain exists. Mark current UI SOURCE-INSPECTED / VISUAL-BASELINE BLOCKED until actual authorized observation.

### GP-07 — Release, aftercare, metrics and general work items need bounded seams, not a new platform commitment

Priority: medium/high scope risk. ASER R-ASE-41–48 and design §§12–15 have real cross-role continuity value, and explicitly reject an AI release authority. This preparation did not inspect a complete native release decision, generic project-task, deployment or incident implementation. Those owner details remain unknown, not absent.

Consequence: accepting every proposed projection as a new runtime owner expands a spec integration into a delivery platform, task engine or metrics product. A readiness summary cannot become an independent approval system or a substitute for exact evidence.

Proposed disposition: record logical joins now: decision-to-fixed-readiness snapshot/build/risk set, deployment occurrence-to-source set, incident/finding-to-remediation/retest, and native task-to-change. Specify the authoritative decision policy before implementation. Defer provider rollout, new operational authority, generalized work engine, full dashboards and performance targets until source discovery and approved use justify them. Descriptive outcomes may support operational efficiency and trust; no pricing, marketplace, or causal performance claim is evidenced here.

### GP-08 — Cross-file vocabulary and scenario references need one explicit semantic crosswalk

Priority: medium. Requirements and implementation plan still cite SK-E2E-01..22 although skills-governance contains 23..28, including raw-source and post-release siblings. R-ASE-40 and design §14 use different consistency classification names. R-ASE-02 mixes proposed/approved/published with applicability outcomes, while other passages use update/unchanged/not_applicable/blocked/deferred.

Consequence: readers and future contracts may silently choose different denominators, conflate lifecycle with applicability, or omit the newly added opposite cases from a gate.

Proposed disposition: keep orthogonal fields where the meanings differ; define aliases only where semantics match. Reconcile the referenced scenario range, per-domain assessment state, artifact publication state, operation outcome, consistency result, and readiness result in all existing documents. This is not a recommendation to force every domain into one universal enum. A target-specific document-integrity verifier should later validate defined IDs and crosswalk coverage on the corrected bytes; no zero-orphan or complete-traceability result is claimed here.

## 5. Second-order opportunity pass

| Shared seam | Three concrete uses in this target | Compression and option value | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Exact versioned reference with explicit resolution outcome | Requirement criterion used by a case; skill assignment used for manual authoring; case/evidence/build set used in a readiness decision | One consistent contract for what was relied on and whether it is still applicable reduces false current-state joins. | Generalize identity and validity semantics. Keep domain content, approval, and snapshots with their owners. No generic historical database. |
| Effective authoring-guidance resolver | Requirement drafting; test-case authoring; manuals/policy drafting | One T422 extension can provide deterministic applicability, read permissions, exact approved revision and explanation across native UI and MCP. | No new agent runtime, skill marketplace, executable plugin platform, or power to amend product policy through skill text. |
| Correlated change/operation receipts | Native Zyra proposal; external-agent composite bundle; provider observation without agent session | Shared correlation and honest committed/failed/not_attempted/uncertain outcomes simplify recovery and role handoffs. | Observation, authoring, approval and execution remain distinct event types. Do not make raw observations require a skill or human session. |
| Typed impact and review projection | Requirement change affecting cases; policy change affecting manuals/enforcement; source change suggesting QA review | Reuse the same edge and impact owners; separate reversible notification delivery from substantive assessment. | No graph database or generic workflow engine justified. An inference opens review; it does not approve, execute or close it. |
| Authorized audience projection | Internal BA/QA requirement view; external manual; high-level release summary | Shared references can support different views without copying authority or exposing restricted evidence. | Audience labels are not ACLs. Check endpoint existence, payload and historic-source permissions independently. |

Mandatory-question coverage:

- Siblings/common substrate/compression/three uses: the table identifies current or near concrete uses in the supplied Tesbo scope. Three uses justify investigation and a semantic seam; they do not automatically justify extracting another service.
- Adjacent categories: conceptual comparisons with CI event ingestion, document revision management and issue tracking reinforce separating observation, approved obligation, work and outcome. No competitor feature or external benchmark was researched or asserted.
- Value of option and prematurity: preserve immutable references, explicit cardinality, operation outcomes and permissions now. This reduces later integration cost without committing to provider adapters, a general-purpose agent control plane, or storage consolidation.
- Source reconciliation: GP-02, GP-05 and GP-06 distinguish pinned code, dated baseline, proposal and live unknowns. Package manifests were read; no new dependency or framework migration is recommended.
- Business leverage: less duplicate review and credible evidence reuse are plausible operational benefits. Measurements must retain missing-data denominators and avoid claiming causality; commercial leverage is unmeasured.
- Cross-channel/role continuity: the same native change, artifact revision, operation and decision references must survive UI, native Zyra, external MCP and notifications. No chat, inbox or role-specific copy owns completion.
- Brand deletion: remove the words ASER, Skills Library and Change Workspace. The surviving needs are governed guidance, change coordination, typed historical relationships and authorized views already named in Tesbo. A new brand adds no unique storage invariant.
- Orthogonal axes: artifact kind/subtype, authoring provenance, source authority, audience, visibility, publication state, activity evidence tier and execution outcome are separate dimensions. Do not import Nesberry marketplace axes or collapse Tesbo's axes into a new module.
- Hardest/opposite siblings and participation scopes are developed below; they constrain the seam, rather than merely adding happy-path scenarios.

## 6. Candidate typed relationships and rejections

These candidates refine incomplete semantics; some are already contemplated at a generic-label level. Their spelling is illustrative until reconciled with FR081/118. The typed-edge registry can describe/provide a projection of these relationships while the native owner below remains authoritative. Every relation must authorize both endpoint disclosure and payload access; a visible parent is insufficient.

| Candidate meaning and direction | Cardinality / authority | Evidence and exact historical grain | Lifecycle / permissions / user value |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Approved requirement criterion revision → covered_by → case assertion/condition revision | Many-to-many. Requirement/case owners approve semantics; same typed-edge registry represents them. | Exact criterion and case/condition revisions, reviewer and mapping rationale. Citation or text similarity alone is insufficient. | A changed endpoint marks current applicability for review; prior links survive. Same-project domain access. BA and QA can identify exactly what obligation is designed to be tested without claiming it ran. |
| Policy revision → enforced_by → rule/guard revision; separately → checked_by → test assertion | One policy may have many controls and checks; a control may satisfy several obligations. Policy owner and native source/test owners remain distinct. | Exact approved obligation, code/source attestation and entrypoint scope; test assertion/attempt is separate proof. | A changed guard or scope triggers reassessment. Protect private enforcement details independently from published policy. Security can distinguish normative rule, implementation and observed verification. |
| Manual section revision → describes → requirement/behavior revision | Many-to-many. KB manual owner and requirement/behavior owner retain authority. | Section identity or anchored content hash, audience/environment and approved subject revision; reviewer rationale. | Changed subject opens manual review without silently rewriting published text. External exposure does not grant internal-source access. Users see which behavior a manual version actually describes. |
| Authored artifact revision → authored_under → effective-guidance resolution receipt | One immutable resolution manifest for an authoring operation may contain many assignment/skill revisions; one revision may participate in multiple scoped operations if explicitly recorded. Existing guidance and artifact owners. | Assignment revision/ID, approved skill revision/hash, resolved scope, actor class and resolution outcome. | Future assignment changes do not relabel history; revocation blocks new reliance as policy requires. Raw external evidence may have not_applicable. Reviewers can explain how an artifact was produced without treating a skill as product authority. |
| Change/source observation → observed_from → source-binding generation and provider event/revision | Many events/source revisions per change; one event may affect more than one change, with explicit deduplication. Native observer and source provider own facts. | Repository identity, binding generation, event/delivery ID, revision and declared evidence tier; provisional-to-committed lineage explicit. | Rebinding cannot rewrite old events. Unauthorized or stale source access yields unavailable/unknown. Developers and QA can distinguish fresh observations from a stale cache. |
| Human readiness decision → relied_on → fixed evidence snapshot/build/risk set | Many decisions over time, each with one explicitly frozen context that can include many obligations and receipts. Existing authorized release/decision owner, still to be source-verified. | Actor authority, exact candidate build, scope, case/run/evidence and approved obligation versions, accepted risk dispositions. | Changed material evidence creates a new context; historical decision remains fixed. Restricted underlying payload stays restricted. PM/operations can explain what was authorized without a new release authority. |
| Finding/incident occurrence → observed_on → deployment/build; remediation change → retested_by → native attempt | Many occurrences may share one defect; fixes, deployments and retests may be many-to-many. Existing Bugs/findings/execution owners; incident extension only if evidenced. | Occurrence/attempt identity, actual target and evidence; explicit cause confidence and independent retest outcome. | Closing a task or merging code is not a passing retest. Rights follow existing bug/run/source scopes. QA can follow recovery without losing recurring or side-finding history. |
| Derived or redacted evidence artifact → derived_from → original file revision/object digest | Many derivatives per source; multi-source transformations allowed with explicit inputs. Existing KB/file/evidence owner. | Immutable input/output digests, transformation and inspection status, relevant observation/attempt; raw bytes availability is separate. | Preserve originals and custody; access to a derivative need not expose confidential input. Attach fixtures, expected references and observations at their proper scopes. This prevents a report image or fixture from masquerading as execution evidence. |

Rejected generalizations:

- A universal verified_by edge that treats a mapped case, current Passed status, blob availability and independent assertion proof as equivalent.
- Mutable latest references as the sole explanation of a prior approval, authored revision, execution or release decision.
- Any project may read any globally referenced skill candidate/history, or copying restricted core skills into a project to avoid permission failure.
- Agent identity/activity inferred from private transcript absence, from an admin token's owner, or from an unobserved provider event.
- A new graph store, generic workflow engine, shared agent runtime, universal approval state machine, or general task platform merely because several modules participate.
- Commercial metrics or post-release trends as proof that a skill or Tesbo caused an outcome.

## 7. Cross-role/channel continuity and hardest siblings

| Context | Primary versus supporting participation | Required continuity |
| --- | --- | --- |
| BA changes acceptance criteria | Requirement revision is primary; code and case references support review. | Preserve the same requirement/change identity through UI and MCP; approval authority and agent guidance remain separate. |
| QA executes an approved case | Attempt and evidence are execution-only facts; requirement mapping and authoring skill are supporting context. | Original case revision/condition and target remain pinned. A current case edit or newest skill cannot rewrite the past attempt. |
| Human approves a skill assignment | Governance decision is primary; candidate content and simulations support it. | Interactive human authority must survive surface changes and be distinguishable from that person's machine token. The same permission boundary applies to alternate endpoints. |
| Provider event arrives without an agent | Source observation is primary; skill authorship and agent session may be not_applicable. | Ingest honest evidence into existing review with no fictional action/skill receipt; notification read state cannot close impact review. |
| PM or external reader views readiness/manual | View is projection-only; native obligations, decisions and evidence remain authoritative. | Filter restricted details, preserve unknowns, and link to permitted exact versions; no copied status or role-owned release record. |
| File used as fixture, expected reference or observed result | Role depends on source context, not file extension. | Reuse storage bytes where allowed but attach the semantic relationship to input scope, approved expectation, or actual attempt respectively. Baseline PD01 remains relevant. |

Conceptual falsification probes — not executed tests:

1. A human revokes an assignment after a requirement draft commits but before the linked manual is attempted. The receipt retains the committed draft under its old resolution; a retry cannot silently authorize the remaining action under stale authority. This rejects treating a composite bundle as one timeless approval.
2. An external source event arrives without a session or active authoring skill. Observation may be recorded with independent provenance; no skill use, approved requirement or code correctness is invented. This constrains the otherwise useful common receipt envelope.
3. A shared skill becomes unreadable in a project. Historical IDs remain subject to access policy; future effective resolution becomes unavailable, and no copied private source/history appears. This distinguishes scoped reference reuse from universal cross-project links.
4. An older case revision passed on a different build; the current case is approved and linked to a review. Current status does not fulfill the new change's exact assertion obligations. Existing run handoff links cannot carry more proof than their schema provides.
5. The project's source binding changes from repository A to B while an A event is delayed. The old observation retains A and its binding generation; it cannot acquire B's current authority or silently compare incompatible baselines.
6. A solo contributor makes a documentation/policy change with no code patch, while another makes a cosmetic code fix with no policy impact. Both use the same coordination identity with honest applicability outcomes; neither requires fabricated case, requirement or repository updates.

The least similar credible sibling is the provider event or raw evidence with no authored artifact. It shares correlation, scope and provenance, but not skill execution, proposal approval, or a human authoring UI. That boundary is necessary now. Full provider orchestration is not.

## 8. Architecture-now, implementation-later and scope decisions

### Specify now within the existing canonical documents

- Exact owner/alias table for change, impact, guidance, typed links and native artifact histories; no overlapping authoritative lifecycle.
- Per-kind endpoints, revision/occurrence grain, cardinality, lifecycle, stale/unknown behavior and scope rules for the first useful relations.
- Applicable guidance versus raw-observation not_applicable; separate product authority, agent guidance, actor class, skill approval, artifact publication and execution proof.
- One logical effective-guidance contract and native write-policy boundary across REST, MCP, Zyra, human UI and composite operations, with durable partial outcomes and explicit permission failure.
- Source-set and binding-generation semantics; immutable reliance context for decisions; existing native UI patterns and dated source/deployment distinctions.
- Full ID/scenario references and separate applicability, lifecycle, operation, consistency and readiness vocabularies.

### Implement later in approved, evidence-backed slices

Current-source discovery can determine the smallest KB/version extension, existing guidance resolver extension, exact native requirement mapping, narrow task subtype and source observer adapter. Test exact authorization, stale writes, partial recovery, historical reads and real MCP schemas at the implementation head. Later provider/deployment integrations and descriptive reporting require their own source and runtime evidence. This receipt does not authorize those changes.

### Not justified by this preparation

A new runtime platform, skill marketplace, generic workflow/graph database, duplicate KB/requirements/case store, new top-level Skills navigation, replacement task system, agent-owned policy or release authority, universal all-artifact approval engine, fabricated provenance, or a new copy of Nesberry's execution harness.

## 9. Formal Green review still pending

The following are explicit unmet stage/gate requirements, not a request to weaken them:

1. Required White pre-gate handoff and authorized design/correction stage have not been consumed by this reviewer. Cold independence was preserved.
2. Corrected canonical/proposal document bytes and their hashes must be handed over. This receipt covers the pinned unchanged inputs only; changes require a separate delta review.
3. The target document-integrity verifier must be declared, scoped, executed against those corrected bytes, and produce its actual exit code and evidence. A checksum helper is not that verifier. No Tesbo version of a Nesberry runtime script has been invented.
4. Green's formal quorum remains two: the required target verifier exit 0 plus independent reviewer sign-off under the declared binding. This single preparatory receipt does not meet it and cannot be counted as a formal pass.
5. Whole-package requirement/actor/state/interface/consumer and semantic-completeness inventory, owner/edge trace, and each required team's current evidence must be reconciled under orchestration. The reading sample here cannot certify full coverage or zero omissions.
6. Source-grounded design reuse is partially prepared; authenticated runtime, current visual baseline and external-client capability evidence are absent. Document adequacy must remain distinct from runtime certification.

Routing at the proper stage: ownership to Blue; historical grain and invalidation to QA; edge/evidence semantics to Graph; source/auth boundaries to the applicable security reviewer; human projections and source-backed component reuse to Purple; unresolved policy or stage decisions to White. No other-team conclusion was needed to derive these findings, and none is pre-endorsed.

Prepared outcome: the existing Tesbo architecture has credible extension seams. The useful generalization is shared identity, exact references and honest projections. This preparation supplies bounded candidates and constraints for integration; it deliberately leaves the formal Green verdict unset.

## 2. Independent deep traces on the affected seams

The six original personas and their ignorance boundaries remain: QA reviewer; external analyst/executor; coding delegate; BA/PM/docs/support reviewer without private chat; independently authorized governance human; tester/release/operations owner. These are conceptual walks of the written experience. The user-facing conclusions below are synthetic assessments, not interview quotations or observed UI behavior.

### D1 — Exact human decision, semantic drift and selected publication

P1 approves case definition A, including material custom fields/images/links. An agent or generic writer then proposes semantic B while carrying an Approved label. Under C4 C03(3–5), B cannot inherit A's assent or become new-run eligible solely from status; a machine owned by P1 cannot substitute for an independent human action. The user can compare/review B, then separately apply only the approved dependency-closed selection. Mandatory audit failure or a native adapter lacking the shared transaction refuses the protected apply before official partial writes. An unchanged approved A remains the valid positive case; an existing historical run retains its original snapshot. **G0 consequence holds; GT-01's current source counterexample remains.** QA correctly narrows the falsifier: the reviewed semantic hash is not naively equated with a status-sensitive pre-approval edit hash. Sources: C1 R-ASE-06/34; C3 §18.2; C4 C03; C5 ASE-INT-03/04/11.

### D2 — Side finding, independent result and ambiguous Bug creation

P6's execution E is Passed by U1 at t1. U2 associates a SIDE Bug. C03(6) requires the relationship to persist without changing E's verdict, execution time or performer. A real failed assertion still has its own valid outcome path; unlinking does not heal an old failure. If the Bug committed but acknowledgement or a later side effect failed, C04 requires authoritative readback before resend; an error is not proof of rollback. **The contract holds, but the inspected create/add-link helper still predicts the contrary result.** QA's important narrowing is retained: not every Bug edit reaches that helper, and sealed automated runs already have protection. Sources: C4 C03/C04; C3 §18.2; C5 native Bug prerequisite/ASE-INT-03; original GT-02 and QA QP-3.

### D3 — Immutable B, editable C and changed guidance

P4 edits draft A→B, submits B, then another permitted edit creates draft C inside15minutes. C03(2) makes B an immutable submitted object with its semantic metadata, references, attachment hashes and method manifest; ordinary C autosave remains usable. Reviewing B cannot silently review C. A changed accepted base, required assignment or semantic dependency requires the applicable conflict/reassessment before promotion. The reviewer may recover exact B, compare C and retain permitted unsaved work. A metadata-only authority/audience/effectivity change cannot mutate accepted meaning. **Conditional GT-03 remedy holds at the contract layer; ordinary KB coalescing remains useful and unchanged source is insufficient alone.** Sources: C1 R-ASE-08/21/27/30; C3 §18.2/Skills§14; C4 C03; C5 ASE-INT-02.

### D4 — Partial staging, lost response and controlled resume

P3 stages requirement candidate R, manual candidate M fails, and case candidate K has an uncertain acknowledgement. These are staged commitments, not published native artifacts. Repeating the same key/digest reads the logical receipt and current journal revision; it cannot finish M as a hidden side effect. K must be read back/reconciled first. An explicit resume supplies the original manifest, expected journal revision and new attempt ID; stale journal or changed required skill blocks eligible remaining work. R's old commitment/history is preserved, but it still needs current review/dependency checks at later protected apply. A new transport key cannot republish an already committed logical operation. **C04 now supplies the deterministic distinction that the initial narrative GJ-28 HOLD did not prove.** Sources: C1 R-ASE-13/39; C4 C03/C04; C5 ASE-INT-04/05/14. Native uniqueness, retention and concurrency remain unexecuted.

### D5 — Revoked authoring skill, MCP OFF and permitted raw evidence

P2 cannot promote a stale authored candidate after its skill is revoked. A runner's raw failure may still enter through its independently authorized evidence route with true provenance/skill N/A. In the different branch where project MCP is OFF, that same MCP transport is denied at discovery/call; the raw-evidence exception does not reopen it. A separately granted provider channel can remain valid. The receipt proves Tesbo received an observation, not that every claimed target action occurred. **The denial/positive pair is coherent and prevents a blanket failure-evidence ban.** Sources: C4 C01/C03/C06; C3 Skills§12.4; C5 ASE-INT-09/14.

### D6 — Rejected proposals, seven domains and independent outstanding work

P3 records a real code change; P4 rejects every candidate. Candidate decisions become terminal, while a mandatory QA impact remains unresolved. C05's required-obligation predicate and blocked precedence keep coordination blocked where that obligation participates; proposal settlement alone cannot close it. With an authorized valid deferral, coordination may settle, but an unmet mandatory release requirement still prevents readiness. A cosmetic policy N/A has a reason and creates no fake policy; a required unsupported manual/policy capability remains a blocker or independently authorized scoped exception. **This is a resolved contract distinction, not a universal done badge.** A low-knowledge PM has explicit next owner/trigger and residual work in UX02/03. Sources: C1 R-ASE-02/20/48; C4 C01/C05; C5 I8/ASE-INT-16.

### D7 — Source rebind, independent releases and phase-aware proof

P3 observes source A/config1, rebinds to B/config2, then receives a delayed A event. C02 preserves A/config1 or quarantines/refuses it under current access; it cannot advance B. One source commit used by releases X and Y does not share their decisions. P6 can decide on tested candidate B1 before deployment; an observed deployed B2/unknown build prevents post-deployment acceptance. Failed smoke and later rollback/retest append facts rather than altering the original decision. A first single-source pilot labels/refuses incomplete multi-source claims. **G0 semantics hold; migration, providers and build proof remain open.** Sources: C4 C01/C02; C3§15; C5 ASE-INT-07/08/15.

### D8 — Lowest-knowledge review, denied payload and context switch

P4 opens an old pending change without the coding chat, follows an exact permitted artifact and returns with queue context/draft/focus. A restricted source exposes only approved summary fields, never private counts; denied/failed retrieval cannot masquerade as successful empty and prompt duplicate authoring. While draft A is open, switching to B fences delayed A; losing access does not copy A's private content into B. Unknown governance capability denies the action and identifies only a permitted recovery owner. **The written recovery contract is usable in principle; actual discovery, return navigation, dirty-state preservation and keyboard/AT completion remain INCONCLUSIVE without the authentic surface.** Sources: C2/C7 UX02/03; C4 C06; C5 ASE-INT-06/13/14.

# GREY / SUJT — Gate C initial journey receipt

**Sealed analytical receipt, 2026-11-19.** Reviewer: `/root/grey`. Target: Tesbo ASER integration proposal, PR149 source head `0c54b8ed28c9dec51ed0ca3bbb3cd3b7b8ab12d8`, development baseline `11fdbe70e5b63c6049b1dc0331be88daae0c62dc`; exact canonical v1.4.27 eight-file package plus the six ASER files identified in `RUN_CONTEXT.md`. This receipt does not review later author changes.

**Analytical verdict: MATERIAL JOURNEY GAPS. Formal Gate C certification: NOT CERTIFIED.** The proposed workflows support the intended connected product, but existing publication, execution and document-history seams cannot be treated as satisfying those workflows unchanged. The original external QA loop also needs its own end-to-end capability proof. This is a pre-design G0/G1 handoff, not a runtime, UI, security or release certification. Major findings await the independent second reviewer required by the unchanged Grey charter. Upstream Blue and Red receipts also retain formal quorum gates; their handoff permits this analysis, not silent certification of those gates.

## 1. Independence, sources and evidence meaning

It contains the four-source reconstruction, six executable personas, each row's actor/authorization, starting state, success oracle, alternative channels, authoritative owner and source crosswalk. **Source depth remains sampled.** The preparation receipt records the full source accounting: four method/context files, all six ASER documents and the whole canonical Workflow/UX specification were read; other canonical files were inventoried and relevant complete sections read. This is not a full semantic read of all approximately 147,000 canonical words or every current writer. Primary code was inspected directly, including selected complete methods and the MCP registered-name census; a cached file's presence is not evidence that its whole body was reviewed. Dated living-baseline decisions were used, not its historical runtime verdicts or images as a current baseline.

Aliases retained from preparation: **C0–C7** are the eight correspondingly numbered files in `input/canonical/`; **AR, AD, AP, AS, AT, A0** are ASER requirements, design, implementation plan, skills governance, agent-rule template and README in `input/Tesbooboo/docs/specs/agent-synchronized-engineering/`. **LB** is `input/Tesbo_Existing_Application_Baseline_v1.0.md`, version15, SHA256 `27adf97964df3094eb4fcbdf858e8d7851c3e5dc8676edef47c335d4ceff8854`, historical evidence only.

**G0_SIMULATED** below means an actual conceptual walk of the named initial state, actions and consequence against the supplied contract. **G1_CODE_GROUNDED** adds a directly inspected current source path; it does not mean code was executed. `HOLDS` is strictly the stated bounded design branch; `FAILS` is the stated source-to-contract or unchanged-reuse counterexample; `INCONCLUSIVE` names the missing integration or implementation evidence. None means runtime PASS. These predicates apply to different evidence layers deliberately, and each row states which layer controls its result.

## 3. Original QA round trip and hardest siblings

The primary job is not satisfied by a coding change record or a verification bundle alone. The retained sequence is **ChatGPT testing prompt → Tesbo MCP → assigned OpenCode using its own Chrome MCP → original evidence/report → Tesbo MCP → ChatGPT analysis/retest → user's exact review/sign-off**. The same record identities and authority boundaries must survive replacing ChatGPT, OpenCode or Chrome with another actually supported runtime/driver. Tesbo MCP supplies project coordination; the executor's driver reaches the target.

The examined G0 round trip starts with project P, zero cases/runs/models, no Tesbo AI key, external MCP explicitly enabled and current scoped access. P1 reviews prompt revision T1; P2 is assigned and claims attempt A1/fence F1. An unavailable target produces an honest blocked report, not a manufactured run. With an available target, reserved report R1 finalizes only after manifest/byte admission. ChatGPT reads R1's original content and separates observations from suggestions. A narrow retest yields T2/A2/R2 linked to R1 without overwriting it. Human review of proposal V1 can accept a case change, reject a result and defer a Bug. Only a subsequent exact dependency-closed apply may publish the selected operations. Neither report ingestion nor assistant analysis supplies the human decision. Changing a selected payload/base/policy requires a new valid decision. The source registry currently does not establish the task/claim/report/analysis/retest producers and consumers for this sequence; native generation-task saves and cycle-linked verification bundles are not equivalent evidence. Sources: C2 WF-01–08/§4/§10; C4 §2.1; C5 QA-135–140/159; C6 required complete demonstration; AD§13; S2/S4.

Hardest siblings assessed:

- **Zero official QA records:** exploratory evidence may be complete while case/run publication remains empty. Creating an artificial case to make evidence admissible violates the user's starting job. The canonical report contract handles this; implementation proof is W-C1.
- **All proposals rejected, real change remains:** a legitimate source change can leave open QA obligations despite no accepted artifact edits. Candidate status cannot be the only owner of source impact (GJ25/30/35; Blue B01/Red R01).
- **One accepted artifact, one deferred artifact:** exact manual or requirement acceptance must not imply case verification or release acceptance. A low-knowledge PM needs the outstanding action and owner, not a single ambiguous done badge (GJ11/21/25/35/36).
- **Raw failed-run evidence after skill revocation:** retain evidence under its independently valid permission; block promotion of governed authored content under stale skill authority. A blanket evidence ban would lose useful observations (GJ24/33; AS§12.4).
- **No hook, no provider, no model:** manual source reconciliation, explicit unknown freshness and conventional case-based testing remain valid. Optional integrations and models cannot become compulsory ceremony (GJ26/39/41; AR37/48).

These are constructive counterexamples, not claims about frequency or user research. No illustrative persona statement is an actual participant quotation.

## 4. Findings for independent challenge

### GT-01 — Approved case label can outlive the exact human-reviewed definition

**Severity/category:** candidate Major; trust gap, state inconsistency, cross-surface inconsistency. **Evidence:** CODE-PROVEN predicates plus source-grounded predicted consequence; no executed exploit. Journeys 11/12/13/15/33/47. Corroborates Blue B02/Red R02 with an independent user trace.

P1 believes an approved case can safely enter a run. In the concrete branch, exact content A is approved, a valid generic update changes a semantic field to B while omitting status, and a later run selects the case. S4 `updateTestCaseWithClient` uses `status=COALESCE($20,status)` and an optional expected revision; it does not make this update depend on a fresh exact approval. S4 insertion accepts `body.status || "Draft"`; S2 create/update expose status-capable native paths. The run policy uses current status Approved as eligibility. Thus status can be true without establishing an exact accepted version of the newly relied-on content. The user consequence is executing or reporting coverage against a changed oracle they did not approve.

**Counterevidence retained:** S4's dedicated approval endpoint requires expectedRevision and records a receipt transactionally; run insertion locks and rejects known unapproved same-project selections. These protections are real and should be extended, not replaced with an unrelated approval engine. This finding does not assert anonymous access, absence of all authorization, or that every current generic writer was executed.

**Exact falsifier:** on an authorized disposable project, approve content hash A with receipt H; edit an expected result to B through native REST and MCP generic update (including a stale/omitted revision case); attempt run insertion through each known insertion entrance. The claim is falsified if every protected path either refuses the unapproved semantic change, makes B ineligible until exact human review, or proves a valid receipt binding B and its governed fields at insertion. Compare testcase semantic revision, approval receipt, selected snapshot and inserted items, not just status. Also create/duplicate/import Approved input under a machine principal and prove no substitute human assent.

**Disposition:** preserve the useful locks/receipts, converge all protected publication and consumers on the approved exact version, and separately prove all writer/transport coverage. Owner: Blue/QA/Security with existing case owner; White gate W-C3. Formal confirmation pending independent reviewer.

### GT-02 — Associating a side Bug rewrites independent execution truth

**Severity/category:** candidate Major; unintended side effect, semantic mismatch, trust gap. **Evidence:** CODE-PROVEN shared service side effect, runtime unexecuted. Journeys17/18/36/42. This reconfirms a conflict already forbidden by the current canonical contract; novelty is not claimed.

Initial state: execution E is Passed by tester U1 at t1 for its stated condition; its run is not a finalized automated run. U2 notices an unrelated side defect and legitimately files a Bug linked to E. `createBug` commits the Bug/link, then invokes `failLinkedExecutions` (S4 lines8476–8555). That helper selects eligible linked executions and changes each non-Failed row to Failed, executed_at now, executed_by U2 (lines8195–8260), without a direct/side or expected-condition predicate. A user who only associated evidence has thereby changed result, execution time and performer. Notification/activity records follow the mutation, so the consequence extends beyond a misleading screen.

**Counterevidence retained:** project/link validation, stable locking and finalized automated-run protection are present. Already Failed executions do not change. This is not a claim that every Bug or link fails every run. The discriminating sibling is an ordinary writable Passed/Untested execution with a side observation, versus a sealed automated run and a genuine direct condition failure. C1 FR-071–079 and C2 WF-08–09 require classification and independently justified result semantics.

**Exact falsifier:** snapshot E's status, executed_at, executed_by, condition results and audit/history; create a same-project Bug with a side observation linked to E through native REST and MCP, then link an existing Bug through its alternate association path. The claim is falsified if association alone preserves those execution fields and a failure occurs only from an authorized explicit direct-condition outcome transition. Unlink must not silently fabricate recovery; deliberate correction/retest remains distinct. Also test a sealed automated run to retain its existing protection. Verify dependent activity/readiness projections against the actual before/after record.

**Disposition:** separate defect association from execution-result mutation at the shared owner; preserve genuine direct-failure workflow and sealed-run protections. Owner: QA + case/run/Bug service owners; White W-C3. Second-review confirmation pending.

### GT-03 — Ordinary KB snapshots cannot alone identify exact governed submissions

**Severity/category:** candidate Major conditional integration finding; history/recovery and trust gap. **Evidence:** CODE-PROVEN autosave mechanism plus SIMULATED-HYPOTHESIS about using it as the governed boundary. Journeys21/22/31/32/35/42. Corroborates Blue B03/Red R03; does not allege that the proposed governed adapter has shipped.

P4 reviews manual/policy/skill text B and later needs to inspect exactly what was submitted or accepted. The existing writer takes an older-body snapshot when the latest snapshot is outside its configured window; inside that window it changes the latest version's diff metadata and writes the new current body. A→B→C can therefore leave A as snapshot and C current without an immutable B object. The examined update also lacks a caller-supplied exact-version predicate at that write. If a new governed submission merely points at the mutable document/latest autosave history, neither the reviewer nor a later release owner can reliably recover exact B or prove stale approval detection. ASER requires immutable governed versions, so this is an integration obligation at a known seam, not a request to discard ordinary autosave.

**Counterevidence retained:** transactional document/version locks and ai_memory-specific approval reset/protected-memory editing exist. Candidate documents may be searchable to authorized reviewers as untrusted data; their mere appearance in search is not proof of activation or a leak. Draft coalescing is reasonable for an editor. Immutable submitted/accepted bytes and semantic metadata require a separate durable boundary at the same native owner.

**Exact falsifier:** edit A→B, submit B, then edit C within the snapshot window via a second authorized writer; approve the submitted B object after C exists, then retrieve/export exactly B and its metadata/authority source by stable ID/hash. This finding is falsified if B remains independently retrievable, immutable and distinguished from C, stale selection is handled, and the authoritative consumer resolves only the permitted accepted/assigned version. Also change a governed semantic metadata field without text changes. The test must leave ordinary draft autosave usable.

**Disposition:** explicitly bind immutable submitted/accepted versions and enforce them in review, retrieval and effective-skill consumers; retain ordinary draft behavior. Owners: KB/review/skills plus QA/Graph/Security; W-C2/W-C3. Conditional finding pending independent challenge.

### GI-01 — Preserve the original QA loop as a named capability acceptance gate

This is a **material integration prerequisite**, not a newly discovered contradiction in the full canonical design and not a formal Major defect against an intentionally proposed feature. C2/C4/C5 already specify the loop in detail. The inspected 58-name current MCP registry has no task/prompt/assignment/claim, original-report reserve/finalize, analysis or retest tool family. Existing native generation saves and AD§13 verification bundles linked to cycles/executions do not prove a zero-case external round trip. The finding is bounded to the inspected current registration path, not a whole-repository or external-client absence claim.

**Discriminating acceptance/falsifier:** complete §3 on an authorized disposable project with zero cases and no Tesbo AI key, use the assigned executor's actual driver, retrieve identical original report/evidence bytes through Tesbo, run a narrow linked retest, and obtain the human's exact mixed selected decisions plus separate publication. Repeat the relevant handoff with a second runtime/driver, and show disabled/read-only/stale-fence/manual-fallback branches. A demonstration that only creates a coding change and run bundle does not falsify this gap. Owner: implementation/QA; White W-C1.
