## 6. Independent reconciliation and White-owned dispositions

Blue/Red's extension/composition conclusion survives the user walks. Canonical change sets/events/impact work, case/run/KB/storage owners and the shared exact-review boundary are the useful starting points. New packet/skill/work/release vocabulary must map to those owners or show the invariant requiring a new linked record. Deleting names does not eliminate independent acceptance: approving a requirement or skill version cannot close a test obligation, assign the skill or approve a release.

Grey narrows or challenges upstream interpretations:

- The full canonical task contract already specifies single fenced claims, leases, expiry/clarification and resumed attempts (C2 §4.1/§10.3; C4 §2.1). An unimplemented general coding work-item lease cannot be treated as evidence that the original external-testing lease contract is unspecified. The question is implementation and correct owner reuse.
- A candidate's authorized discoverability is not its activation. Blue B04 is an integration consumer concern; neither source search admission nor a synthetic prompt establishes runtime instruction obedience, exfiltration or an ACL leak. Red's narrowing is retained.
- A bounded single-source pilot is a valid starting point. The relevant obligation is stable binding/configuration history and correct scope when it is changed or widened, not a mandate to build every provider now.
- Native requirements and original-loop tools are explicitly proposed capabilities. Their present source absence is a capability/milestone gap, not proof that the proposed requirement is missing or that an uninspected external client lacks its own tools.
- Ordinary autosave, exact case-approval receipts, same-project locks, eligible-case rejection, sealed-run protections and source capture are genuine existing safeguards. Findings concern the missing end-to-end invariant across those seams.

| Gate | Owner / concrete disposition before stronger claim |
|---|---|
| **W-C1 Original external QA loop** | White retains a named implementation/integration gate; QA demonstrates the exact zero-case/no-native-AI-key loop and original bytes, linked retest, explicit human decisions, manual/denied/stale branches and interchangeable actual runtimes. No coding-only bundle can substitute. |
| **W-C2 Canonical owner and status convergence** | Blue/Graph/Red reconcile change packet→canonical change/impact owner; general work→generation/Bug/impact owners; skill candidate/version/assignment→canonical standards/pack; requirement/native typed identity; source config and release snapshot identities. Show all-rejected and partially accepted changes with open downstream obligations. Exact policy/root-recovery product decisions stay explicit. |
| **W-C3 All protected writers/readers and independent finding review** | QA independently challenges GT-01/02/03; Blue/Security map every applicable native/entity REST, both MCP aliases, bulk/import/clone, Zyra, SDK/automation/scheduler, provider and background path. Test principal class separately from issuer role and compare authoritative before/after values. This mandatory high-risk entrance gate remains open for all affected inventory rows. |
| **W-C4 Runtime, concurrency, storage and operational evidence** | Authorized disposable tests prove immutable report/governed bytes, retries/uncertain commits, stale fences, snapshots, migrations/unknown history, queues, events/order/gaps, access changes, exports/public sharing, real clients/target execution, release and incident recovery. All49 rows lack live observation; no current runtime identity was established. |
| **W-C5 Authentic UI and lowest-knowledge completion** | Purple/QA obtain authentic current source-to-runtime baseline and walk role-specific navigation, read/return, pending/unknown/conflict states, draft recovery, keyboard/AT and responsive behavior. No visual parity, ease-of-use or accessibility PASS from this receipt. |
| **W-C6 Quorum, coverage and delta control** | Independent Grey Major reviewer plus QA/Graph/Blue reconciliation of the discovered inventory and outstanding branch ledger; retain upstream quorum limits. A changed spec/source requires a separate delta receipt against affected journeys and consumers. Preserve this initial receipt and preparation unchanged. |

**High-risk disposition:** all43 high-risk IDs have a bounded trace; the concrete failing source branches are GT-01/02/03, and all unproved entrances/runtime/UI/owner consequences remain the named gates above. No high-risk branch is silently converted from unexecuted to passed. Inventory accountability is supplied; exhaustive branch execution, complete repository writer coverage, live verification and formal full-assurance completion are not claimed. This receipt is ready for White's pre-gate consideration with those limits.

The broader coding-change → applicable engineering records → verification/release/aftercare loop joins these same records; it does not replace the QA loop.

**Materiality rule:** one row is a distinct actor job with an independently assessable outcome. Failure/retry/role variants are branches within rows, not extra jobs used to inflate the denominator. ### All-entry boundary obligation (not yet executed)

For authorization, official publication/deletion, tenant isolation, target actions, confidential data movement and irreversible effects, the initial receipt must cross **every known applicable entrance** above. A new composite MCP facade does not excuse legacy CRUD, import, native Zyra, SDK, background or direct REST. An unavailable/uninspected entrance is `UNVERIFIED` and a named continuation gate, not N/A. Machine-owned versus interactive-human context must be tested independently of token issuer role. Source-level guard presence does not establish a runtime denial.

## 6. Planned branch and escaped-pattern ledger

Each high-risk row will include H (happy), N (denied/negative), B (boundary/missing), M (mutation), F (failure/retry), X (cross-module/channel), L (history/longitudinal) and R (recovery) or an explicit N/A reason. No full Cartesian claim. Mandatory mechanism variants:

1. **Late response after context switch:** GJ04/05/19–21/30/35/39/42; return data/draft must stay in original project/session/source/revision or be ignored, never overwrite the new selection.
2. **Ambiguous commit and same logical retry:** GJ01/06/08–09/11–18/21–24/26–28/30–33/36–37/40–41/45/47–48; exact receipt reconciliation before replay, changed key payload conflicts, distinct valid operations remain distinct.
3. **Valid action mutates unintended secondary truth:** especially GJ11/14/17/20–22/30/32/36/40–41/44–45; compare before/after official case/result/approval/assignment/pointer/access/event facts, not only a success toast.
4. **Alternative writer/transport:** all guarded native record mutation rows, including existing MCP aliases, entity REST, legacy tools, bulk/import, Zyra saves, SDK/automation/scheduled jobs and provider intake where applicable.
5. **Role/revocation mid-flight and history:** all high-risk read/write rows; historical identity remains while current access is rechecked. Missing proof is not automatically a missing producer.

QA supplies its final escaped-failure oracle ledger after its own stage. Grey has prepared concrete triggers independently; this preparation is not a claim of received QA results.

## 7. Denominator and continuation gates

- **Inventory:** 49 provisional material journeys, of which **43 high-risk** and **6 medium-risk** (medium: 23,34,38,39,43,49).
- **Simulated:** 0; **runtime observed:** 0; **blocked journey statuses:** 0 at preparation; **unreviewed/NOT_SIMULATED:** 49. The Gate C dependency itself is blocked, not a product failure verdict.
- **Dimensions:** both native/external entry paths; seven ASER applicability domains; original task/report/retest/human loop; requirements/cases/plans/runs/Bugs/KB/skills/activity/settings; source/change/provider/release/incident continuation; typed/model-based paths; data retention/export/share; migration/legacy writers and role changes.
- **Source depth:** four sources supplied, but source 4 is sampled. Runtime, native UI screenshots, keyboard/AT/reflow, all real current queues/providers, whole-repository writer completeness and real external clients are unexecuted/unverified. The 49-row denominator is bounded to discovered material integration scope, subject to Blue/Red reconciliation.
- **Reasoned exclusion:** no money/payment-purchase journey, marketplace semantics, default global DMIS/Chrome/Appium dependency, new hosted executor product, or arbitrary external posting is added. Existing account/billing/entitlement internals are prerequisites checked for access/lock/recovery effects (GJ46/47), not newly commissioned payment or identity redesign. Optional unsupported adapters stay NOT_SUPPORTED; absence does not block a supported external-browser pilot.

**G-C-DEPENDENCY:** Root must hand off completed Blue and Red receipts before canonical Gate C completes.  
**G-C-COVERAGE:** Grey then reconciles any missing owners/entrypoints, runs all high-risk G0/G1 traces, assigns exactly one status/result per row and issues `receipts/grey-initial.md`. Any untraced high-risk row is a White-owned outstanding gate.  
**G-C-QUORUM:** Major/Critical formal findings require a second independent journey reviewer; until then they remain independently reproduced candidates.  
**G-C-RUNTIME:** Later authorized disposable-project native/API/MCP/DB/queue/client tests, original report byte round-trip and authentic current UI/AT evidence are separate release gates. No simulator result closes them.  
**G-C-DELTA:** Material changes to source/spec require a separately recorded delta re-review of changed journeys and consumers.

No formal product verdict is issued in this preparation receipt. No target or charter was edited.

## 3. Disposition of Purple's independent findings

| Finding | Actual v3 evidence and assessment | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **PUR-PREP-01 — Skills placement fork (P1)** | Canonical ASER-UX02 now explicitly extends **Zyra → Change review** and places searchable Skills Library plus separately protected Assignments inside **D-14 QA Standards & Playbooks**. View design §7/§18.1 and skills-governance §7 use the same placement. Existing settings/context links and one T-422 resolver remain the owners. | **Resolved at proposed specification level.** No new top-level Skills page is authorized. Actual rendered placement and discoverability remain untested. |
| **PUR-PREP-02 — Job/action/disclosure mapping (P1)** | ASER-UX02 supplies a concrete eight-job table with safe information, required server capability and next owner. A solo person may do several jobs; title, project membership, KB ownership and machine-token ownership confer no extra governance authority. Unknown mapping denies only the affected action. ASER-UX03 specifies permitted denial/recovery and restricted-evidence handling; ASER-C03/C06 cover independent human evidence and read surfaces. | **Specification remediation satisfied; actual authorization admission remains OPEN.** This is sufficient to stop an implementer inventing rights from persona labels. It does not establish the real principal/role mapping, approved human appointments, summary field allowlist or positive/denied API/UI tests. Those are explicitly required before exposure. |
| **PUR-PREP-03 — Exact review/return continuity (P2)** | ASER-UX03 explicitly preserves project/changeset/exact artifact-revision-operation-or-attempt identity, validated return context, queue filters, selection/scroll where applicable, comments, keyboard focus, historical comparison, pending pagination and response fencing. ASER-C02–04 prevents mutable-latest reliance, silent replay and generic retry of uncertain effects. Plan F5 and ASE-INT-13 bind the connected journey to the existing native entry and owners. | **Resolved at proposed specification level.** The existing source route was already present; v3 correctly extends it rather than inventing a missing entry. Browser navigation, draft retention and focus behavior remain NOT RUN. |
| **PUR-PREP-04 — Failed Requirements reads become empty (P1 source risk)** | View design §18.2 explicitly identifies the pinned failure-to-empty mechanism. Plan's **Requirements owner + A/F4/F5** row requires successful-empty versus unavailable/partial/denied/stale states, timeout/403/partial-source and source/project-switch tests. ASER-UX03/C06 forbids false zero, duplicate creation and inaccessible-as-N/A; ASE-INT-14 adds a successful-empty neighboring case. The plan expressly says these source conditions are not repaired by the documentation. | **Remains OPEN as an implementation/source risk; correctly allocated as a prerequisite.** No code fix or runtime reproduction is claimed. It does not require another speculative UI or a duplicate Requirements store. |

The distinctions in this table are intentional. A written clarification can resolve a proposal ambiguity. It cannot close a source defect, establish an unconfigured authority or supply the missing current UI evidence.

## 4. Connected semantic challenge of the corrected candidate

The following are actual desk/source-grounded challenges of the v3 contract. They are **not executed journeys**.

| Challenge / valid alternative | v3 result at the specification boundary |
|---|---|
| BA, QA and PM inspect the same change; one person may hold several permitted capabilities | Common `changesetId` and exact native records survive across jobs. Requirement acceptance, case review/run/correction, summary access and release decision remain separate capabilities. Default denial plus an identified configuration/next-owner gate is explicit. This resolves the original mapping ambiguity without creating eight roles or dashboards. |
| PM needs actionable progress while policy/history/evidence is restricted | The allowed summary is independent of protected payload access; next-owner disclosure is itself permission-limited. Unknown or restricted evidence cannot become zero, passed or N/A. The actual allowlist and endpoint/cache implementation remain admission work. |
| Skill v2 is approved but v1 remains assigned; assignment then changes mid-edit | Skills Library and Assignments stay separate subviews/decisions. A candidate remains inspectable as untrusted data, and the exact active manifest governs authoring. Revalidation preserves candidates and history on stale context; an agent cannot use candidate content or owner credentials to activate v2. Planned ASE-INT-01/02/06/11 discriminates the relevant alternatives. |
| A reviewer opens an old link, edits a comment, drills to a native run, then switches project while a response is late | The contract preserves the exact historical revision and valid return state, distinguishes current comparison, and fences late data/permissions. Existing source request guards are preserved, not claimed to prove the entire new interaction. ASE-INT-13 requires actual UI/keyboard/AT evidence separately. |
| A composite stages one artifact, times out on another and later encounters changed skills | ASER-C04 distinguishes exact-duplicate readback from explicit resume with expected journal revision and a new attempt. Uncertain effects are reconciled first; changed intent/dependencies require the necessary new candidate/review without replaying earlier commitments. Selected official apply remains atomic under C03; partial staging does not become partial human authorization. |
| Every proposal operation is rejected, or a small patch needs no new policy/manual | C01 separates applicability, work disposition and per-operation decision. C05 allows rejected-only coordination settlement while independent QA/release duties remain open. Seven assessments do not force seven mutations. ASE-INT-16 includes both a cosmetic positive case and required-but-unsupported work that cannot silently pass. |
| A code-first change has no model/case; separately an external QA task has no existing cases or native AI key | ASER-UX01 retains both entry paths and the original assigned-executor/original-report/retest/human-selection loop. Coding-change receipts do not replace it. Optional model preview is not made an entry requirement. This is specified, not demonstrated. |
| Pre-deployment decision is legitimate; production is not yet observable; later a different build deploys or smoke fails | R-ASE-42, design §15 and C01 now separate readiness, human decision, deployment and post-deploy proof. No impossible prerequisite for future production proof is imposed on the initial decision. Ordinary release without known risks has an ordinary label; the known-risk label requires an observed deployment and actual approved risk set. Neither is a safety guarantee. |

These challenges support the disposition above at the contract level. They do not measure comprehension, time-to-complete, accessibility, trusted interactive-human proof, migration feasibility or real execution. No benchmark/competitor assertion was independently researched for this Purple delta; those are outside its claim scope.

## 1. What this preparation establishes

Tesbo's proposed human experience is a coordinated change review across existing product records. A useful result lets a person determine what changed, why it matters, which exact version is being discussed, who can act, what evidence exists, what remains uncertain, and how to continue from that uncertainty. A source observation, an agent's statement, a saved candidate, a human approval, a published artifact, a run result, and an authorized release decision answer different questions. They cannot share an undifferentiated green completion label.

The input already states many of these distinctions well. The proposed Change Packet is a coordination receipt, not another requirement/case/evidence store. Machine credentials do not provide human assent. Skills publication and assignment are independent. Optional model previews and code-first work do not require a new modeling ceremony. Those constraints should survive correction. This is a source/specification preparation for later design review; it does **not** establish that the proposed experience is implemented, usable in a live tenant, visually faithful, accessible, or effective with real users.

There are four bounded concerns below. Two concern the specificity and reconciliation of the proposal, one concerns its navigation/recovery acceptance contract, and one identifies an existing source mechanism that must be accounted for when extending Requirements. These are independent findings, not a count target. The missing live evidence is recorded separately, not disguised as a discovered product defect.

The 2,923-line design-assurance charter was sampled in its status/ratification and application sections, active design-pass rules, purpose/hierarchy advisory sections, whole-page journey rule, and severity/verdict section; I do not claim a full line-by-line execution of that charter. Its status is **draft, unratified**, version `PURPLE-2026.10.18-r7`.

These are supported by Tesbo policy, not imported from another application. |

Their runtime satisfaction is not assumed. |

| Unratified machinery | `PUR-GATE-001`, an ART artifact package, PUR-FC closeout, and a full Graph/release closeout are **NOT RUN**. This receipt does not activate them. |

## 4. Reconstructed jobs, visibility and action semantics

These are user jobs, **not proposed new RBAC roles**. One authorized person may do several jobs. A job title, owner relationship or administrator's machine token cannot substitute for the operation's actual authorization. Canonical `02_WORKFLOW_AND_UX.md:507–515` expressly leaves the exact QA closure role open; this receipt does not settle it.

| Job / starting context | Required truthful information | Legitimate action and recovery contract to make concrete |
|---|---|---|
| Solo developer or human coding contributor; often starts with code | Exact source revision, current approved requirement/skill context, observed versus reported activity, seven-domain impact dispositions, remaining proof | Continue from small safe work; preserve candidates when context changes; propose changes and follow native approvals. A small change may justify unchanged or not-applicable domains. |
| External coding or testing agent | Same authorized facts and exact governing revisions, plus actual tool availability and scope | Additive candidate work and per-artifact receipts through existing owners. No human review/approval/assignment by token. Missing capability leaves local work honestly unsynchronized and resumable. |
| QA engineer / reviewer | Approved criteria, changed case candidates, link versus coverage versus execution, exact run/build/evidence and known gaps | Review exact permitted revisions, select approved cases for native runs, investigate failures, preserve incomplete evidence and resume safely after uncertain create/apply results. |
| BA / product contributor | Intent, acceptance criteria, requirement ownership, downstream effects and disagreements | Propose or review requirements using granted capabilities; do not silently ratify code as its own oracle. Explain an unavailable source or restricted decision without leaking private content. |
| PM / delivery coordinator | Owner, current blocker, accountable next actor, partial outcomes and evidence confidence | Route or follow up on work and inspect permitted readiness facts. Overview access need not imply policy-edit, skill-activation or release authority. |
| Policy/security reviewer or authorized skill governor | Exact candidate/current versions, authority and audience, affected consumers, approval versus active assignment, conflict/revocation | Make separately authorized decisions; preview impact; retain historical pins. A required missing/conflicting skill must have an accountable resolution path and preserve the author's work. |
| Support/manual/operator contributor | Intended audience and approved product behavior, manual version, source/access boundary | Update the correct native document with its approval/history; external-facing text must not expose internal policy, tool payloads or private history merely because it links to them. |
| Release decision-maker / executive reader | Exact readiness snapshot, build and environment, proof gaps, accepted risks and authorized decision record | Inspect and decide only with explicit rights. A green engineering summary does not prove deployment, post-deploy verification or release authorization. Read-only readers still need a safe owner/blocker view. |

The information architecture should reuse the current Zyra/Change review entry as the coordination view, Requirements and cases for their native records, KB/manuals for content, Tasks for work, native run pages for execution, and the existing settings/guide family for method governance. This is a proposed integration map grounded in the inspected routes, not a claim that the full ASER implementation already exists.

At least these independent facts must remain separately legible: domain applicability; candidate saved/reviewed/published state; exact human operation selection; global skill approval versus active scoped assignment; source-observation trust; native execution result; build/environment match; evidence sufficiency; and release decision. The proposed packet/readiness summaries may derive from these facts. They must not replace their native owners or fabricate a new universal completion state.

## 5. Independent findings

Severity below describes expected user consequence if implemented literally. It is not a charter release verdict. Source-observed mechanisms are separated from proposed-design uncertainty.

### PUR-PREP-01 — Skills destination has an unresolved canonical placement fork

**Priority:** P1 proposal reconciliation. **Evidence:** binding Tesbo placement rule; advisory purpose/placement analysis. **Not a proven runtime defect.**

Canonical `07_UI_DESIGN_TASKS.md:439–449` explicitly extends D-14 “QA Standards & Playbooks” rather than a new Skills page. Canonical backlog T-451 (`05_IMPLEMENTATION_BACKLOG_AND_TEST_PLAN.md:970`) extends existing Project Settings and Copilot, and says not to create a new page/application. ASER `design.md:153`, `skills-governance.md:62–69` and implementation S1 (`implementation-plan.md:39`) introduce “Skills Library” navigation/UI and a separate “Skill Assignments” governance view without an explicit placement disposition against that existing constraint.

The logical concepts are useful: searching content differs from changing effective assignments. The ambiguity is whether those concepts mean subviews of the existing owner, an unrelated destination, or an approved change to canonical navigation. An implementer should not have to infer which instruction wins. A new top-level library could split discovery, make the effective-method source harder to find, and duplicate governance context.

**Bounded correction:** specify the intended placement within the existing settings/guide family and contextual entry points, with separate permissioned assignment controls; reconcile the same decision in the canonical eight before implementation. If a different placement is desired, record the actual owner decision and resulting canonical change rather than silently importing it through the proposal.

**Valid counterexample / falsifier:** a searchable library and separately gated assignment subview inside D-14, reached from existing context, satisfies both concepts without a new Skills subsystem. The phrase “library” alone does not prove a violation. A later explicit, authorized canonical placement decision would resolve the ambiguity.

### PUR-PREP-02 — Role-sensitive overview lacks a concrete action and disclosure mapping

**Priority:** P1 implementation/design gate; proposal can remain explicitly unresolved. **Evidence:** specification gap against target authorization/truth rules. **No current authorization bypass alleged.**

R-ASE-41 and R-ASE-46 (`requirements.md:227–230,252–255`) promise a common role-sensitive Change Workspace and a PM-safe failure/owner view without restricted payloads. `design.md:271–292` lists the common information areas, while implementation F5 (`implementation-plan.md:133–136`) still says to define role-specific information needs. The input does not yet map those jobs to the actual permissions for viewing private source/history, proposing, reviewing, publishing, policy approval, skill assignment and release decision. Its independent protections are strong, but a developer still has to invent some rendered denied/unknown states and next-actor paths.

Existing KB UI role normalization is not that missing contract. Nor is canonical “human reviewer” permission to assign skills or release. An overview can be useful to a PM without granting the PM the underlying decision. Conversely, hiding every blocked item's existence may make ownership/recovery impossible; exact permitted summary disclosure needs an explicit decision, not a generic “role-appropriate” promise.

**Bounded correction:** add a compact proposed capability/visibility/state matrix using the current authorization model. Include permission unknown, denied, revoked, missing required skill, stale context, restricted evidence and partial result; for each name the safe displayed facts, permitted action and accountable next actor. Keep undecided product rights visibly open and gate only the affected action. Reuse native operation authorization rather than creating new roles merely to match the job table.

**Valid counterexample / falsifier:** the same authorized user can perform BA, QA and PM jobs without separate personas/accounts. A shared page with capability-filtered sections and accurate denied-state guidance is sufficient. This finding does not require eight separate dashboards or make all role decisions prerequisites for read-only preparation.

### PUR-PREP-03 — Connected review and return-state promises need explicit acceptance anchoring

**Priority:** P2 proposal specificity; affected interaction gate before UI acceptance. **Evidence:** existing route plus stronger canonical interaction contracts. **Not an absent-entry finding.**

R-ASE-41 promises drill-down into native proof and return context. The proposed Change Workspace lists approvals, impact and remaining work, but the ASER description does not explicitly anchor those journeys to the canonical exact-revision/operation links, durable pending discovery, stale-review handling and project-safe return-state contracts. Those are already defined in `02_WORKFLOW_AND_UX.md:228–292` and `07_UI_DESIGN_TASKS.md:174–184,539–571`; they should be inherited rather than reinvented.

Actual source already has **Zyra → Change review**, native QA navigation, observation paging and same-run recovery. Calling the new experience a “Change Workspace” must not obscure that existing entry or reset a reviewer to an unrelated top-level page after inspecting a requirement/run. A notification read marker is also not an assessment or review decision.

**Bounded correction:** reference the existing canonical contracts from the ASER acceptance slice. Specify the entry/queue, stable packet plus exact artifact/revision/operation identity, accessible native drill-down, return position/filter/project context, stale revision behavior, and preservation of in-progress comments/edits. In blocked states identify a real next action or owner; avoid a generic retry when the prior operation's outcome is uncertain.

**Valid counterexample / falsifier:** existing route/query-state and common review primitives may satisfy this without any new navigation component. A direct linked native record can remain a valid alternative entry. The correction is a connected acceptance contract, not a compulsory new screen or wizard.

### PUR-PREP-04 — Requirements source converts link-read failures into successful-empty data

**Priority:** P1 source risk to truthful coverage/next-action projection; user-visible failure still needs execution proof. **Evidence:** exact pinned source, not runtime reproduction.

In `requirements/page.tsx:428–468`, each failed Jira/Linear/Notion linked-key read resolves to `{ keys: [], counts: {}, tasks: {} }`, and those values replace the current local link/count/task maps. Summary failure becomes `null`. The inspected derivations around 303–355 use these maps for link/count/task projections, while the page renders coverage metrics and source-oriented empty states around 734–849. Within this path, a failed read has lost its distinction from a successful read of no links. That is a concrete source mechanism; I have not exercised the live failure or established every final rendered combination.

This matters to ASER because a reviewer could be directed toward “uncovered”, “not started” or new case work when the data is unavailable. It conflicts with the target's existing requirement to distinguish unavailable from zero (`07_UI_DESIGN_TASKS.md:408`) and with R-ASE-03/05/19/41 reuse, evidence and comprehension goals. It is an independently observed unresolved mechanism already anticipated by canonical policy, not claimed as a newly discovered historical issue.

**Bounded correction:** include explicit unavailable/stale/denied status at the existing Requirements owner before using these projections for ASER reconciliation. Preserve permitted prior data with an honest stale label when appropriate; prevent decisions that require an unknown fact. Verify successful empty separately from timeout/403/partial provider failure, and verify source/project switching. Record the upstream/API result as well as the UI result.

**Valid counterexample / falsifier:** an authenticated successful empty result should still show zero. A later demonstrated owner-level mechanism that retains the failure distinction in every affected rendering could narrow the severity. This finding does not mandate exposing restricted counts or changing native coverage semantics.

## 6. Independent counterexamples for the corrected design

These are proposed adversarial examples, not executed test results or a replacement for the input's ASE/SK scenario suites.

| Counterexample | Required distinction / acceptable outcome |
|---|---|
| A solo developer makes a small code-first correction without a model or new manual/policy | Assess relevant domains and record justified unchanged/not-applicable results; allow the smallest useful next action. Do not generate empty artifacts to make seven progress marks green. Optional model preview remains optional. |
| Skill v2 is published but not assigned; v1 remains active for a project | Library approval and current effective assignment remain separate. Historical work still points to its actual pinned version. A machine token cannot activate v2. |
| A required assignment is absent or conflicts while a candidate is already being edited | Preserve the candidate, show why the dependent action is blocked, and identify an authorized resolution path. Do not pretend the candidate was approved or lose the edit on context refresh. |
| A PM opens a failed policy change whose source/history is restricted | Reveal only the permitted failure/owner/next-step facts. Authorized reviewers can reach allowed proof; the PM's overview must not disclose hidden source titles, content or tool payloads by accident. |
| A requirement changes after a review link was issued | Show the exact historical candidate and current applicability/staleness; preserve input and require the appropriate fresh decision. Do not approve the latest revision under the old link. |
| A composite commits one native artifact, fails another and loses the third response | Per-artifact committed/failed/uncertain receipts and a safe resume path; no blind retry that duplicates the uncertain artifact and no whole-packet success label. |
| A project has no issue-tracker integration but needs a native requirement | The proposed native requirement owner must provide a permitted path. The inspected current “Connect an Issue Tracker” empty state is not acceptance proof for this future journey. |
| An old project's observation or source response arrives after switching project | Preserve the existing request/context fencing; the new project's view cannot inherit old source facts, actions or permissions. Source guards were observed, but no runtime race test was performed. |
| A source report matches a commit but the deployed build is unknown | Report the match at its actual evidence tier. Do not infer execution, environment coverage, deployed verification or release approval. |
| A release is ordinary and no known risks are recorded | The design should clearly express the authorized decision through its real native lifecycle. Do not force “released with known risks” as the only human interpretation; readiness vocabulary is already marked pending canonical reconciliation. This is a design question, not a demand to invent another status store. |

## 8. Handoff boundary

This preparation is complete and frozen as an input-only receipt. It authorizes no implementation, publication, skill assignment or release. It does not approve the final design.

After White's pre-gate and a separately identified corrected snapshot are handed off, Purple can review the delta against these findings and the preserved primary constraints. That later receipt should state which ambiguities were resolved, which new consequences arose, and whether any finding remains disputed. A full user-facing design verdict still requires the applicable artifact/runtime evidence; the absence of that evidence should remain explicit rather than be converted into a source-only PASS.

The five charter failure families remain applicable. Their current status is **SIMULATED_ONLY for the bounded conceptual challenges, OPEN for implementation evidence**; none is TESTED:

- **Temporal/context binding:** A→B project/source/assignment switch with delayed A must preserve A or reject, never mutate B. UX03 and INT-07/13 address it; real callbacks, independent tabs and all consumer caches remain untested.

INT-05/14 specify discriminating cases. Logical commitment retention, concurrency and expiry remain native proof obligations.
- **Causal side effects:** case edits, Bug links, autosave and rejected-only proposals cannot rewrite independent approved/executed truth or hide open impact. INT-02/03/04/16 address these mechanisms; the wider projections/outbox/audit/export chain remains open.
- **All-entrance equivalence:** C03/C06 and R-ASE-34 require native/entity REST, both MCP aliases, imports/bulk/clone/restore, Zyra, jobs, integrations and consumers. This is an inventory obligation, not evidence that those paths were enumerated and tested.
- **Outcome-oracle integrity:** raw report receipt, candidate count, case link, CI green, model path, coordination settled and emitted notification remain distinct from target execution, human approval, bytes, deployment, impact closure and delivery. INT-08–16 preserve the necessary distinctions; actual producer-to-consumer proof is still absent.

## 5. Original loop and cross-domain continuation

QA conceptually replayed the original loop using the preserved specification, not a running system:

1. Enabled project, zero official cases/runs/models, no Tesbo model key: exact ChatGPT prompt/task T1 is persisted and dispatched without generating a case.
2. Assigned OpenCode instance claims A1/F1 and uses its own authorized Chrome/Playwright or equivalent supported driver. Missing driver is blocked/untested, never Pass; server lease expiry does not claim to kill the local process.
3. R1 is reserved, required evidence uploaded and finalized, then the analyst retrieves original permitted bytes. Missing/quarantined bytes do not become complete from metadata.
4. Ambiguity yields a linked narrow T2/A2/R2 with new evidence, preserving R1. Material changed evidence can stale affected proposal decisions.
5. Human edits/rejects/approves exact selected operations. Protected apply independently verifies current authority/dependencies and publishes only the approved dependency-closed subset once, with mandatory audit. Copy-only or rejected Bug items create no native defect or outbound issue.
6. Fresh client/session discovers that lineage; manual fallback retains the true submitter. A second supported runtime/client must prove equivalent identities and boundaries, not merely share a vendor label.

UX01, I3-Q and ASE-INT-12 expressly preserve this path; original QA-135–140/159 and T-404 provide operation-specific and second-project/client proof handoffs. A coding-source receipt or cycle-linked verification bundle cannot replace it. The 75 new scenario labels are complementary to those original gates.

The corresponding code-first trace retains one changeset and exact source configuration, seven justified domain assessments, native staged operations, partial receipt/resume, independent QA impacts and role-safe review. Rejected-only proposals may settle while independent work remains open; required unsupported domains block relevant readiness. Pre-deployment approval binds the tested candidate and risk context; deployed build observation and smoke follow separately. A wrong build, policy drift, failed smoke, rollback/fix-forward and later retest preserve prior decision/attempt history. These G0 consequences are coherent under C01–06. Actual provider, native transaction, queue, visibility, target-build and operational evidence remains required.

## 7. QD-V2-01 — Four scenario definitions lack a direct proof-column edge

**Category:** traceability/proof handoff. **Evidence:** C5's 48-row requirement crosswalk and the complete 75 scenario definitions, independently parsed and read; mirrored implementation-plan.md contains the same crosswalk. **Impact:** a reverse trace asking which R-ASE requirement requires one of these cases cannot be answered directly from that column. A 48-row count alone conceals this omission.

The four IDs are **SK-E2E-18, SK-E2E-23, SK-E2E-24 and SK-E2E-26**. Their definitions still exist and their meaning is supported by the contract: modified payload after review needs new approval; disabled skill does not forbid separately authorized raw observation; composite candidate actions require their per-operation skills/guards; skill format cannot override approved policy.

**Disposition:** add direct references to appropriate existing R-ASE proof rows, without inventing new scenario IDs or execution results. Multiple sensible associations are possible; requirement mapping is not a claim that one test proves an entire requirement. For example exact-review non-escalation can link to R-ASE-23; observation/disabled-skill behavior to R-ASE-37; composite behavior to R-ASE-39; independent policy consistency to R-ASE-40. These links require a new snapshot/delta, not editing this frozen receipt.

**Falsifier:** enumerate the 75 distinct definitions, expand slash/range references in the proof column and verify every defined scenario has at least one justified requirement edge while every reference resolves. Keep a negative control removing an edge to detect recurrence. This proves trace accounting only. Actual test execution and discriminating assertions remain separate.

The original 48 rows are otherwise individually present and their references resolve; no requirement or scenario definition was lost. Some broad requirements naturally need more operation-specific fixtures than their short proof column can encode. Before implementation acceptance, the required per-task evidence record must instantiate each critical invariant, denial/recovery and valid neighboring case rather than interpreting a linked scenario name as complete coverage.

## 8. Required handoff and final limits

The correction now makes the principal owner/lifecycle choices and test boundaries concrete enough for this bounded QA design challenge: one changeset/derived workspace; T-422/D-14 guidance owner; shared staged operations and real native atomic apply; exact governed submissions; independent source/release contexts; one consistency vocabulary; explicit receipt/resume; and phase-aware release outcomes. This is a proposed allocation and testable contract, not proof the chosen storage/current implementation satisfies it.

Retain the named gates from White and qa-initial:

- Exact current writer/reader/consumer inventory, native transaction participation, migrations/legacy exceptions, stable commitment retention, current schema and source/tool/build identity.
- Actual human requirement/policy/release capability mapping, interactive proof, root recovery/quorum, precedence/no-skill policy, retention/holds and operating limits.
- Authorized API/DB/MCP/client/queue/storage/history/concurrency/provider tests with authoritative before/after, side-effect closure and valid positive controls.
- Original real-client own-driver/report-byte/retest loop, including manual/denied/stale/unsupported branches; two-runtime portability where claimed.
- Authentic current UI Before/After, navigation/return/drafts, loading/denied/stale/partial states, responsive/keyboard/AT evidence. Status remains SOURCE-REVIEWED / VISUAL-BASELINE BLOCKED.
- Independent Graph/White and formal role/quorum dispositions at the exact reviewed revision; no specialist or human decision supplied by a structural checker.

No product test, DB query, API call, browser action, target-driver session, outbound notification, production change, live permission check or deployment validation was executed in this delta. No new user-study result or measured quality benefit is asserted. This receipt preserves qa-initial and is ready for the independent Graph/White reconciliation of **v2 with QD-V2-01 open and all implementation/runtime gates retained**.

## 2. Independent outcome oracles, recorded before Grey

| Oracle | Required authoritative outcome |
|---|---|
| O1 Scope | Every read and writer uses current tenant/project/resource/principal, credential, opt-in, role, plan and visibility. Switching or revocation cannot expose disabled/foreign work; permitted reads remain permitted. |
| O2 Authority | Draft, exact human approval, selected protected apply and release decision are distinct. An issuer's identity or delegated native browser session is not human assent. A rightful human positive path remains usable. |
| O3 Independent expectation | Approved expectations are independent of implementation observations. Missing expectations produce UNKNOWN. Conforming behavior and justified N/A are not forced to create records. |
| O4 External execution | Zero-case/no-native-AI-key tasks persist exact prompt/context; one assigned instance claims a fenced attempt and uses its own authorized target driver. Cancel/revoke does not claim to undo earlier external actions. |
| O5 Original evidence | Reserve/upload/finalize produces complete immutable originals and required bytes, without official case/result/Bug mutation. Manual submitter is truthful; retest is a new linked task/attempt/report. |
| O6 Publication | Exact selected dependency-closed protected publication and mandatory audit share their required transaction. Cross-owner draft preparation may be partial with journals. Staging/copy does not silently create results, Bugs or outbound actions. |
| O7 Recovery identity | Stable logical operation plus request/payload identity makes uncertain commits recoverable. Identical retry returns the receipt; changed intent conflicts; a fresh transport key cannot republish an already committed logical item. |
| O8 Temporal binding | In-flight work and drafts remain bound to A after context switches to B. A late response updates A or is refused, never B. Cancellation alone does not prove persistence safety. |
| O9 History | Original definition/attempt/source/skill/approval provenance remains recoverable and immutable. Reconstruction stays labelled UNKNOWN/reconstructed; edits and deletion cannot silently rewrite accepted truth. |
| O10 Domain continuation | Every relevant domain has an owned disposition and next action. Cosmetic changes need no artificial artifacts; governed effects cannot hide behind unsupported unchanged/N/A labels. |
| O11 Trace meaning | Links retain direction, exact revision/occurrence, validity and scope. Citation, relationship, approval, selection and executed assertion are distinct coverage denominators. |
| O12 Skills | Immutable candidate/version, approval and active assignment are separate. Effective resolution and drift checks occur at protected reliance. Root recovery is independently authorized; existing writers share enforcement; old provenance is not fabricated. |
| O13 Usable projections | Roles see the same underlying identities/statuses without gaining restricted content. Pending work is discoverable and resumable without private chat or pasted identifiers. |
| O14 Delivery | One change continues through build/deploy attempts, smoke, incident, rollback/fix-forward and new retest. Missing captures/events/side effects show incomplete state and a recovery owner while preserving earlier facts. |
| O15 Metrics | Sources, denominator, time window and qualification rules are explicit. Missing telemetry is NO_DATA, not zero; activity counts and method scores are not execution or causal improvement. |

Valid alternatives were recorded at preparation: one logical owner may use several storage components; cross-owner draft preparation may intentionally be partial; read-only computation need not persist merely to be legitimate; a specifically authorized migration exception may be valid; non-web execution adapters are optional unless claimed; lower-risk combinations may be sampled without omitting high-risk entrances.

## 4. Independent disposition of every Grey Major/Critical candidate

### GT-01 — Exact case approval does not survive arbitrary definition changes

**QA disposition: CORROBORATED, Major source-to-contract integration gap.** Categories: authorization-state, concurrency, integration/wiring and semantic completeness. Journeys 11–15, 33 and 47. Runtime reproduction: NOT RUN.

Precondition: same-project case content A is approved through the existing exact endpoint. Event sequence: an otherwise authorized generic update changes a semantic field to B while omitting status; later the case enters a run. Expected truth: the relied-on case definition is exactly the human-reviewed definition, or B is withheld pending review. In L updateTestCaseWithClient, status uses COALESCE($20,status), expectedRevision is optional and semantic fields may change without invalidating approval. T's update handler exposes that optional legacy behavior. L create also accepts body.status, defaulting only when absent. R/run insertion selects current nondeleted same-project rows by status Approved. The inspected predicates therefore allow B to retain Approved and become a run snapshot without establishing review of B.

**Counterevidence and narrowing:** approveTestCaseForUser requires expectedRevision, uses a transaction and compare-and-set, and inserts a unique approval receipt. R locks selected current rows, atomically rejects known unapproved new cases, preserves existing run membership as a historical no-op and distinguishes skipped missing/foreign IDs. These safeguards are real. A stale case-edit predicate may reject a competing edit when supplied; it does not itself supply approval of the new content. A finding against every selection or all history would overstate the evidence.

V151 records the **pre-approval edit hash**; status changes during approval. Consequently, a repair/test must not naively require that hash to equal the post-approval row's hash if status participates. Define the reviewed semantic content and its exact protected boundary, including governed custom fields/tags or an explicit exclusion policy. Optional ordinary draft edits can remain usable; exact review may be enforced by invalidation, immutable revision selection or another equivalent native invariant.

**Falsifier/evidence required:** approve A, edit B through each real semantic writer, attempt new run selection, inspect case revision, decision receipt and cycle snapshot. Either B is refused/unapproved until separately reviewed, or the selected immutable version is A with truthful provenance. Positive controls: approved unchanged A selects; known Draft rejects; existing historical member remains unchanged; stale compare-and-set returns conflict. Exercise create/import/clone with Approved input and missing expectedRevision, and race edit against approval/selection. Owner: case/review/run native mutation owner, with Security for principal proof and QA for DB/API/channel tests.

### GT-02 — Side Bug association mutates independent execution truth

**QA disposition: CORROBORATED, Major source-to-contract integration gap.** Categories: semantic completeness, causal side effects, retry/idempotency and integration/wiring. Journeys 17–18, 28, 42 and 47. Runtime reproduction: NOT RUN.

Precondition: eligible nonfinalized execution E is Passed by U1 at t1 for its stated condition; U2 associates a side defect. Expected truth: the association is recorded while E's condition verdict, original performer and time remain unchanged. L createBug and addBugLink validate project/access and commit the Bug/link transaction, then invoke failLinkedExecutions. That helper sets any eligible linked non-Failed execution to Failed, executed_at=now() and executed_by=the linking actor. The predicate has no direct/side classification or assertion-failure condition. The shared MCP create_bug reaches this writer. Predicted consequence: association changes Passed/U1/t1 to Failed/U2/now, laundering the linker into execution provenance.

**Counterevidence and narrowing:** same-project validation/locks exist; finalized automated runs are explicitly protected; already Failed executions do not undergo this update. The inspected updateBug link replacement path does not call the same helper, so this is not a claim that every Bug modification has the same result. Those alternate paths require comparison. A mandatory assertion actually failing legitimately requires a result transition, but the link alone cannot provide that independent observation. Unlinking a Bug is not a retest and must not heal an old failure.

Because the Bug/link can commit before the helper or later delivery fails, client-visible failure can coexist with a durable created Bug. Recovery must read the logical operation/Bug receipt before retrying; an error response is not proof of rollback.

**Falsifier/evidence required:** repeat side linkage using create, add-link and update paths, then read authoritative execution/attempt, Bug/link, run projections and audit rows. E must remain Passed/U1/t1; a separate actual failed-assertion fixture must still record its own failure correctly. Include sealed automated and already-Failed controls, unlink/relink, concurrent linking, dropped acknowledgement and injected post-commit helper failure. Owner: Bugs/executions native writers and their publication adapter; DB integration plus API/MCP/channel evidence required.

### GT-03 — Governed acceptance cannot rely on coalesced autosave history

**QA disposition: CORROBORATED CONDITIONALLY, Major integration/history gap if reused as the governed acceptance boundary.** Categories: lifecycle, concurrency, history and semantic completeness. Journeys 21–22, 31–33 and 47. Runtime reproduction: NOT RUN. No shipped ASER immutable-version adapter was inspected or alleged defective.

Precondition: document A is edited to B, B is submitted/accepted for a governed purpose, and C is edited within the snapshot window. Expected truth: exact B and its governed semantic metadata remain independently addressable with its submission/decision/assignment provenance. L updateKnowledgeDocument takes document locks and passes to writeKnowledgeDocument without a caller exact-version predicate. The writer snapshots the old body when its snapshot window permits; inside the window it changes existing version diff metadata and writes the new current body. A→B→C can leave snapshot A and current C without immutable B. If the governed submission points only to that mutable current/history state, the exact acceptance cannot be reconstructed reliably.

**Counterevidence and narrowing:** advisory/row locks, JSON-value comparison, protected Zyra memory restrictions and ai_memory content-change approval reset exist. Ordinary draft coalescing is useful. Candidate discoverability to an authorized reviewer is not activation or an ACL leak. AR/AS already require immutable governed versions; the gap is explicit native-owner integration, not absence of the normative requirement. The browser write queue prevents particular same-mount reorder/retry cases, not concurrent server revision loss across tabs/clients.

**Falsifier/evidence required:** submit B, edit C concurrently within the window, retrieve/review/export B by stable identity/hash and bind the effective consumer to the authorized exact B. B and metadata must remain immutable, C distinct, stale actions explicit. Repeat semantic metadata-only changes such as authority/audience/effective date and retained/removed source references. Preserve ordinary autosave usability and independently test raw evidence that does not need an authoring skill. Owner: native KB/review/skills version boundary; DB/API and UI recovery proof required.

### Additional authority branch retained for implementation verification

A's API-token guard blocks credential/member/project administration but permits scoped same-project write routes generally. The inspected case approval controller passes req.userId to approveTestCaseForUser, whose examined contract consumes user identity and expectedRevision, not machine-versus-human principal class. The middleware retains apiToken separately, so the distinction is available. This is a **source-grounded all-entrance test obligation**, not a claim of a live exploit or a new demonstrated skill-approval endpoint. Test a write token owned by an administrator as a machine principal, a native delegated model invocation and a genuine human review separately. Broad issuer role or exact revision knowledge cannot substitute for the human decision required by O2. Security/native review owners must determine and prove the intended boundary; do not close it by testing only the new MCP facade.

## 6. Bounded executable handoff plans

These are plans, not fabricated test scripts or run results. Runtime evidence for every plan is NOT RUN. An authorized environment, real build identity and native-owner implementation are prerequisites. DB tests must obey repository DATABASE_URL policy; no local fallback or live mutation was attempted here.

### QP-1 Original ChatGPT → Tesbo → executor → report → retest → human apply

**Scenario:** complete the original case-free loop and its denied/blocked siblings. **Actors/principals:** human QA reviewer; external ChatGPT analyst; assigned OpenCode executor using its own Chrome MCP; second supported runtime/driver for interchangeability; read-only/revoked principal. **Initial state:** authorized disposable project, zero cases/runs/models, no Tesbo AI key, MCP explicitly enabled, exact prompt/context T1.

**Command/event sequence:** create/dispatch T1; claim A1/F1; execute target; reserve R1/upload/finalize; retrieve originals in analyst; create narrow linked T2/A2/R2; produce proposal V1; human accepts case operation, rejects result and defers Bug; separately apply selected dependency-closed operations. **Expected state after each step:** exact T1 first; one current attempt next; observed scope then immutable complete R1; distinct R2 linked to R1; independent decisions; only selected publication at last step. **Expected side effects:** attributed events/receipts/audit, evidence availability, explicit remaining work. **Forbidden side effects:** early case/run/Bug/result creation; invented target execution; implicit human approval; report overwrite.

**Retry behavior:** recover reserve/finalize/apply receipts after dropped ACK; no duplicate logical effect. **Concurrency behavior:** competing claims, prompt revision and revoked old fence cannot advance replacement task. **Legacy-data fixture:** manual primary/supplemental report with unknown historical definition and truthful submitter. **Authorization fixture:** OFF/read-only/foreign project/revoked token; missing driver yields blocked report. **Recovery:** discover task/report/pending review without prior chat; retry narrow scope, not whole official publication. **Test layer:** real MCP client + target driver + DB/storage/API + native UI. **Runtime evidence:** NOT RUN; retain build/client/driver identities, ordered IDs/fences, exact report hashes/bytes, before/after native records and human decision/apply receipts.

### QP-2 Exact publication and approval preservation

**Scenario:** GT-01 plus mixed review/apply. **Actors/principals:** human reviewer, authorized editor, machine token belonging to the reviewer, denied project member. **Initial state:** Draft A, known revision/content digest, empty run, no publication. **Command/event sequence:** approve A; edit semantic B; select; approve B separately; select; retry selected apply. **Expected state after each step:** exact receipt for A; B cannot inherit protected reliance; invalid selection no insertion; exact B decision then valid B snapshot; retry same mapping. **Expected side effects:** required audit and approved subset in one protected transaction. **Forbidden side effects:** status laundering, rejected/deferred operations, hidden Bug effects or auditless publication.

**Retry behavior:** same operation/request/payload returns original receipt; new key does not republish; altered payload conflicts. **Concurrency behavior:** edit/approve/select interleaving, revocation and conflicting base force truthful refusal/review. **Legacy-data fixture:** Approved row without exact receipt stays explicitly unresolved or uses approved migration policy; existing run membership stays historical. **Authorization fixture:** machine principal cannot supply human assent; foreign child references denied. **Recovery:** retain candidate and compare/rebase, no lost draft. **Test layer:** DB integration, all writer APIs/MCP and native editor. **Runtime evidence:** NOT RUN; authoritative case/hash/status/receipt, run snapshot, item mappings and transaction/audit evidence required.

### QP-3 Bug association and failed downstream effects

**Scenario:** GT-02 control pair and uncertain creation. **Actors/principals:** executor U1, Bug author U2, denied linker. **Initial state:** Passed E/U1/t1, separate genuinely failed assertion F, sealed automated execution Z. **Command/event sequence:** create SIDE Bug linked to E; add/update/unlink/relink; link F and Z; inject error after Bug transaction; retry. **Expected state after each step:** E unchanged with correct link; F remains independently failed; Z preserved; durable Bug discoverable after uncertain response. **Expected side effects:** relationship and attributable audit only unless separate actual result transition authorized. **Forbidden side effects:** linker replacing performer/time; link healing/failing E; duplicate Bug after timeout.

**Retry behavior:** recover committed identity and compare canonical result before resend. **Concurrency behavior:** simultaneous links and run finalization cannot overwrite sealed history. **Legacy-data fixture:** unknown old definition not reconstructed as observed pass/failure. **Authorization fixture:** foreign execution/child and revoked user denied. **Recovery:** explicit failed delivery/helper receipt without erasing committed Bug. **Test layer:** DB/API/MCP and run projections/UI. **Runtime evidence:** NOT RUN; exact execution/attempt/Bug/link/audit before/after and injected-failure trace required.

### QP-4 Immutable governed documents and skill reliance

**Scenario:** GT-03 and S1→S2 drift. **Actors/principals:** author, independent governance approver/assigner, second editor, machine candidate author. **Initial state:** draft A under assigned S1. **Command/event sequence:** edit B; submit B; edit C within snapshot window; approve B; separately assign approved skill version; change to S2; attempt old candidate apply. **Expected state after each step:** B immutable at submission; C separate; B decision points to exact content/metadata; approval alone leaves assignment unchanged; stale candidate retained and not silently governed by S2. **Expected side effects:** explicit version/decision/assignment/provenance history. **Forbidden side effects:** accepting current C as B, draft search becoming active authority, source/policy rewritten to pass, historical S1 relabelled S2.

**Retry behavior:** same submission/decision/assignment returns stable receipt, altered intent conflicts. **Concurrency behavior:** second tab and authority-only metadata edits use explicit version checks. **Legacy-data fixture:** unknown old skill provenance, ordinary ungoverned draft, raw evidence permission after revocation. **Authorization fixture:** administrator-owned machine cannot approve/assign; root authority missing/revoked uses independently defined recovery, not candidate self-approval. **Recovery:** retrieve exact B, compare/rebase C, restore permitted draft; no mandatory mass rewrite. **Test layer:** DB/API/MCP and native KB/review UI. **Runtime evidence:** NOT RUN; immutable hashes/versions, exact decisions, effective resolver result and denied-action no-effect proof required.

### QP-5 Cross-domain delivery continuation and partial recovery

**Scenario:** real unplanned change with mixed/no artifact acceptance and later failed deployment. **Actors/principals:** coding delegate, BA, QA, policy owner, PM, release/operations owner without the coding chat. **Initial state:** exact source/binding A, current requirement/manual/policy/test owners, approved effective skills, no fake work for cosmetic scope. **Command/event sequence:** capture change; record seven justified dispositions; prepare R and M; commit R while M fails/ACK lost; reject remaining proposals; emit impact with bell failure; QA claims/verifies; human records exact readiness; merge; deploy; smoke fails; incident→rollback/fix-forward→new retest. **Expected state after each step:** stable source/change/config identity; per-domain disposition; R receipt/M pending; real source impact stays open despite rejection; pending work discoverable; readiness exact and independent; merge not deployment; failed deploy remains historical with new recovery attempt. **Expected side effects:** native owner links, durable impact/outbox, attributed decisions, build/deploy/incident/retest chain. **Forbidden side effects:** partial draft journal mistaken for atomic protected apply, bell/read receipt closing impact, CI counted as target proof, manual approval closing QA work, incident rewrite, source omission labelled unchanged.

**Retry behavior:** duplicate/reordered hook, provider response, capture and item submission reconcile by logical identity; possibly delivered message remains uncertain until checked. **Concurrency behavior:** A→B binding change plus delayed A, skill change and role revocation never migrate old work to new context. **Legacy-data fixture:** one-source pilot, missing provider/no hook/no model, reconstructed old change and unknown metric denominator. **Authorization fixture:** role-specific views share IDs but withhold restricted source/history; recipient authorization current at send. **Recovery:** named native owner/next action; provider/capture gaps repaired, read/return usable without chat; rollback is new history. **Test layer:** DB/API/MCP, real provider/storage/deploy adapters and authentic role UI. **Runtime evidence:** NOT RUN; end-to-end event/operation IDs, source/build/attempt IDs, per-owner before/after, receipt/outbox state and target smoke/retest evidence required.

## 7. Coverage limits, preserved alternatives and handoff

The ASER proposal already provides meaningful protections: justified applicability, immutable skill versions, candidate/approval/assignment separation, stale-governance checks, partial-write journals, independent source expectations, raw-evidence permission, source/CI/deployment distinctions and post-deployment continuation. These should be preserved while converging with the canonical owners. AR/AD's partial cross-domain preparation must remain distinct from the canonical atomic exact selected publication of protected operations. One logical owner does not require one physical table, and naming a new entity does not establish its authority.

The original loop remains a named implementation gate. The inspected 58-tool registration path does not establish task/prompt/assignment/claim/report/analysis/retest producer-consumer integration. This is bounded source capability evidence, not a whole-repository absence claim or a newly missing requirement: C2/C4/C5/C6 already specify the loop. A coding-only change and cycle-linked verification bundle cannot substitute. Real external execution must use the assigned executor's available driver; unavailable execution is a truthful blocked outcome.

Source controls retained: explicit Bearer takes precedence over cookie and invalid Bearer does not fall back; MCP rechecks project membership/scopes and write plan policy; project control-plane REST is denied to tokens; exact approval CAS/receipts and known-unapproved selection rejection exist; sealed automated runs are protected; KB has locks and memory-specific restrictions; source binding uses approved server checkout configuration/current membership and pinned-ref revalidation; preview and parent context use generation/abort/key controls. None closes every entrance or downstream consumer.

| Evidence layer | QA status and practical limit |
|---|---|
| Independent oracles and conceptual challenges | Recorded before Grey; specified G0 traces assessed. No statistically representative user research or benchmark. |
| Source review | Selected exact methods/controls inspected; all three Major candidates challenged. Full writer/read/export/deletion/cache/background census remains OPEN. |
| Unit/automation presence | Some cached test files exist; no test suite or complete test census assessed. Existence is not execution. |
| DB/API/MCP/client/concurrency/storage/provider/deployment tests | NOT RUN. No running target identity or authorized disposable fixture established here. |
| Authentic current UI source | Selected source consumers reviewed; not all affected routes/shared consumers. |
| BEFORE RENDERED / AFTER RENDERED | NOT RUN / NOT RUN. No synthetic image offered as baseline. |
| VISUAL DIFFERENCE REVIEWED / INTERACTION TESTED | NOT RUN / NOT RUN. Keyboard/focus/AT/touch/responsive/overflow/conflict/loading/empty/permission states BLOCKED pending runtime. |
| Deployment verified | NOT RUN; no build or evidence deployment claim. |

Initial evidence therefore supports source/design corrections and specific acceptance gates, not architecture freeze or release certification. Before stronger assurance, White's disposition must identify canonical ownership and explicit outstanding policy decisions: native requirement/criterion identity; change/impact/packet continuation and all-rejected work; general work versus generation/Bug lifecycles; Standards/Playbooks T422/default/effective pack versus proposed skills; exact document/case/source/release snapshot boundaries; root recovery authority; impact closure policy; and any bounded migration exceptions. QA will not invent those semantics to make tests pass.

After White's pre-gate and corrected snapshot are handed off, QA should issue a **separate delta receipt**, keeping this receipt unchanged. The delta must show disposition of GT-01/02/03, map every affected writer/consumer and all 49 journey obligations to changed owners, preserve valid conforming branches, and name remaining runtime/UI/provider evidence gates. Any later executed evidence must record the actual build, fixtures, principals, channels, authoritative before/after values and recovery behavior. Until then: **49 obligations accounted; three Major candidates independently challenged; runtime coverage UNKNOWN; executed product tests zero; formal post-design disposition pending.**

## 2. Reading and evidence boundary

White's pre-gate receipt was read, including W-PRE-01–04 and W-C1–6. Its initial block remains part of the history; this reviewer does not replace White's later decision. The unchanged Red charter and orchestration from the initial review remain governing. No charter changes or audit of the method were performed.

Red compared all eight canonical targets with their originals and all six views with the original six files. The canonical diff consists of revision/framing/header updates and appended 1.4.28 amendments; the historical bodies were not rewritten by these diffs. The review read the changed requirement, design, skill, agent-rule and roadmap clauses, the owner allocation, the complete ASER-C01–06 and ASER-UX01–03 contracts, the 48-row crosswalk, the 16 new ASE-INT scenarios and the incorporated journey obligations. Previously read unchanged proposal material was carried forward, not falsely reported as a new full reading.

To avoid treating repeated text as separate independent evidence, Red compared nonempty canonical amendment lines with the six views after removing heading-depth markers. Matching counts were 18/50 for 00, 145/147 for 01, 41/42 for 02, 365/372 for 03, 56/57 for 04, 320/323 for 05, 70/72 for 06 and 41/43 for 07. The unmatched lines were inspected; they are canonical framing, file allocation, redirected references, acceptance placement or the final UI allocation. This check corroborates incorporation and exposes duplicate text for maintenance; it does not prove the text correct. Canonical 04 remains the declared field/lifecycle contract owner and the six views remain subordinate.

**Historical sampling remains bounded.** The approximately 147,535-word original canonical package was not reread line by line. The original Red preparation records the sampled canonical/source census. This delta also rechecked original P-07, FR-027–037/040 and relevant workflow wording when testing the corrected publication contract. It does not certify all historical MBT, UI, permission or QA sections. The existing fieldwork dates and unverified production identity retain the limitations stated in the original receipt.

Source comparisons rely on Red's previously inspected pinned implementation and the primary blocks independently checked during the initial review: case writers, exact-approval controller/receipt, bearer guard, run admission, KB writer/history, RAG readers and source-binding owner. The correction changes documentation, not that source. New source facts supplied through White about additional Bug/provider paths are treated as routed prerequisites, not a new exhaustive Red source investigation or executed reproduction.

No application test, API/DB request, live MCP discovery, browser operation, screenshot, deployment observation or product mutation was performed. External reference pages were not revalidated in this bounded lifecycle/ownership review; no new claim about their current capabilities depends on that material. Neither the planned scenario count nor the imported Grey inventory is independent runtime evidence.

## 3. Disposition of the original findings

| Original finding | Exact corrective contract | Specification disposition | What remains open |
