No new writable packet approval/completion owner. | Complete current producer/consumer/table fit and migration evidence, particularly general tasks and global/workspace/project provisioning. This is not approval of a physical schema or every inherited owner reference. |
| **ASER-R02 — protected case publication** | R-ASE-06/13/23/34; C03/C04; design §18.2; agent rule steps 6/7; ASE-INT-03/04/11 | **Addressed at proposed contract level.** Unpublished logical operations precede native IDs; exact human semantic review, current principal class, selected dependency-closed native transaction and run eligibility are distinct. Existing approved definitions and historical attempts are preserved. | Actual every-writer/read-consumer inventory, human action-bound proof policy, dependency manifest coverage, current permission mapping, native transaction participation and denied/authorized-success execution. Existing source paths remain unchanged. |
| **ASER-R03 — immutable governed KB revision and reader authority** | R-ASE-21/25/30/31; C03.2/C06; Skills §14; design §18.2; ASE-INT-02/06/09 | **Addressed at proposed contract level.** Explicit immutable submitted/accepted snapshot and CAS are separate from autosave; digest includes semantic metadata/package/dependencies; authorized candidate reading is separate from effective instructions. | Actual snapshot schema/writer/CAS/migration and complete retrieval/cache/export consumer implementation, history/access policy, revocation behavior and tests. Current coalesced history and generic retrieval cannot be called repaired. |
| **ASER-R04 — source and independently accepted scope** | R-ASE-37/42/43; C02/C01; design §§4/15/18.2; ASE-INT-07/08 | **Addressed at proposed contract level.** Stable binding/configuration revision extends the existing source owner; current row can be a pointer; many-to-many changeset/release participation and independent policy/skill/case decisions are explicit. | Concrete migration, provider authenticity and delayed-event handling, all affected consumers, exact release authority and build evidence. Single-source pilots must advertise their boundary and cannot certify a multi-source release. |

The original findings remain valid descriptions of the original input. Their unresolved implementation parts are carried forward rather than silently relabeled Closed. A later implementer must produce the prescribed effects and readback; this receipt establishes that the correction now tells that implementer what the relevant boundary is.

### R01 hostile resolution: rejected-only and mixed domains

C01 separates domain applicability from work disposition and individual operation decisions. C05 derives coordination from canonical events/operations/impacts and defines precedence. Rejecting all proposals can settle the **proposal** without publishing anything; an outstanding required QA impact still blocks the corresponding aggregate/readiness claim. A validly deferred item carries owner, scope and review trigger, and cannot satisfy an unmet mandatory release requirement. ASE-INT-16 states the discriminating sibling. This addresses the original ambiguity without making one `done` state authoritative over all work.

For the opposite skill scope, platform packs, workspace pins and project overlays use one T-422 owner without a fake project or copied core. Approval alone does not activate a version; ASE-INT-01 keeps G1 effective after G2 approval until the separately authorized assignment. The correction no longer presumes a new top-level Skills destination. Exact physical task/guide implementation remains a gate rather than an asserted fact.

### R02 hostile resolution: approved label, machine owner and shared apply

The correction explicitly refuses generic status-based inherited approval, includes dependent custom/tag/link/reference-image meaning, retains machine credential type even when its owner is an administrator, and requires server-owned action-bound review evidence. That directly addresses the source counterexamples in the initial receipt rather than assuming a new facade protects legacy paths.

Partial **staging** and external handoffs do not weaken atomic native protected **application**. C03 requires one real native transaction/client, exact selected dependency closure and mandatory audit/commitments; unsupported participation must fail before official mutation. ASE-INT-04 includes an already committed dependency and audit failure. The permitted combined human UI action in R-ASE-06/Skills §12.2 must retain the unchanged explicit authorization for selected apply; approval alone is never that authorization. C03 and the inherited FR-032/035/040 support this interpretation. No automatic-publication amendment is adopted.

Current run admission and case writers remain exactly the implementation concern reported initially. A declared semantic manifest is not proof its fields, hashes, hooks and run consumers have been implemented correctly. It must not be reduced to the pre-approval whole-row hash whose status/timestamp changes at approval.

### R03 hostile resolution: an accepted intermediate body and a readable candidate

ASE-INT-02 now reproduces the original distinguishing sequence: mutable A, submit B, edit C within the snapshot window, concurrent stale submission/assignment change, then exact B readback and permitted draft restore. It requires B's immutable semantic content and dependencies to survive while ordinary autosave remains usable. C03 supplies the explicit storage/transaction invariant missing from reliance on the current history.

C06 and Skills §14 inventory the authority-sensitive and visibility-sensitive consumers without banning legitimate authorized candidate review. The effective manifest remains derived from accepted active assignments; an ordinary non-AI-memory type, accessible ID or self-declared approval cannot activate instructions. ASE-INT-06 includes revocation and caches. These close the specification gap, while the current RAG predicates remain evidence for the required migration work.

### R04 hostile resolution: delayed events and two releases

C02 supplies stable source binding/configuration identity, provenance-limited legacy migration and treatment of delayed observations after rebind. A delayed A/config1 event cannot advance active B/config2. The same changeset can participate in separately decided releases, and source-free document/policy work does not require a fabricated commit. Policy effectivity and skill assignment remain their own decisions.

C01/R-ASE-42 additionally separates pre-deployment candidate evidence from post-deployment acceptance, avoiding both fabricated production proof and an impossible requirement for future production evidence before initial deployment approval. ASE-INT-08 preserves the original decision when a different/unknown build deploys or later policy/smoke evidence changes. Provider attestation, actual builds and decision authority are still unproved.

## 4. Compression and negative-design recheck

The correction does not need a second broad negative-design exercise to bless an ASER subsystem: it removes that proposed independent owner. The original five-alternative analysis continues to apply. The surviving narrow facts were challenged again:

| Surviving fact | REUSE / EXTEND / EXTRACT / COMPOSE / PROJECTION-ONLY result |
| --- | --- |
| Immutable submitted governed body | REUSE ordinary KB history unchanged fails the A→B→C reliance counterexample. EXTEND its rightful asset/version owner is viable. EXTRACT snapshot/hash validation only as shared implementation code. COMPOSE exact content/package/dependency references. PROJECTION-ONLY cannot reconstruct B if it was never retained. No independent skill byte/history store is justified. |
| Human assignment/action-bound decision | REUSE an applicable existing review/binding owner where it has the right semantics. EXTEND narrow immutable decision/scope metadata where absent. EXTRACT shared enforcement rather than a new governance service. COMPOSE version, assignment and current permission. PROJECTION-ONLY cannot invent the original human assent, though the effective manifest is derived. |
| Historic source configuration | REUSE one mutable current project row unchanged fails delayed-event history. EXTEND the same source owner with immutable configuration identity. EXTRACT common provider validation if needed. COMPOSE exact source and capture references. PROJECTION-ONLY cannot reconstruct overwritten configuration reliably. The current row can remain a convenience pointer. |
| Release participation and decision | REUSE shared human review/audit and provider deployment owners. EXTEND typed decision/evidence scope where needed. EXTRACT read calculation only if useful. COMPOSE multiple changes/builds/attempts and independently scoped decisions. PROJECTION-ONLY is appropriate for readiness, not for manufacturing approval or deployment facts. |

The surviving design preserves the consequential distinctions and deletes redundant mutable answers. No new physical table, platform capability class, universal UI or standalone registry is approved by this reasoning. The table-fit and enforcement proof must still be furnished before implementation exposure.

## 5. Opposite siblings and recovery trace

All results here are **conceptual contract checks**, not passing application tests.

| Challenge | v1 contract result | Residual falsifier |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Same idempotency key after partial staging | C04 returns the same logical receipt with current journal revision/history; lookup performs no extra work. | Commit then lose response; duplicate must create no second staging or official effect. |
| Remaining skill/base changes before resume | Explicit resume supplies original manifest, expected journal revision and new attempt identity; changed intent/context needs the necessary new candidate revision. | Stale resume must fail while exact prior target commitments remain discoverable. |
| Uncertain item versus known failed item | Authoritative readback/reconciliation precedes action; unresolved uncertainty stays blocked. Only eligible work can resume. | Provider/native timeout that actually committed must not be treated as a retryable failure. |
| Cancellation while some effects committed | Canonical terminal event and lineage preserve actual commitments and residual obligations; cancellation is not rollback by assertion. | Race cancellation against native commit/outbox delivery, preserving exact actor/history. |
| Revoked skill plus raw failed report | C03.6/R-ASE-25/30 and ASE-INT-09 retain independently authorized observation; stale authored promotion is refused. | Report intake does not certify payload truth or gain official result authority. |
| External MCP OFF, independent provider granted | C03 requires P-18 before external discovery/calls; separately authorized provider intake is distinct. | Both MCP aliases/cached sessions deny; native authorized and provider paths retain their own rights. |
| Source-only change without requirement/model/session | C02 and UX01 preserve observation/proposal distinction; no invented accepted oracle or mandatory model. | A policy contradiction must not be “fixed” by generating a matching requirement and self-approving it. |
| Case-free external QA round trip | UX01, I3-Q, ASE-INT-12 retain own-driver execution, original bytes, separate retest and exact human apply. | Execute the original loop without a native case/model key; a coding-source demo is insufficient. |
| Required unsupported manual/policy capability | I8 is breadth sequencing, not waiver. C01/C05 and ASE-INT-16 keep the affected readiness blocked or explicitly excepted by real authority. | Cosmetic N/A must remain possible without fake records; mandatory work cannot become N/A through failed lookup. |
| Restricted evidence and project switch | C06/UX02/03 carry permission-safe uncertainty, current authorization and response fencing; hidden counts are not disclosure. | A delayed old-project response and revoked cache must not surface payload or renew authority. |
| Skill primary versus supporting versus historical | One version owner, current assignment relation and historical usage retain distinct scope. | Revocation cannot rewrite old authored meaning or relabel an execution as using the new skill. |
| Current approval versus old run snapshot | C03 retains historical definitions/attempts and requires current semantic eligibility only for new reliance. | Generic edit/clone/status mutation must not inherit approval, while historical run remains readable as history. |

The C05 precedence and terminal rules are now sufficiently discriminating to evaluate a model implementation; they are not an implementation of that reducer. The paired denied and legitimate-success cases in the new scenario inventory are useful proof obligations, not a substitute for execution or complete branch enumeration.

## 6. New finding

### ASER-R05 — One integration reference uses an undefined scenario prefix

**Severity:** Minor. **Category:** Invariant (traceability). **Status:** Open in frozen v1.

**Evidence:** `review-snapshots/v1/views/implementation-plan.md`, final paragraph at line 317, refers to **`ASER-INT-02/03`**. The defined scenario namespace is **`ASE-INT-02` / `ASE-INT-03`**. The same sentence is incorporated in canonical 05. The surrounding crosswalk and actual scenario definitions use the correct prefix.

**Impact:** a literal reference lookup or reviewer following this summary can fail to resolve the intended immutable-snapshot and protected-writer scenarios. The document verifier's successful missing-heading negative control does not establish general reference integrity. This typo does not invalidate the substantive scenario definitions or create a new runtime defect.

**Recommendation:** correct that sentence in the existing view and canonical 05 in a later author snapshot, preserving the definitions/IDs. Do not edit the frozen v1 input or this receipt.

**Falsifier / closure evidence:** both corrected target sentences reference the actual ASE-INT-02/03 definitions; the new hashes and narrow delta show no semantic change to those scenarios. No runtime execution is required for this editorial correction.

No additional Major finding was established. Absence of a new finding is limited to the examined delta and inherited source sample; it is not exhaustive completeness certification.

## 7. Residual gates and formal handoff

1. **Existing implementation/migration:** current case/status/principal paths, exact governed KB snapshot/CAS and RAG/reader authority, source-binding history and complete multi-source consumers still need actual implementation and tested migration. General-task fit, bytes/report paths and all entrance coverage remain bounded evidence gaps. Preserve UNKNOWN for missing historic approval, skill use or source configuration.
2. **Human policy/authority:** native requirement and criteria acceptance, policy/release authority, root bootstrap/recovery/quorum, no-active-skill exceptions and relevant retention/operating limits need their accountable human decisions. The correction uses deny-by-default for affected actions and does not appoint reviewers to these roles.
3. **Actual enforcement and evidence:** require the exact build/schema/tool identity, all relevant direct/legacy/background paths, scoped authorized-success and denied-effect tests, transaction/outbox/readback proof, and original QA-loop execution. No scenario has become executed by incorporation in canonical 05.
4. **Authentic UI:** D-14 and existing Zyra/native-route placement is corrected, but source layout is not current rendered evidence. Actual Before/After, role, fixture, viewport/theme, focus/keyboard/AT and responsive tests remain **VISUAL-BASELINE BLOCKED / NOT RUN**.
5. **Formal assurance:** second Red membership disposition is pending at seal time. The upstream formal gates, other required specialist/delta reviews and Graph then White closeout remain independent. Root's author corrections and document-verifier result cannot supply those votes.

**Handoff:** this Red member supports the corrected proposed lifecycle/ownership allocation for continued independent assessment, with R01–R04's specification components addressed, their named implementation/authority gates retained, and R05 requiring a narrow reference correction. The initial pre-gate history is preserved. This receipt grants no final freeze or operational approval, and any later correction is a new reviewed snapshot.

## 4. Typed-edge and scope challenge

The new C02 table distinguishes criterion-to-assertion designed coverage, policy-to-control enforcement, manual descriptions, exact authoring-method resolution, source observation provenance, fixed human decision context, occurrence/build/retest relationships and evidence derivation. It states direction, cardinality, native authority and historical grain. Accepted edge history is separate from current endpoint validity; a changed endpoint cannot silently accept a successor.

The following opposite cases preserve the original invariants:

- **Planned coverage versus actual result:** a criterion-to-case link does not prove execution, a Passed label does not supply exact-build assertion evidence, and a Bug/task link cannot prove retest. Native occurrence/attempt facts remain required.
- **Shared platform guidance versus project-owned records:** the narrow exception requires an authorized T-422 assignment/binding. It grants neither arbitrary cross-project edges nor private candidate/history access. Endpoint existence, edge metadata and payload have separate authorization.
- **Readable derivative versus confidential source:** permission to read redacted evidence does not disclose its original. Digests/custody preserve lineage without converting a fixture or expected reference into observed evidence.
- **Raw source event without agent session:** source/binding provenance can exist without fabricated skill use, authored expectation or human acceptance of behavior. The edge's attribution/history does not replace the existing observation authority in C03.
- **Revoked guidance and historical reliance:** current effective reliance stops as applicable policy requires; permitted historic references remain without copying restricted content or relabeling old authored meaning.
- **One commit in two releases:** fixed evidence/build/risk contexts belong to independent decisions. A relied-on relationship is not deployment authorization and does not inherit another release's outcome.

These are conceptual contract checks, not executed acceptance. Mapping the proposed meanings to the installed registry schema, wire enums, native validators and migrations is explicitly required before exposure. No additional finding emerged within this scope.

The second member additionally inspected the stored comparison's paths and full `api.ts` wrapper patch, with the same limit.

The future all-entrance run-admission inventory must include the added **`POST /api/cycles/schedules/:scheduleId/run`** path through `SchedulesService.runNowForUser` and `LegacyService.createCycleFromScheduledRun`, alongside due schedules. Its complete authorization, concurrency and eligibility behavior remains outside this bounded document delta. The new author market-research record is not counted as another independent review.

Open gates remain:

1. Complete actual writer/reader, dependency, transaction/outbox, migration and legacy-client enforcement, including newly added schedule entrances. No source defect is declared fixed by this amendment.
2. Accountable human requirement/policy/release and root-recovery authority, approved exceptions, retention and operating/resource limits. Review quorum supplies no operational authority.
3. Exact deployed build/schema/catalog, denied and authorized-success tests, provider/client compatibility, original external QA-loop execution and real report/evidence-byte verification.
4. Authentic current UI, Before/After, keyboard/AT/responsive and role evidence; **VISUAL-BASELINE BLOCKED** remains.
5. Other required specialist dispositions and Graph then White closeout. This Red receipt does not rewrite White's historical pre-gate or issue its final decision.

**Seal:** the two actual Red members concur on the bounded proposed-contract remediation at frozen v3; R05 is corrected and R01–R04's source/runtime/authority obligations remain explicit. Original and v1 receipts stay immutable. Any later target change requires its own delta disposition.

## 2. Independent problem reconstruction and premise result

The surviving problem is continuity of trustworthy delivery work. A human or agent changes source, possibly before a formal requirement exists. Later QA, BA, policy and release reviewers need to identify the source scope, accepted expectations, proposed changes to those expectations, actual execution/evidence, independent decisions, outstanding follow-up and the build to which each assertion applies. The workflow must also work without a coding-agent hook, with provider-owned requirements, with a solo human performing several roles, and without a behavioral model. These are the underlying jobs; ASER, Change Workspace and Skills Library are proposed names for parts of the experience, not evidence of separate domains. [ASER requirements R-ASE-01–20, 37–48; canonical FR-115–124; historical B2/B3.]

Deleting ASER and all agent-brand names leaves native requirements/cases/documents, immutable proposed revisions and acceptance decisions, observed source changes, scoped effective guidance, attributable evidence and durable follow-up. Existing canonical owners cover most of this structure. A separate ASER database, generic approval engine, authoritative readiness status or copied artifact store does not survive the counterfactual removal test. [Canonical 03 v1.4.19–20; ASER design §§2–4, 10, 12–15.]

Deleting *all* new metadata also fails. Current source does not prove stable immutable submitted KB snapshots, multi-source binding history or every-writer enforcement. Some narrow extensions are necessary if those requirements are retained. The correct compression boundary is to add the missing fact to its rightful owner, then compose the experience. “No new top-level subsystem” does not mean “no new columns or relation can ever be justified.”

**Gate B premise outcome:** retain the connected workflow intent and the separation of observed, proposed, approved, applied and executed facts. Reject approval of a separate persistent ASER domain on this evidence. Exact canonical aliases, migration boundaries and acceptance criteria must precede schema freeze. Existing ASER I0/S0 already provide the place to resolve them; the findings specify what their exit evidence needs to contain.

## 4. Negative design proof and compression

Every row tests all five required alternatives. “Viable” means a candidate design path; no row grants a new persistence primitive. Where an existing implementation is partial, the source insufficiency is explicit rather than used to justify an entirely new service.

| Proposed persistent concept | REUSE unchanged | EXTEND | EXTRACT | COMPOSE | PROJECTION-ONLY | Result / surviving invariant |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Change Packet / packet status | Canonical `quality_change_set` identity and proposal receipts already cover core facts; existing graph captures alone do not own authored acceptance. | Add any missing applicability/correlation receipt to canonical change/impact owners. | Extract shared orchestration from existing services only if duplicate adapter code warrants it. | **Viable:** changeset, event, impact, proposal/application and work links. | **Viable for displayed packet summary**, not for a new human assessment that must be recorded. | No independent packet approval/completion truth justified. State exactly which authored fact, if any, lacks a canonical home. |
| Skills Library / skill identity and versions | T-422/FR-076–077/default-pack asset owner is the canonical allocation. Current V45 project KB alone is insufficient for global packs and exact governed versions. | **Viable:** typed asset metadata, immutable package/content versions and workspace/project binding. | Shared resolver/validator may be extracted within that owner; no second authority registry. | Existing pack, KB presentation, review and provenance services. | **Viable for library navigation/search**, not for authored version bytes. | “Skills” can be a governed guide/asset representation; separate library ownership/navigation requires explicit canonical amendment. |
| Skill assignment / active version | Reuse the specified guide/method binding where its scope and authority fit; current source proof is incomplete. | **Viable:** separately authorized applicability and activation relation, exact version pin. | Common permission/resolution code may be shared; a generic permission platform is unearned. | Compose approved version, scope binding and precedence/dependency rules. | Insufficient for a human's activation decision; effective resolved manifest **is** derived. | Separate assignment semantics survive compression, but not a duplicate content store or copied active skill body. |
| Skill review, candidate iteration, usage | Reuse common proposal operation/review and audit identity; coalesced KB history alone is insufficient. | Immutable submitted snapshot plus hash-bound receipt and consumed assignment/version provenance. | Shared review UI/receipt helpers, not new competing review lifecycle. | Proposal + version + assignment + native artifact provenance. | Valid for review queues and usage reports, not the original decision or exact consumed version. | Keep only missing durable decision/provenance facts at those owners. |
| Native requirement | External provider key cannot express an independently authored native requirement; typed KB note alone lacks accepted semantics. | **Viable:** existing Requirements/native KB owner with exact revision, authority/criteria and links. | Shared relation validation may be extracted from provider adapters. | Native expectation + source citations + case/evidence edges. | Insufficient for newly authored requirement text; coverage/readiness are projections. | No second wiki. Canonical open authority/acceptance decisions must be resolved, not inferred from imported text or changed code. |
| Decision / policy / rule | Reuse governed content and trace owners; don't flatten the three meanings into one status. | **Viable:** typed normative content, effectivity and accountable decision metadata. | Common review/permission code only where warranted. | Decision authorizes policy revision; rule implements it; tests/evidence verify behavior. | Reports are derived; an accepted normative revision is authored truth. | No generic policy database justified merely by new views. Audience, origin, confidentiality and authority remain independent. |
| General work item / task | Reuse Bugs for defects and existing task ownership where semantics fit. A generation task's `done` does not mean QA accepted. | **Viable:** native general work/assignment/blocking semantics if current task service can own them. | Extract shared board/work plumbing if necessary without collapsing lifecycles. | Link generation task, canonical impact obligation, Bug and independent retest. | Board is a projection; assignment/acceptance may require durable native facts. | No universal task-done truth. Exact general-task fit remains unresolved; don't copy impact records into tasks. |
| Agent/provider event and activity | Existing source captures/activity can be reused for their actual evidence tier. | Add stable attested event identity/provenance where absent. | Adapter normalization may be shared. | Native receipts + provider observations + attributed reports. | Timeline/summary is a projection; independently received event is a durable observation. | No fabricated complete agent history; absence of a hook is not absence of a change. |
| Source/release participation | Existing single project binding works for its bounded scope; not proven for multi-source. | **Viable:** stable source identity/configuration revision, preserving old capture scope. | Shared source resolution/attestation boundary. | Link several source changes/builds/attempts to independently decided release scope. | Release readiness is derived; historical binding and authorized release decision cannot be regenerated from current config. | No parallel ASER source registry or packet-as-release authority. |
| Consistency / readiness / metrics | Reuse exact native/provider facts; current coverage is partial. | Add missing evidence links/definition metadata to the rightful owner. | Shared read/reason calculation may be extracted. | **Viable:** assemble required scope, evidence and policy decisions. | **Preferred** for consistency reasons, readiness and metrics. | No mutable green badge, coverage count or metric becomes another source of truth. UNKNOWN and no-data survive. |

**Compression outcome:** remove duplicate artifact bodies from packets, copied active-skill content, independently writable readiness flags, universal approval/completion states, parallel review boards and a new top-level Skills navigation assumption. Keep durable authored decisions, exact immutable revisions, permission-scoped relation identity, uncertain partial receipts and historical provenance. These distinctions change what users may safely rely on and therefore cannot be compressed into one status.

## 5. Findings

### ASER-R01 — Canonical owner convergence is not yet concrete enough to freeze

**Severity:** Major. **Category:** Coupling. **Status:** Open. **Gate affected:** I0 / architecture and schema freeze. **Preparation origin:** C1/C2; independently corroborates B01.

**Finding:** ASER design §§3–4 and skills §§2/7/10 describe packet, skill, assignment, review, usage and general task concepts without a decisive allocation to canonical `quality_change_sets`/events, `quality_impact_items`, FR-027–037/T-304–305 review/apply, and T-422/FR-076–077/106–114 guide/default-pack owners. Canonical 07 v1.4.19 explicitly extends D-14 and disallows a new Skills page. ASER's no-duplicate statement and illustrative storage caveat are valuable but do not identify which lifecycle is authoritative when records disagree. [Canonical 03 lines 663–824, 07 lines 427–508; ASER design §§3–4, 10; implementation plan I0, S, D.]

**Impact:** an implementation can appear compliant while adding a second resolver, packet completion state or review queue. One domain's accepted proposal could be presented as completion of outstanding QA impact. Global default-pack identity may be forced into project-bound KB rows or a second store without explaining the canonical relationship.

**Recommendation:** produce a canonical owner/alias table for each material ASER fact: existing ID, missing extension, writer, reader, lifecycle, migration and retirement rule. Make the packet a composed view of one canonical change identity unless a distinct invariant survives the negative proof. Keep human assignment a separately governed relation at the guide owner. Resolve whether “Skills Library” means the existing D-14 family; amend canonical UI explicitly if a different product decision is intended. Do not create a universal task entity solely to normalize generation, impact and defect statuses.

**Falsifier / closure evidence:** a reviewed owner allocation and two opposite examples: (a) reject all candidate edits while QA impact remains open; (b) approve one native artifact while another is deferred and a release remains unverified. Each must have one authoritative fact per decision and no mutable duplicate summary state. A platform pack without a project and a project-specific assignment must resolve through the same intended canonical owner without a fake project. If existing exact definitions already supply this, cite them and demonstrate the mappings; new tables are not required to close the finding.

### ASER-R02 — Reusing the case writer does not yet enforce exact protected publication

**Severity:** Major. **Category:** Boundary / Spec-Code-Drift. **Status:** Open. **Gate affected:** S0/I3 before any new governed agent writer. **Preparation origin:** C4; independently corroborates B02.

**Finding:** the source has useful exact-revision human approval machinery (`legacy.service.ts` 1821–1869; V151) and an Approved-only run gate with locks (`approved-run-cases.policy.ts`). However, case creation accepts supplied status, generic update retains Approved when status is omitted while changing steps, duplicate copies status, and the run policy checks status rather than a current-definition approval relationship. Approval receives `req.userId`; bearer middleware also sets that from the token owner, while the global REST guard permits scoped project writes outside its named administration exclusions. These source paths do not establish the target human-only/exact-definition boundary. [Pinned source `legacy.service.ts` 5149–5249, 5982–6150, 6188–6255; controller 603–611; `auth.middleware.ts` 17–39; `api-token-rest.guard.ts` 13–56; `app.module.ts` 60–61.]

Canonical P-07 and FR-027–037 additionally distinguish unpublished proposal, exact-revision approval and selected dependency-closed application from the native Draft/In Review/Approved case lifecycle. New official native case IDs appear at protected publication; merely inserting a Draft case through the current writer is not proof of that staged contract. ASER R-ASE-06's “existing case approval” must be read together with its I3 P-07 commitment, not used as an implementation shortcut.

**Impact:** a machine-authored semantic change can inherit or request the eligibility label, or be attributed to a human token owner without preserving credential type at the protected decision. A run can then rely on meaning that the cited approval did not review. Existing executed run snapshots must remain historical even when current eligibility changes.

**Recommendation:** explicitly map candidate submission → immutable operation revision → authorized review → selected atomic native application → definition approval/run eligibility. Preserve principal class through every privileged decision, including admin-owned machine tokens and alternate REST paths. Reuse V151/CAS/locks, but define semantic version identity and approval invalidation across all writers, including imports, duplicate, custom fields/tags and background agents. Keep native review status and shared publication state separate. Do not compare a pre-approval whole-row hash blindly to the post-approval row: the approval transition itself changes status/time.

**Falsifier / closure evidence:** a shared guard or database invariant at the pinned implementation that defeats the static paths, plus targeted tests for create-with-Approved, approved-case content update, duplicate, admin-owned token REST approval, stale revision and rejected proposal. A valid test must show no unauthorized accepted native mutation/new run association and must preserve historic run definitions. Also show one authorized interactive review/application success. The complete writer inventory and exact role policy remain prerequisites; no exploit was executed here.

### ASER-R03 — Governed revision identity must survive ordinary KB editing and retrieval

**Severity:** Major. **Category:** Invariant / Spec-Code-Drift. **Status:** Open. **Gate affected:** immutable version/assignment contract before S0/I3 governed use. **Preparation origin:** C3; corroborates B03 and narrows B04.

**Finding:** current KB history is a useful editing history, not proven immutable submitted/accepted asset identity. The locked writer snapshots the pre-edit body only outside its time window and updates the prior version's diff within that window. Two submitted bodies within one window do not necessarily each have retained immutable content. A non-AI-memory document can accept `body.status`; the writer lacks an expected-revision predicate. V94 ordinal uniqueness and V102 diff metadata do not fill that gap. [Pinned `legacy.service.ts` 12040–12185; V45/V94/V102, inspected during preparation.]

**Concrete counterexample:** draft A changes to B, B is submitted/reviewed, then C is saved inside the same coalescing window. A retained history ordinal can identify A while current content is C; there need not be an immutable B that a skill usage or policy approval can resolve. Serialization ensures atomic ordering, not preservation of each submitted meaning.

Current ANN and FTS retrieval additionally admit ordinary document types regardless of approved status; only AI-memory is filtered. This does **not** prove unlawful access or normative activation. It makes the consumer distinction material if new skill candidates are stored as ordinary KB content. An authorized reviewer may read candidate text, but the effective instruction resolver and downstream authoring must retain its untrusted/candidate status. [Pinned `rag-retrieval.service.ts` 265–282, 318–334; ASER skills §§12.1, 12.3, 12.6, 13.1–13.3.]

**Impact:** review, effective assignment, exported provenance or later authoring can refer to the wrong body/package or consume candidate text as accepted guidance. Confidential history may also require finer checks than visible summary identity. These are conditional migration hazards; no current runtime leak was shown.

**Recommendation:** extend the KB/guide owner with an explicit immutable submission/publication boundary, exact content plus semantic metadata/package digest and stale-base guard, while preserving ordinary autosave. Resolve approved authority independently of content readability. Inventory all authoring-context, recent/folder, ANN/FTS, direct history/export and cache consumers for their actual duty: authority selection where effective guidance is consumed; ACL and truthful provenance where drafts are legitimately read. Do not impose a blanket “only approved documents may be searched” rule or move governed content to a shadow service.

**Falsifier / closure evidence:** two submissions inside one autosave window, concurrent writers, later title/type/package changes, approval against stale base, revoked assignment with cached readers and historic usage lookup. Every reviewed revision must resolve to its exact bytes/semantic metadata; stale promotion must fail; historic links must remain accurate. An authorized draft-review search must still work without activating the draft. A reader inventory must show where authority is selected and where data is merely displayed. Old unpinned rows remain explicitly UNKNOWN; do not synthesize historical approval or skill provenance.

### ASER-R04 — Source and acceptance scope need a stable multi-source migration contract

**Severity:** Major. **Category:** Assumption / Invariant. **Status:** Open. **Gate affected:** multi-source/release design freeze; bounded single-source I1 may proceed only with explicit limits. **Preparation origin:** C5; independently corroborates and extends B05.

**Finding:** ASER R-ASE-37 and release workflow admit multiple repositories/branches/releases. Design §4's singular source object is illustrative, so it is not by itself a defect. The concrete source owner is nevertheless one `project_source_bindings` row per project, updated in place; it has no stable configuration revision identity in that table. Current trusted capture and fingerprints are useful existing protection, but do not alone establish delayed external-event reconciliation across rebinds or independent source/release acceptance. [V146; `project-source-binding.management.ts` 68–133; independently read `project-source-binding.ts` and `current-cache-graph.history.ts`; ASER design §§4, 12, 15.]

**Opposite-scope counterexample:** one shared-library change participates in two releases with different deployments and QA outcomes. A later webhook from repository A arrives after the project binding has changed to B. A separate policy revision becomes effective after the code deployment. One “packet approved/current source/released” field cannot decide all three relationships without contaminating a parent or rewriting history.

**Impact:** an event may be attributed to the wrong current source, a valid acceptance reused for the wrong build/scope, or a successful release cause another independent release to appear ready. Rejecting every new metadata field would also be wrong: source configuration history and independently authorized release decisions are durable facts.

**Recommendation:** extend the existing binding owner with the minimum stable source/configuration identity needed by retained event and capture references. Specify cardinality and scope for change → source revisions → artifact proposals → runs/builds → release decisions. Compose releases from the participating facts; retain decision/effectivity at its own relationship scope. Preserve existing observation fingerprints and unknown/rebind boundaries. If the first slice supports one source only, make multi-source input visibly unsupported/deferred and forbid claims that the slice satisfies R-ASE-37's full breadth.

**Falsifier / closure evidence:** delayed/out-of-order provider event after rebind; one commit in two releases; two repositories in one release; source event without agent session; configuration-only change; rollback and independent manual/policy effectivity. Queries must identify the exact scope each decision/evidence item supports and preserve old history. A shared source row per current project can remain as a convenience pointer if immutable referenced identity supplies the historic meaning.

## 6. Hostile siblings, participation and mutation consequences

These tests distinguish model adequacy from executed acceptance. Every outcome below is a required/proposed assertion, not a reported passing test.

| Attack | Opposite or hostile case | Required consequence / minimal owner |
| --- | --- | --- |
| False flatness / wrong parent | Requirement from an external provider; source-only change before a native requirement exists | Provider truth, observed source and proposed native expectation retain separate identity. No fake native requirement or session is fabricated to satisfy a packet shape. |
| Parent contamination / independent acceptance | BA accepts requirement, QA rejects a case, policy owner defers effectivity | Independent native review/application receipts survive. Change summary reports each disposition and outstanding impact; it cannot grant blanket acceptance. |
| Scope mutation | Skill assignment changes or project loses access while a case proposal is in flight | Re-resolve authority at governed commit, preserve submitted snapshot and reason for refusal. Historic artifact meaning remains pinned; payload access still follows current authorization policy. |
| Opposite-scope sibling | Skill is the primary object when authored; supporting method during case authoring; historic provenance during execution | One version owner plus appropriately scoped relations. No global assumption that all appearances mean current authority. |
| Raw evidence after governance revocation | Runner reports a real failure after the author's skill is revoked | Authorized observed evidence can be retained without inventing new skill-authored content; stale candidate promotion remains blocked. ASER skills §§12.4/13.2 and SK-E2E-23/28 already support this. |
| Meaning versus widget | Run marked Completed with untested cases; case marked Approved without any execution | Calculate execution/readiness from real attempt facts and required scope. Historical fieldwork documents these distinct meanings; it does not establish the current live state. |
| Mutation after reliance | Accepted policy or case changes after a run/release consumed it | New immutable successor; old decisions and attempts retain exact meaning. Current display label cannot rewrite historical expected behavior. |
| Derived-authority duplication | Cached workspace says ready after evidence revoked or source scope changed | Recompute or display stale/unknown with reasons. Cache/version tokens are implementation metadata, not a second release authority. |
| Partial failure / retry | Requirement commits, case call times out, notification fails | Durable per-item receipt distinguishes committed/uncertain/not-attempted; idempotent replay reconciles the operation. Notification outcome cannot roll back or certify domain truth. |
| Consumption / assignment race | Two agents claim the same work; one loses claim before publishing | Work ownership/lease, if required, is enforced by existing task owner and versioned assignment. Agent presence or a timeline event cannot independently consume the task or authorize publication. Exact lease requirements remain to be established, not invented here. |
| Universal UI | Solo tester, BA reviewer, external-policy reader and project with MCP disabled | Shared facts may have different labels/actions. No fictitious departments, compulsory model, forced seven-domain edits or hidden MCP activation. |
| Confidentiality | Public manual cites an internal policy or a global skill includes restricted attachments | Link visibility and payload visibility remain separate; derived summaries must not leak source content or create permission by association. |
| Failure cascade | Resolver unavailable during raw event ingestion or read-only source review | Authoring may fail closed for missing required governance; unrelated authorized observation and historic evidence remain truthful. No invented fallback active skill. |

**Omission check:** the minimum query set is “what changed; compared to which accepted expectation; what source/attempt/build was actually verified; what remains unknown; who may decide next; what committed despite a lost response; and which independent release/policy scope is affected?” The model cannot answer these using links, status labels or an agent narrative alone. Canonical impact, exact revision and evidence owners supply the necessary distinctions; omissions do not automatically justify new domains. The current sample does not prove complete role policy, native requirement acceptance authority, QA closure policy, provider/deploy trust, retention, scale limits or current UI parity. These remain explicit evidence/policy work, not assumed facts.

## 7. Rejected alarms and surviving alternatives

1. **“Skills must never exist” is rejected.** Versioned methodology and separately authorized assignment solve a real repeatability/governance need. The objection is duplicate ownership and unproven invariants, not the concept or name.
2. **“Every observation needs an active authoring skill” is rejected.** Preparation C6 is not promoted to a finding: skills §§12.4/13.2 and SK-E2E-23/28 explicitly preserve raw observations and external evidence without fabricated authoring provenance. Enforce that distinction across adapters during implementation.
3. **“Skill text grants policy/tool authority” is rejected as a claimed specification defect.** Skills §13.1–13.3 explicitly separates authoring guidance, normative policy, project consent, human publication and executable-resource permission. Runtime enforcement still needs proof.
4. **“Candidate readability is activation” is rejected.** Review/search can legitimately expose authorized candidate content with provenance. Effective resolution and confidential data access are distinct concerns. Blue B04 is useful only when narrowed accordingly.
5. **“The singular example JSON forbids multi-source” is rejected.** The proposal marks storage/API illustrative. R04 concerns a real source migration and decision-scope obligation, not an array-format preference.
6. **“Current service reuse means the target is already enforced” is rejected.** Existing transaction locks, CAS, guards and snapshots are valuable, but their exact scope matters. Preserve working mechanisms and close the named gaps.
7. **“Every governed artifact requires new generic infrastructure” is rejected.** Narrow typed extensions and composed native views remain viable. Source insufficiency cannot be used as proof that all existing owners are absent.

## 8. Historical coexistence and authentic UI boundary

Legacy Draft/Approved cases, ordinary KB documents, generation tasks, provider caches and source captures must continue to have their actual old meanings during migration. Do not backfill exact approvals, skill-use pins, native requirement authority or source provenance that was never recorded. Preserve run snapshots and old receipt identifiers; represent unknown provenance explicitly. Additive migrations need bounded compatibility, stale-client behavior and a declared point at which new governed writes require the stronger contract. [ASER implementation S2; canonical 03 every-writer policy; source and findings above.]

**UI status: VISUAL-BASELINE BLOCKED.** The historical fieldwork differentiates user-supplied existing screenshots, signed-off proposed mockups and authenticated DOM observations. None supplies current pin/build/role/theme/viewport proof here. No screenshot pixels were inspected during this review. Two old approved test-case mockup concepts do not approve a new Skills or Change Workspace layout. ASER should extend existing Requirements, KB, Zyra change review, cases, tasks and settings using actual current components. Exact before/after controls, responsive/focus states, role restrictions and indirect shared-component consumers require source plus authentic current UI evidence from the relevant later gates. The canonical D-14 restriction remains a product constraint independently of visual proof.

## 9. Handoff and stop boundary

White/Grey may use this receipt to consider the bounded intent and unresolved model/acceptance cases. It is not a green Gate B token. The four findings are all **Open** against the original pinned inputs. Root may author corrections in a separate working set; that does not amend this receipt or mark findings addressed. A later Red delta review must compare those corrections and their evidence against the falsifiers above.

For formal closeout, obtain the required second Red member's recorded disposition, resolve the upstream Gate A evidence/quorum limitation, and review exact changed source/spec identities. For architecture freeze, first provide R01's canonical owner allocation and explicit dispositions of R02–R04's migration contracts. Before governed implementation/release claims, the corresponding negative and authorized-success tests, real source/deploy/tool identity, and authentic UI/evidence gates must be executed in their authorized environment.

**Final analytical position:** keep the connected workflow, compress it onto Tesbo's native and canonical owners, preserve independent revision/approval/effectivity/evidence scopes, and retain missing information as UNKNOWN. No new ASER top-level persistence primitive has passed a negative design proof in this review.

## Reconstructed problem, independently of the proposed nouns

A developer may change source before a formal requirement exists. A QA engineer, BA, policy reviewer or PM arriving later must be able to reconstruct the authorized intent, exact change, effects on existing expected behavior and documents, real test scope/results, review decisions, remaining work and source/build to which those facts apply. The problem exists equally when an external agent is offline, a human edits manually, a change is documentation-only, an external provider owns a requirement, or the target has no approved behavioral model.

The minimum needed truths are: (1) stable project/source/record identity; (2) approved expectations distinct from observations and agent interpretations; (3) attributed revisioned observations and proposals; (4) current effective instructions without granting authority through text; (5) per-domain human acceptance and selected application; (6) immutable real execution/evidence at attempt and build grain; (7) durable follow-up ownership; and (8) derived, authorization-filtered views of the above. A change summary, board card, chart, graph, tool name or marketing label does not create another owner for these truths.

Actors include human project owner/manager, actual coding principal, delegated authoring agents, independent QA/BA/policy reviewers, external requirement/source/CI/deploy providers, native result reporters, and people consuming permission-limited status. They do not share privileges merely because they participate in one change. A single solo user can perform multiple human roles subject to project policy; a token owned by that user remains a machine credential.

Material transitions include provisional source capture → verified bytes; proposed expectation → reviewed exact revision; reviewed operation → applied native record; selected case → actually executed assertion; release decision → deployment observation → subsequent failure/retest. Stale source, no independent oracle, missing evidence, declined proposal, canceled work and inaccessible history must remain representable. Notification delivery and reading a notification do not close QA work. Case approval status and protected publication are different transitions.

The product questions that survive deletion of the ASER label are: What actually changed? Against which accepted expectation? Which exact records need reconsideration? Who may decide/apply? What evidence exists for this build and scope? What was attempted, refused, left unknown, or committed despite a lost response? What can the next actor safely do?

## Preparation questions to falsify after Blue handoff

These are **candidate design concerns**, not a completed finding list or expected Gate B result. Blue may supply evidence that changes them.

### C1 — Does Change Packet add a second change/review lifecycle?

ASER R-ASE-01, design §§3–4 and work package D propose `changeId`, mutable packet status, artifact dispositions and cross-artifact review. Canonical FR-115/116/119, technical v1.4.20 and API `start_quality_change` already define versioned changeset/event identity, authoritative impact obligations and source attestation; FR-027–037/T-304–305 own shared proposal/operation review. The ASER files do not name these exact owners/IDs. Calling the packet coordination metadata is insufficient to show whether it aliases, extends or competes with them.

**Prepared attack:** delete packet persistence and compose the existing changeset, review-operation receipts and impact lifecycle into a projection. Which authored fact remains unrepresentable? If a new durable packet is needed, identify its sole missing invariant and relationship to changeset/event/proposal identity before adding another current/completed field. Existing graph-capture history is observed truth and should not be overloaded with authored acceptance.

**Falsifier:** one source revision produces one canonical change identity; two sessions/providers can relate to it without duplicate work; rejecting all proposals can finish the proposal process while the source impact remains separately unverified. A conforming alias/extension is acceptable; no requirement to eliminate legitimate new metadata.

### C2 — Is Skills Library a new owner or a projection of the already planned guide owner?

ASER R-ASE-21–36, design §10.4 and skills §§2/7 describe skill identities/versions, assignments, reviews, usages and library/governance navigation. Canonical FR-076/077/106–113, T-422/T-448–452 explicitly reserve one QA Standards & Playbooks owner/resolver and independent asset versions. Canonical UI v1.4.19 says extend D-14 rather than introduce a new Skills page. Pinned V45 is project-scoped and cannot establish a global pack simply by being called a reusable KB.

**Prepared attack:** classify skill text as governed guide content, assignment as separately governed applicability relation, effective manifest as projection, and usage as historical provenance. Test reusing/extending T-422 for each. The new security boundary on assignment may be valid; it does not by itself justify a second library/resolver/review database or changed navigation. Clarify whether “Skills Library” is only a renamed view inside the reserved owner and preserve canonical platform/workspace pins.

**Opposite sibling:** platform-approved default read without a project yet; project-specific policy authoring; no-model-key native user; disabled external MCP. No single project-KB identity can implicitly own all these scopes.

### C3 — Is KB version history strong enough for the promised exact immutable references?

ASER R-ASE-04/08/21/30 and design §3 rely on KB identity and versions. Proposal does acknowledge that compatibility must be proved. Pinned `writeKnowledgeDocument` snapshots the pre-edit body only when its throttling window has elapsed; a later edit in the window updates the prior snapshot's cached diff rather than preserving every submitted state. V45 history rows have no approval-state, effective-binding or package-digest semantics. V94 proves ordinal uniqueness only.

**Prepared attack:** two candidate submissions or a reviewed policy revision inside one snapshot window. Can a retained revision identify the exact approved body and attachment/package set after a subsequent edit? Reuse document identity/bytes/history mechanisms, but extend the proper owner for immutable submitted snapshots instead of pretending the current history already supplies them. Do not require unrelated ordinary KB edits to inherit new publication restrictions without a scoped migration decision.

### C4 — Case lifecycle approval is not the protected proposal/application boundary

ASER R-ASE-06 stresses candidate cases, existing case approval and approved-only run membership; design says native services. Canonical technical §3 explicitly distinguishes native Draft/In Review/Approved case state from unpublished proposal state and allocates new official IDs only at protected publication. Canonical FR-032–037 and API §2 forbid agent approval/merge and require selected dependency-closed apply. Pinned MCP directly invokes native case writes; new case default Draft alone is not the canonical shared staging invariant.

**Prepared attack:** revision of an existing approved case through legacy MCP while a separate candidate is under review; rejected candidate; approved-only run retaining old approved definition. Require exact mapping between ASER candidate, shared proposal operation, canonical case identity/status, human decision and apply receipt. A proposed hardened shared writer is valid; source reuse alone does not establish it. This concern is about the stated bridge, not a demand to implement code during specification review.

### C5 — Multi-source change scope and independent decisions may not fit one flat packet

R-ASE-37 admits several repositories/branches/releases. Design §4 shows one optional source binding, one base/candidate commit and one work item; design §15 requires exact release evidence. The single-source JSON is labelled illustrative, so this is an untested boundary, not proof that every design must use arrays.

**Prepared opposite cases:** one shared library commit used by two independent releases; one release includes two repositories and a configuration-only deployment; policy becomes effective after code and manual publish on different schedules; spontaneous worktree change not yet committed. Require explicit cardinality, lineage and independently scoped approvals/validity, with historical decisions preserved. A release view should compose those owners rather than promote the packet into the release authority.

### C6 — Universal skill enforcement versus observed/raw evidence and continuity

R-ASE-25/30/34 and S0/S2 use broad every-write/provenance wording; skills §12.4 correctly exempts raw externally sourced evidence from invented authoring skill use and §13.2 says raw observations do not become newly authored artifacts. Final review should check these exceptions are authoritative at every adapter, not just prose guidance.

**Prepared counterexample:** a runner reports a failed real test after its author's skill was revoked. Refuse promotion of a stale candidate, but preserve authorized raw evidence, denial reason and incident/QA continuity. Another case is a human-uploaded regulator document: ingest truthful provenance without pretending an AI authored its rule or choosing its authority. Strict pinning where authoring occurred and honest N/A where it did not is a valid alternative to universal blocking.

## Preliminary negative-design alternatives (not approvals)

| Proposed concept | Best alternative to try first | New truth that may survive compression | What remains to prove |
|---|---|---|---|
| Change Packet | COMPOSE / EXTEND canonical changeset + common review | Cross-domain applicability receipt and correlation | One ID/lifecycle owner and historical migration; not a second QA completion state |
| Skills Library | EXTEND T-422 guide/asset-version owner; UI PROJECTION | Additive authoring candidate plus immutable package metadata | Exact fit to platform/workspace/project scopes and existing canonical UI |
| Skill assignment | EXTEND existing guide/workspace/project binding | Approved version/context applicability and separate activation history | Explicit reuse mapping, current resolution, revocation and human assent |
| Skill review/usage | REUSE/EXTEND reviewed operation + audit/provenance edges | Exact hash-bound review, historical consumed assignment | No parallel approval queue; invariants stronger than current coalesced KB snapshots |
| Native requirement | EXTEND current Requirements with scoped KB-backed content if compatible | Native approved expectation/version/criteria without provider | Canonical open authority decision and source link identity; a KB note is not enough |
| Policy/decision/rule | Typed governed content + relationships, not generic new store | Effective normative meaning and accountable authority | Approval/effectivity/retention scope and exact relation version |
| General work item | EXTEND/EXTRACT suitable board substrate; compose Bug links | Non-generation assignment/blocking/acceptance | Do not collapse generation done, implementation done and QA verified |
| Activity timeline | PROJECTION of native immutable receipts/attested observations | Attributed external event receipt if not yet stored | Report vs observed tiers, stable causality and confidentiality |
| Readiness/metrics | PROJECTION only | Human release decision remains independent durable truth | Grain, stale reasons, scope, denominator; no new green-badge authority |

REUSE unchanged must be tested, not presumed from similar columns. EXTEND, EXTRACT and COMPOSE remain valid when they preserve existing invariants. A new primitive is justified only for a surviving distinct truth whose lifecycle/scope cannot coherently be expressed at the existing owner. Missing evidence remains UNKNOWN.

## Prepared mutation and participation probes

1. Skill is intrinsic subject when proposed/evaluated; it is supporting methodology when a case is authored; a usage row is historical provenance during a run. Revoking an assignment cannot rewrite old case meaning or erase failed evidence.
2. A policy is primary when changed; supporting authority for an implementation; a public manual may project only a permission-filtered description. External origin does not mean public exposure.
3. A source observation can exist without a session, expected model or formal requirement. Missing agent report cannot imply no change. A source binding change cannot compare unrelated scopes as a clean reverse diff.
4. A Bug may originate in one QA attempt, cause a code fix in another change, and need several independently scoped retests; no single board status proves closure for all.
5. A human can accept one proposal and reject another. Explicit apply receipts, source impact assessment, record publication and release decision remain separate even when the UI composes them.
6. Access revocation changes permitted current read/act outcomes; it must preserve durable historical identifiers without leaking restricted payload. A summary permission is not payload permission.

## Handoff boundary

Next action: receive Blue's Gate A receipt from root, review its archaeology/reuse/consumer evidence, challenge these preparation hypotheses against it, resolve false positives, and then produce immutable `receipts/red-initial.md` with final scoped findings, negative-design proof, conceptual falsifiers and explicit evidence limits. No final Gate B/freeze claim is made now.

## 4. Independent reconstruction of the intended system

The proposal is useful if it coordinates existing work without changing the meaning of approved requirements, approved cases or actual verification. A developer/agent establishes project/change context, identifies affected domains, resolves the governing published method and applicable assignments, drafts changes using native owners, and records rationale for affected or not-applicable domains. Authorized humans decide protected publication. Native execution/evidence then informs verification. A coordination receipt can explain which artifacts committed, remain drafts, are blocked, or still require review; it cannot make all linked records current or proven merely by existing.

The three kinds of truth that must remain distinguishable are **intended approved behavior**, **observed implementation/source**, and **observed verification**. A coding-agent assertion is not an independent source observation. A requirement link is not coverage. A run selection is not execution. Evidence metadata is not readable evidence bytes, and readable bytes are not proof that the evidence supports the asserted result. Source commit equality reported by a run is not proof of the deployed build. Historical unknowns must remain explicit.

Canonical publication is stronger than a boolean status. FR-030/032 and the design's selected-apply mechanism require stable logical operations, immutable operation revisions, exact review/base/profile/policy identities, dependency-closed explicit selection and durable commitment. A previously committed logical operation cannot publish again through a fresh request key or a later revision; a real correction is a new explicit operation. Protected apply and required audit belong in one native transaction; external effects need recoverable post-commit handling. Partial/mixed dispositions are legitimate, and aggregate completion should not require publication of rejected or copy-only work.

The proposal's seven applicability domains are coordination dimensions, not seven mandatory writes. Small changes may legitimately leave most domains unchanged with evidence-backed reasons. Human-only skill publication/assignment is distinct from ordinary artifact authoring; neither an administrator-owned credential nor approved skill text acquires human decision authority. Imported skill files/scripts are inert content unless separately permitted. Structural conflict checks can be deterministic, but unrestricted prose cannot be declared semantically safe by a mechanical classifier. The proposal already states some of these limits, and a later review must not falsely report them as entirely missing.

Source is visible and wired; current deployment, opt-in grant enforcement and complete conformance are UNKNOWN here. |

T-422 implementation maturity was not exhaustively established. Do not call the owner absent merely because no dedicated Skills page appears. |
