## 5. Precommitted falsifiers and alternative tests

These are independently selected review questions, not a finding list supplied by the author or another reviewer. They are deliberately falsifiable. A later corrected contract may answer them. Each disposition must be judged against the exact text/source rather than counted by matching words.

### S2-P01 — one effective-method authority

**Primary basis:** canonical FR-076/077, FR-106–114; `03_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md` 663–724; original ASER skill/governance design.

**Concern:** a separately described Skills Library/resolver/assignment system could compete with T-422's platform pack, workspace reference and project overlay. Merely saying “reuse KB” is insufficient because V45 requires project/organization ownership and does not supply a platform immutable pack.

**Falsifier:** one platform approved method is installed at a workspace, a project adds a permitted overlay, and a task requests a conflicting weaker instruction. Trace one governing resolver, exact precedence, authorized version/assignment pins, and the same output through internal/external entry points. Two independently writable active pins or duplicate precedence owners falsify the one-owner claim.

**Valid alternative:** narrowly add the missing platform revision/workspace-pin metadata within T-422 or its approved shared representation, while preserving project KB identity. No mandate to force platform content into a fake project or to implement a generic policy engine now. A corrected owner crosswalk plus scoped seam may satisfy the design stage; a new runtime subsystem is not automatically required.

### S2-P02 — approval survives all entrances and edits

**Primary basis:** canonical P-07/FR-030/032/033; case create/update/approve route and transaction; API-token middleware/guard; Approved-case admission policy.

**Observed source:** case insert uses `body.status || "Draft"`; update directly applies supplied status. The dedicated approval transaction requires an expected revision and records it, which is positive evidence. API authentication distinguishes `req.apiToken` but also supplies its issuer's `userId`; the inspected approval controller/service accepts that user identity. I observed no machine/human check in that inspected route chain. This is a source-level boundary concern, not a demonstrated runtime exploit or assertion that every application gate was inspected.

**Falsifiers:** attempt an administrator-owned machine credential against approve, direct create/update with Approved, and an approved case edited afterward. Also compare MCP, UI/REST, bulk/import, legacy Zyra and automation entrances. The proposed policy must refuse unauthorized protected publication in the authoritative writer and must not leave changed content falsely authorized merely because status remained Approved. A refusal solely in the UI is insufficient.

**Valid alternative:** a shared guarded adapter can retain existing native writers while admission and exact revision proof are centralized. Raw source/evidence observations may still be recorded without pretending they are human approval. The contract should stage compatibility migration explicitly rather than claim current complete enforcement or disable unrelated legitimate evidence capture.

### S2-P03 — immutable published identity is not a snapshot label

**Primary basis:** FR-112; V45; KB write/version logic at 12040–12230 and the 15-minute snapshot interval; original version/usage claims.

**Observed source:** current KB history is interval-coalesced. Within the snapshot window it can update the latest version's change summary/fields while editing the current document; the inspected general update path does not require an expected revision. That is useful history but cannot by itself prove immutable submitted/published skill or policy revision identity.

**Falsifier:** edit content twice inside the snapshot interval, submit one exact draft, concurrently modify it, approve a different revision, and later inspect an artifact generated under the older effective assignment. The historical artifact must identify the bytes and governing assignment actually used. Current content, a mutable history annotation or a retrospectively inferred skill cannot substitute.

**Valid alternative:** extend the existing content/version owner with immutable publication/draft references and exact approval identity; do not require a second content store. Mark legacy reconstruction UNKNOWN/RECONSTRUCTED and keep unaffected linked artifacts historical until a separately justified change is reviewed.

### S2-P04 — coordination and retry identity remain causal

**Primary basis:** canonical FR-030 and design 212–249; original R-ASE-13 idempotency and Change Packet; current graph review identity.

**Concern:** project/operation/client idempotency keys alone do not establish logical operation commitment across uncertain acknowledgement, changed packet revisions or multiple clients. A new Change Packet must have an explicit relation to the existing change/review envelope, rather than silently becoming the second lifecycle owner.

**Falsifiers:** a native write commits and its acknowledgement is lost; a second agent restages the same logical operation with a new request key; a later packet version includes an already committed dependency; an external effect fails after commit. The outcome must preserve exactly which native operation committed once, its returned identity, remaining actionable work and recoverable effects. Conversely, two intentionally different cases with identical text must not be collapsed by global content-hash deduplication.

**Valid alternative:** compose a receipt/projection over the existing logical-operation and change identities, with narrow metadata where an independent invariant truly needs persistence. A partial native success with explicit blocked siblings is valid; inventing a global cross-service atomic transaction is unnecessary.

### S2-P05 — optional MCP exposure is separate from method availability

**Primary basis:** canonical P-18 and `04_MCP_API_CONTRACTS.md` 615–659; current MCP service/controller/token/guard; original external-agent design.

**Observed source:** current code has useful project/member/scope checks and a real registry/schema fingerprint. The inspected paths do not establish the canonical default-off, named client/session grant policy. Therefore “no fingerprint exists” would be a false finding, while “current P-18 enforcement is complete” is unsupported by this evidence.

**Falsifiers:** external MCP is disabled or its grant is revoked while an agent retains a token/session; internal authorized method use still works. The external read/list/discovery/write paths must obey current admission and visibility. Rediscovery after a schema change must not replay previously committed logical operations. Permission revocation cannot be renewed by an effective-method cache.

**Valid alternative:** extend the existing gateway and shared domain checks, keeping project authoring methods useful through authorized internal/native channels. Tool count, schema fingerprint or token ownership is evidence of neither interoperability nor publication permission.

### S2-P06 — evidence and requirement uncertainty cannot become readiness

**Primary basis:** canonical FR-110/112/115–124; current cached requirement projector and run/evidence links; original R-ASE-37–48.

**Falsifiers:** a provider requirement is missing or ambiguous; a current cache link exists but upstream freshness is unknown; a run reports the same commit without deployment attestation; all attachment metadata rows exist but bytes are missing; a graph is unchanged but no applicable tests ran. The resulting consistency/readiness view must preserve each relevant unknown, source authority and exact version rather than emit a clean pass or invent acceptance criteria.

**Valid alternative:** a useful coordination view may say “observed”, “reported”, “provisional”, “blocked” or “unknown” and link to the existing records. A missing native requirement owner may block authoritative expected-behavior publication while still allowing source observation and draft review. The source already demonstrates these distinctions in several places; corrected specs should preserve and extend them.

### S2-P07 — scope, visibility and semantic judgment are independent axes

**Primary basis:** original manuals/policies/skill-governance design, especially 12.3 and 13; KB permissions; canonical access and content-owner rules.

**Falsifiers:** a guide applies at workspace level but only selected project members may read a linked confidential policy; an imported SKILL.md includes instructions to transmit secrets or execute a script; two free-text skills are structurally valid but conflict semantically. Applicability must not grant visibility, text approval must not grant execution/network authority, and structural resolution must not be advertised as proof of semantic safety.

**Valid alternative:** a bounded deterministic rule subset with human adjudication and explicit unresolved conflict state is sufficient. Some raw evidence legitimately has no skill-derived authorship. Do not force every observation through a generation policy or universal “skills used” claim. Credential security and native authorization remain their established owners.

### S2-P08 — useful native journey, authentic UI and operational bounds

**Primary basis:** canonical workflows/backlog/UI tasks; historical baseline; current Zyra change page and navigation; original implementation plan and budgets.

**Falsifiers:** follow a small change affecting only one domain; follow a cross-role change requiring QA, BA and policy decisions; navigate after a write whose result arrives late; re-enter after a partial commit; inspect a private linked artifact and an unavailable source. A packet must make actual remaining action clear without forcing seven artificial updates or falsely completing rejected work. A proposed Skills surface must reconcile existing D14/QA Standards placement. Source structure alone cannot establish visual compatibility.

**Valid alternative:** extend existing pages/panels and shared projections. Record a seam now for future use rather than implementing a speculative universal workflow. Unknown latency/context/concurrency budgets must become explicit owner/measurement gates before runtime delivery; no invented SLO is needed to make the proposal honest today.

## 6. Green second-order preparation and scope restraint

The first useful application of the invariant is a generated test-case proposal governed by a published testing method. The adjacent application is a manual/policy revision informed by the same change but with its own audience, decision authority and revision history. A credible third use is a later incident or release review that needs to recover the exact historical methods, requirement assumptions, approved definitions and observed evidence without rewriting them. The shared invariant is identity, temporal provenance and authority across native records. The participation roles differ: a method governs authoring; a requirement supplies expected behavior; an evidence file supports an observation; a task tracks human work. They should not be flattened into a single attachment type or universal approval state.

The hardest sibling is an unplanned implementation change with no approved requirement or trustworthy deployment identity. The system should still observe and route work, while leaving expectation and verification unresolved. This tests whether the design is a useful coordination layer rather than a machine for manufacturing certainty.

Deleting the marketing names “ASER” and “Skills” should leave understandable native owners and relationships. Current source and canonical plans already support much of the vocabulary. A coherent resolver/receipt/reference extension can be justified; a new graph store, policy engine, method catalog, event bus or workflow service needs its own source-backed negative reuse proof. Nothing in this preparation recommends implementing those speculative systems.

## 7. Handoff conditions and deliberately unresolved questions

The later corroboration must be a separate receipt bound to the actual corrected bytes and actual first-member receipts. I will record agreement, narrower agreement, challenge or unresolved uncertainty per substantive claim, including the strongest valid alternative. I will not edit this cold reconstruction to match later conclusions. If the corrected snapshot changes, any prior acceptance is limited to the reviewed bytes.

Outstanding independent limits include: full writer/consumer enumeration; complete existing T-422 implementation status; detailed requirement/AC authority choice; all source/change/model dependency behavior; deployed schema/source/runtime identity; real browser baseline and approved mockup assets; external coding-agent clients; test execution; and measured budgets. A design may specify appropriate gates for these without pretending they are resolved. Conversely, a gate label without an owner, necessary contract or relevant acceptance obligation is not automatic closure.

This file supplies one member's independent baseline and falsifiers. It grants no human approval, no implementation authorization beyond the stated task, no deployment readiness, and no automatic second vote.

# Security delta v1 — corrected specification, independent review

**Reviewer:** `/root/security` · **Date:** 19 November 2026 · **Target:** immutable `review-snapshots/v1/{canonical,views}`, proposed Tesbo 1.4.28. **Method:** unchanged canonical Security/orchestration at `d83549c85a4dcd047fb40dc4fba4ec62737ab0fc`, with the same Tesbo portability binding as the initial receipt.

White pre-gate was read in full after explicit handoff. Its **BLOCKED — DUPLICATE/UNCLEAR OWNERSHIP** verdict permits bounded corrective drafting/re-review and withholds freeze/implementation admission. This delta is that bounded specialist review; it does not override White, establish formal quorum, ratify product authority or certify runtime behavior. No other new team receipt or author discussion was read. The original Security receipt is unchanged.

Paths: **C/** = `review-snapshots/v1/canonical/`; **V/** = `review-snapshots/v1/views/`; **B/** = `source/blue/Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/`. All product source remains pinned to `0c54b8ed28c9dec51ed0ca3bbb3cd3b7b8ab12d8`.

## 1. Threat-model delta

The correction introduces no new runtime capability: it makes actual-principal preservation, protected submission/apply, candidate retrieval, source history and composite recovery explicit across existing owners. The security consequence is a narrower design interpretation: readable candidate text cannot acquire instruction authority, machine issuer identity cannot supply human assent, and a successfully received observation cannot manufacture approval or execution evidence. Those controls remain proposed until implemented and independently tested.

## 2. Boundaries and changed contract

| Boundary | Corrected authorization/integrity decision and owner | Fails closed? |
|---|---|---|
| Machine → human approval | C/04 ASER-C03:951–956 preserves actual credential kind, requires server-owned action-bound human review evidence, rejects issuer-ID/client-flag assent, binds semantic dependencies and atomic protected apply. | Required by contract; **not proved in implementation**. |
| External client → discovery/read/write | C/03:1377 and C/04:951 require P-18 before both aliases, initialization/discovery/calls and cached sessions; native access and separately authorized provider intake are independent. | Explicit rollout prerequisite; current source gap remains. |
| Candidate → readers/effective context | C/01:910–913,925–928; C/03:1544–1550; C/04:991–997 cover direct/history/list/search, RAG, folder/recent/fallback, graph/export/summary and caches with separate authority and exposure. | Required by contract; reader inventory and real tests remain open. |
| Composite → committed native effects | C/04:958–989 separates receipt lookup, explicit resume, uncertainty reconciliation and partial staging from atomic selected publication; final owners reauthorize. | Design is determinate enough to falsify; no transaction test run. |
| Raw evidence → governed artifact/result | C/04:956,974; C/05 ASE-INT-09:1528 preserve independently authorized raw failures after skill revocation, without promotion or fabricated execution proof. | No general bypass authorized by the exception. Implementation unverified. |
| Source/release → historical reliance | C/04:941–947 pins binding/configuration and many-to-many release scope; delayed events cannot advance an unrelated active binding. | Historical/migration contract specified; actual schema remains narrower. |
| Restricted data → role summary | C/02:693–720 and C/04:929–935,993 require field-filtered projections, safe incomplete states, current authorization and no hidden counts. | Specified; no UI/export/cache or runtime proof. |

## 3. Finding dispositions and regression review

| Finding | Severity / confidence / evidence | v1 disposition |
|---|---|---|
| **SEC-P01** — status and issuer-identity approval bypass | **HIGH / HIGH**, source-read and reasoned composed effect. Same prerequisites and same-project blast radius as initial receipt. | **Addressed at contract level; implementation OPEN.** C/01 R-ASE-06:771 and R-ASE-34:926, C/03:1373, C/04:951–955, C/05:1444,1522–1523 explicitly cover generic status paths, semantic dependencies, actual principal, exact human assent and new-run eligibility. No wording claims the source is fixed. |
| **SEC-P02** — missing P-18 boundary in inspected request chain | **HIGH / HIGH**, source-read. Requires a valid bound token and current membership; not anonymous/foreign-project compromise. | **Addressed at contract/rollout level; implementation OPEN.** C/03:1377 says closure is required before integration exposure, C/04:951 includes both aliases and cached sessions, and C/05:1445/1528 supplies the OFF/unknown/revoked and positive cases. This applies to external read exposure as well as new writes. |
| **SEC-P03** — conditional KB candidate retrieval exposure | **MEDIUM / HIGH** source condition, reasoned future consequence. Applies if governed candidates reuse current KB readers unchanged. | **Addressed at contract level; substrate admission OPEN.** C/03:1375,1546–1550 and C/04:993 prohibit relying only on a subtype/new resolver. Authorized candidate inspection remains valid; confidentiality and governing authority are tested independently in ASE-INT-06. |
| **SEC-DV1-01** — canonical file calls itself a subordinate view | **LOW / HIGH**, document source-read; S0 authority clarity. No attacker or runtime exploit established. | **Advisory correction requested.** C/03:1346 and1542 contain copied view-only wording inside the canonical file. This could confuse the authority location, although C/00:612 and C/03:1387 explicitly establish the canonical owners/precedence. Replace only the misplaced canonical wording; do not weaken the subordinate status of repository views. |

No additional High/Critical contract regression was identified in the changed text. This is a bounded conclusion about the correction, not an assertion that the application has no other vulnerabilities.

### Source challenge, independently checked

The code remains consistent with the initial findings: the approval controller passes only `req.userId` (B/legacy/legacy.controller.ts:603–611); the native method checks membership/revision and writes issuer attribution (service:1821–1853); the REST guard allows project-bound write methods outside its narrow administration block (B/auth/api-token-rest.guard.ts:22–54). MCP still checks project/membership and broad scope before registry/call dispatch (B/mcp/mcp.service.ts:142–169,198–257). RAG still admits non-`ai_memory` documents independently of draft status (B/rag/rag-retrieval.service.ts:269–280,319–336). Contracts cannot falsify those source observations.

One additional primary file was fetched at the pinned SHA: `src/legacy/testcase-edit-revision.ts`, Git blob `45825b7a895953b72e6c3f2cc1abebe2a688b49f`. Lines3–10 explicitly define a stored-row edit precondition and exclude independently stored custom-field/tag/relationship coverage. This corroborates the correction's semantic dependency requirement; an exact row hash alone is not a complete authorization/approval oracle.

Two material consumer mechanisms were also directly checked, without inheriting another team's verdict. KB updates serialize but coalesce snapshots and update cached diffs; ordinary non-memory status is directly mutable and the inspected UPDATE has no caller CAS predicate (B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:12040–12181; constant15 minutes at11057). `createBug` and `addBugLink` invoke `failLinkedExecutions` after association, and it can change non-Failed execution status, time and actor for runs not rejected by the finalized-automation guard (8195–8254,8476–8553,9249–9266). C/04:952,956 and C/05:1446–1447,1521–1522 correctly require governed snapshots and separation of association from reviewed result correction. These are source corroborations, not runtime Major closure or a substitute for QA's independent journey disposition.

## 4. Attack-chain disposition

The original **HIGH integrity chain**—machine modifies a definition/status, then status-only admission accepts it for a new run—remains possible under the inspected source conditions and was not executed. The corrected contract explicitly forbids both links and tests the final native effect. The conditional KB-poisoning chain is also addressed across retrieval and final authorization, rather than relying on semantic lint; model compliance and future implementation remain untested. No Critical chain or new cross-project exploit was established.

## 5. Closure and check status

Each original High retains **one selected closure mechanism: a real API/DB regression test**, not a document check or implicit risk acceptance.

| Finding | Concrete required regression and canonical mapping | Status |
|---|---|---|
| SEC-P01 | QA-444/445 plus ASE-INT-03/04: machine token owned by an admin and other permitted issuers; create/update/bulk/clone/import/restore/direct approval; semantic dependency mutation; new-run admission; valid interactive human transition; stale and mandatory-audit failure. Read back zero unauthorized approvals, revisions and run items; retain old run snapshots. | **NOT RUN / OPEN**. |
| SEC-P02 | QA-180/182/436, ASE-FLOW-15 and ASE-INT-09: real HTTP on both MCP aliases, current enabled positive control, then OFF/unknown/revoked policy using the same valid token/session and direct guessed calls. No protected discovery/data; independent authorized native/provider route remains legitimate. | **NOT RUN / OPEN**. |
| SEC-P03 | ASE-INT-06 and SK-E2E-14/19/21/25: malicious test-only candidate plus classified markers through every reader/context/export/cache; authorized inert candidate positive case; revoked audience and cross-project denial. | **NOT RUN / substrate gate OPEN**. |

**Binding checks:** unchanged review method, target P-07/P-11/P-14/P-18 and repository policy; exact immutable input identity; preservation of original findings; actual-principal/object authorization and truthful evidence boundary. The proposed amendment is assessed for compliance, not granted independent policy authority.

**Advisory checks completed:** full corrected amendment reading, source-to-contract consequence tracing, original-falsifier reconciliation, alternate consumer/raw-evidence/composite checks, and corrected tests' required/forbidden-effect scrutiny. Source controls and explicit uncertainties were retained rather than interpreted as missing merely because runtime was unavailable.

**Mechanical only:** this reviewer independently verified14/14 snapshot SHA-256 values and44/44 cached source Git blobs (39 original,4 preparation supplemental,1 delta supplemental). `verification-v1.json` is a document-integrity report; its successful negative control is not a product-security result.

**NOT RUN:** product tests, database migrations, actual HTTP/MCP and two-client interoperability, race/revocation/expiry execution, provider-signature validation, malicious-content execution, evidence-byte recovery, production configuration, secret/dependency scanning, browser/UI/AT and operational controls. The changed source inventory is still incomplete beyond named inspected owners. No metric or test-count inference supplies these results.

## 6. Residual admission gates

Before affected capability exposure: close the complete writer/reader inventory, deny/positive regressions, migration/legacy dispositions and source/build/schema identity; configure independently authorized human review/root recovery and explicit role/capability mappings; implement actual expiry/grant/revocation semantics; decide bounded resource/retention and recovery policies. Source-binding administration and trusted attestation scope must be deliberately authorized; no universal human-only policy is inferred from the separate skills rule. Current server-approved checkout restrictions must survive migration.

C/03:1391,1481–1512 and C/04:997 keep these as admission gates with safe defaults. Free-text maliciousness cannot be proven absent by lint; scripts, network authority and tool rights remain separate even for approved text. A compromised fully authorized human session and uninspected operational controls are not resolved by this design review.

## 7. Verdict

**ACCEPTABLE-RISK — mitigations planned; specification-delta advice only.** The original actionable risks now have concrete canonical owners, contract restrictions, rollout prerequisites and non-vacuous tests. This is the Security charter's advisory category for planned mitigation, **not acceptance of the runtime risk** and not implementation/release approval. SEC-P01/02 remain open High implementation prerequisites; SEC-P03 remains a conditional substrate gate. White's pre-gate/freeze restrictions and other outstanding role/quorum/Graph decisions remain in force.

## 8. False-positive and regression log

- The correction does not allow partial official publication: C/04:954,971 separates it from resumable staging. A receipt read cannot silently resume (965–969).
- The evidence exception does not authorize stale governed writes or MCP use while disabled: C/04:951,956 and ASE-INT-09 require independent authorization.
- Candidate readability does not imply effective assignment: C/04:993 and C/03:1546 explicitly preserve safe review.
- The current exact approval CAS, project/token checks, run locks, finalized-automation protection and KB transaction locks are real source controls. They are narrower than the new whole-invariant requirements, not absent protection.
- Readiness cannot become self-authored release authority: C/04:939,945,978–989 pins independent decisions, input revisions and surviving obligations.

## 9. Coverage, integrity and immutable handoff

All eight1.4.28 amendment bodies and changed headers were read. All six views were reviewed against their fully read originals through every changed line; repeated paragraphs were matched to the canonical text, and unmatched view metadata was read. Heading/link normalization was only a comparison aid, not proof of URL or semantic identity. The shared C/02:670–722 and C/07:619–671 UX block was also compared exactly and matched. Paths abbreviated below: **C/** = `input/canonical/`; **A/** = `input/Tesbooboo/docs/specs/agent-synchronized-engineering/`; **B/** = `source/blue/Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/`; **S/** = `source/security/Tesbo-Backend-Nest/src/`. Source citations are to the pinned head above, not a deployment. All 39 files in the provided source manifest and four supplemental files passed local Git-blob hash comparison with their recorded blob SHA. Hash checking establishes cache identity, not whole-file review or runtime correctness. Supplemental files were fetched at that exact commit and are recorded in `source/security/source-manifest.json`.

## 1. Threat-model delta

ASER lets more coding agents and source providers contribute changes, candidates, delegated activity, evidence references and summaries to existing Tesbo owners; these inputs can influence later human review and privileged native writes. Skills introduce a particularly sensitive transition from authored text to an approved, assigned effective version, while composite operations and shared context increase the number of routes that must preserve actual principal identity, current authorization and evidence provenance. The principal threats are agent-to-reviewer authority laundering, candidate content entering trusted context, confidential material leaking through derived views, and reported source/execution claims being promoted beyond what was independently observed.

The target actors are Tesbo human reviewers and project/workspace authorities, coding-agent credentials, delegated actors, independently authenticated provider/CI observers, native jobs, and differently entitled readers. Protected assets are exact approved revisions and assignments, testcase/run/evidence integrity, source-binding identity and scope, private drafts/history, secrets and traces, and durable approval/audit receipts. Nesberry seller roles, marketplace fraud rules, imagery prohibitions, Medusa-specific tests and deployment assumptions are not Tesbo requirements. S8 is applied here to forged provenance, authorization-sensitive enumeration and abuse of review/business transitions.

## 2. Boundaries crossed

| Boundary | Required authorization or integrity decision | Inspected owner / contract | Fails closed? |
|---|---|---|---|
| Browser identity / machine credential → request principal | Bearer cannot inherit a cookie's authority; downstream owner retains actual credential kind | B/auth/auth.middleware.ts:16–39; B/common/request.types.ts:1–19; C/03_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md:99–144 | **Partial.** Middleware separates them; the approval controller discards the distinction. SEC-P01. |
| External client → project MCP discovery and calls | Valid project credential, current membership, explicit project opt-in and client/operation entitlement | B/mcp/mcp.controller.ts:44–60,111–117; B/mcp/mcp.service.ts:129–169,198–257; B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:3906–3929; C/04_MCP_API_CONTRACTS.md:437–485 | **Partial.** Project, membership and read/write scope checks exist; specified opt-in/client gate is absent from the inspected chain. SEC-P02. |
| Authenticated author → approved testcase → new run item | Independent authorized human approves exact current definition; every equivalent writer preserves that decision | B/legacy/legacy.controller.ts:603–611; B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:1821–1860,5999–6047,6295–6316,7250–7305; B/legacy/approved-run-cases.policy.ts:18–42 | **No for the claimed approval invariant.** A status lock does not establish who approved its bytes. SEC-P01. |
| KB content / candidate → search, history and agent context | Per-object exposure and candidate classification apply before every retrieval, independently of instruction authority | B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:11728–11742,11807–11846,12967–13020,21385–21415; B/rag/rag-retrieval.service.ts:106–156,269–280,319–336; A/requirements.md:176–194 | **Unproven for future governed assets.** Existing project-wide reads and draft retrieval cannot themselves supply the new guarantees. SEC-P03. |

Provider-event and source-scope governance for ASER are design gates, not verified implementation. |

**Credential lifecycle observed:** `ApiTokenService` issues project-bound hashed random tokens and exposes raw bytes once, validates only `read`/`write`, rejects invalid/empty scope selections, and deletes tokens to revoke them (B/auth/api-token.service.ts:27–49,69–76,123–175). The inspected authentication lookup has no expiry predicate or instance/client grant evaluation; membership is rechecked by the adapters. Thus canonical instance ceilings, expiry/rotation and current capability policy remain work under T-101–106/T-411, not properties proved by the current token primitive. This does not assert that any deployed credential is compromised.

## 3. Independently derived findings

These are **existing coupled implementation prerequisites** for truthful ASER reuse, not newly invented policies. P-07/FR-033/FR-037, P-18 and the proposal already require the relevant protections. Severity describes the consequence if the inspected path is relied upon as that boundary; no exploit was executed.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **SEC-P01 — Approval can be supplied as mutable status or performed under issuer identity** | S0/S5/S8; **HIGH / HIGH** | **Source-read:** B/mcp/mcp.tools.ts:516–633,775–815; B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:1821–1860,4988–5002,5235,5999–6047,6295–6316; controller:603–611; auth guard:22–54. Requires a valid same-project write token, current issuer membership, a writable project, and an accessible testcase; the exact-approval variant also needs its current revision. | Same-project definition, approval attribution and future run integrity. Invariant: no machine-authored or subsequently changed definition becomes eligible as human-approved solely through write scope, a client status field, or the issuer's user ID. | Preserve actual principal and independent human assent at the native approval owner. Converge generic/legacy/MCP/bulk and indirect definition writers on the existing protected-publication policy; invalidate approval eligibility when approval-relevant content changes. Run admission must resolve a valid approval for the exact eligible revision rather than trust client-writable status alone. |
| **SEC-P02 — Specified MCP consent and client policy are not an enforced request boundary in the inspected source** | S0/S5; **HIGH / HIGH** | **Source-read:** B/mcp/mcp.controller.ts:44–60,80–117; service:129–169,198–257; B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:3906–3929; B/auth/api-token-rest.guard.ts:22–54; B/app.module.ts:57–62. Place this dependency before external I1/I2 context/activity exposure as well as I3 writes. Do not infer it from token issuance, a feature flag display or native UI membership. |
| **SEC-P03 — Conditional KB reuse would expose candidates through existing retrieval paths** | S0/S12; **MEDIUM / HIGH** for source condition, **reasoned** future consequence | **Source-read:** B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:11728–11742,11758–11804,11807–11846,12967–13020,21385–21415; B/rag/rag-retrieval.service.ts:269–280,319–336. Requires the future implementation to store protected skill/manual/policy candidates in the existing KB substrate without changing these readers. | Same-project private draft exposure or unapproved candidate text entering agent context; not a demonstrated present skill-system exploit. Invariant: a candidate omitted from authoritative skill resolution also cannot be surfaced as approved/trusted instruction through another retrieval adapter or disclosed to an unauthorized reader. | Make KB reuse conditional on a shared audience/classification and candidate-state predicate across list/get/history/search/export, embeddings/RAG, selected-document and fallback context. Exclude protected candidates from authoritative context, render review content inert, and keep readable source material explicitly untrusted. Preserve legitimate authorized ordinary-draft workflows. |

### SEC-P01 evidence chain and concrete falsifier

The MCP registry accepts `status` for create, update and bulk. Create/update pass it to native writers under the MCP actor; bulk passes the issuer user ID. The native insert uses `body.status || "Draft"`; ordinary and bulk updates set status directly, and an ordinary content edit can retain `Approved` because an omitted status preserves the old value. The run gate correctly locks same-project case rows, but checks only `status === "Approved"` before snapshot insertion (B/legacy/approved-run-cases.policy.ts:22–41; B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:7253–7295).

Separately, the dedicated approval route passes only `req.userId`. Bearer middleware populates that value from the token issuer, the global REST guard permits a matching-project write token on this suffix, and `approveTestCaseForUser` checks membership plus revision before writing `actor_id=uid`. Its transaction and stale-revision check are real controls; neither establishes that an independently authorized human gave assent. The inspected module/global-guard wiring contains no additional approval-specific human guard (B/app.module.ts:57–62; S/auth/auth.module.ts:34–37; S/legacy/legacy.module.ts:18–33; S/plan-limits/project-write-lock.guard.ts:30–59).

**Falsifier:** a reachable shared server predicate before these writes that retains actual credential kind, enforces current reviewer authority/assent, rejects machine status promotion, and prevents edited/unapproved bytes from entering a new approved-only run. Produce the actual route-to-transaction source and a real API/DB negative test; a disabled UI button, exact hash alone or an unrelated approval endpoint does not falsify the path. Contract anchors: C/00_README.md:25; C/01_PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md:89–94; C/03_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md:103–144; A/requirements.md:35–39,107–111,191–194.

### SEC-P02 evidence chain and concrete falsifier

Both MCP routes use the same service. It verifies token project and issuer membership, then `tools/list` returns the registry and `tools/call` checks only `read`/`write` plus write-plan limits before dispatch. `requireProjectAccess` selects active-project membership in the issuer's workspace; it does not evaluate external MCP enablement or a named client. No such gate was found in this fully inspected controller/service/global-guard chain. This is stronger evidence than a keyword search, but does not prove how an uninspected production gateway is configured.

**Falsifier:** an enforced current project/client decision in that actual request path, demonstrated for both routes, cached sessions, discovery and direct calls after disable/revocation, with no restricted metadata or content returned. Contract anchors: C/00_README.md:36; C/04_MCP_API_CONTRACTS.md:437–485; C/05_IMPLEMENTATION_BACKLOG_AND_TEST_PLAN.md:289–304,961–1002; A/implementation-plan.md:123–126. The canonical sequencing already puts T-411 before external exposure; the integration should preserve it explicitly.

### SEC-P03 evidence chain and concrete falsifier

Current KB list/get/history/search authorize project access and then retrieve project document content. Creation enqueues a new draft for embeddings. RAG excludes unapproved `ai_memory`, but its predicate accepts every other document type regardless of status; `knowledgeSnapshot` applies the same rule, including a fallback that supplies recent documents. Adding a new candidate subtype while guarding only `resolve_effective_skills` would therefore leave alternate delivery paths. The proposal's existing conditional-reuse language and inert-candidate requirements are appropriate; they need a concrete reader inventory and implementation acceptance gate, not another system by default.

**Falsifier:** exact owner/source proof that protected candidate classification and audience are checked at every applicable read/embedding/context path, plus a deliberately malicious/secret-bearing candidate that is absent from unauthorized listings/snippets/history and does not become authoritative context, while an authorized reviewer can inspect it inertly. Contract anchors: C/01_PRODUCT_REQUIREMENTS.md:538–544; C/03_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md:665–724; A/skills-governance.md:17–24,57–60,123–130,160–176. Sanitizing Markdown alone does not establish confidentiality or instruction trust.

## 4. Attack chains

**Source-supported composition, not reproduced:** an authorized write agent creates or edits a case, supplies `Approved` (or retains it after an edit), and then causes the new definition to satisfy the native approved-only run gate. The end state is **HIGH integrity impact**: unreviewed expectations can appear as approved run inputs. It does not itself demonstrate fabricated execution PASS, cross-project compromise or arbitrary code execution.

**Conditional integration composition:** an agent stores a persuasive candidate in an ordinary KB subtype; search/RAG supplies it as context; a downstream agent attempts a privileged publication. SEC-P03 supplies the exposure condition and SEC-P01 demonstrates an existing publication weakness, but actual model compliance and the future candidate implementation were not tested. No Critical chain is established. A read exposure through SEC-P02 remains bounded by the token's project in the inspected chain.

## 5. Closure plan and check status

Each High finding has **one selected closure mechanism: a regression test**. A static rule alone would not prove composed credential, transaction and run-admission behavior. Planned tests are not closure; **SEC-P01 and SEC-P02 remain OPEN**.

| Finding | Selected closure / existing canonical mapping | Required non-vacuous result | Current status |
|---|---|---|---|
| SEC-P01 | **Regression test**, Nest/API integration with a real boot and authorized isolated migrated PostgreSQL; extend existing testcase/MCP/authorization owners. Maps QA-444/445, AC-079, Phase 1 T-103/T-106. | Owner-issued and lower-role-issued machine tokens fail direct exact approval and create/update/bulk status promotion; an approved definition edited afterward is ineligible for a new approved-only run. Assert no forged approval receipt, no unauthorized protected revision or run item; independently authorized human exact approval succeeds, stale revision fails without writes. Cover every inventory-equivalent writer before exposure. | NOT RUN; no production/shared-DB test authorized or attempted by this review. |
| SEC-P02 | **Regression test**, real HTTP MCP/API integration at both routes against the current policy owner; maps QA-180/182/436, T-411 and ASE-FLOW-15. | Establish an allowed positive control, disable external MCP, then attempt initialize/discovery/read/write with the same valid token and prior session. Every protected operation denies without content/existence leakage; native UI access remains governed independently. Re-enable only through authorized policy and verify current client/capability restrictions. | NOT RUN; feature/policy state implementation not established. |
| SEC-P03 | Targeted integration/contract falsifier for KB reuse, maps QA-431/441 and SK-E2E candidate/privacy controls. | A candidate carrying test-only injected instructions and test-only classified markers cannot leak to a non-entitled actor or enter effective approved context through search, history, RAG, fallback or export. Authorized inert review remains possible; revocation invalidates relevant caches. | NOT RUN; required before choosing/activating the conditional substrate extension. |

| Check class | Authority / result |
|---|---|
| **Binding review procedure** | Canonical Security/orchestration/portability methods applied for preparation. Original independent receipt preserved; formal stage dependency remains open. |
| **Binding product contract** | Tesbo P-07/P-11/P-18, FR-010–017/033/037/111–113, NFR-001/005–007/011 and target AGENTS/CLAUDE. These determine what must be enforced; this review cannot amend or waive them. |
| **Proposed contract** | Six ASER files are design inputs, not proof of implementation, approved global skill content or runtime acceptance. Human-only skill governance, evidence tiers, script-default-off and per-item bundle authorization are sound stated constraints. |
| **Source checks completed** | Exact-source adapter/native-owner traces, selected persistence/read predicates, guard wiring and cache-blob hash identity; no unpinned fallback. These are source evidence only. |
| **Advisory checks** | Threat reconstruction, calibrated consequences, falsifiers, rollout dependency placement and reader/writer inventories. Security has no CI, deployment, product-approval or risk-acceptance authority. |
| **NOT RUN** | Runtime penetration, malicious-content execution, real MCP/client interoperability, test suites, migrated DB behavior, concurrency/revocation races, secret scans, dependency scans, rendering/accessibility, deployment policy, provider signatures, production logs/retention and operational incident response. These are not passed or absent merely because not observed. |

No repository-specific Nesberry Semgrep fixture, seller test, live endpoint or marketplace rule was transplanted. The reusable obligations are mapped to Tesbo native auth, object ownership, approval, retrieval, storage and evidence owners.

## 6. Residual risk and unresolved implementation decisions

Even after the selected tests pass, hostile text can evade semantic screening; the proposal correctly treats review, least privilege, inert previews and independent authorization as containment rather than promising perfect prompt-injection detection (A/skills-governance.md:123–130). Provider-attested origin proves an observer's identity and scoped observation, not intended correctness or real product execution. Fixed readiness scopes and append-only activity are useful only if native evidence and source/build pins are independently resolved.

Before implementation, owner decisions are still required for approved-skill root/bootstrap, exact composition/no-assignment policy, protected audiences and retention, source-attestor trust/scope, and quantitative payload/depth/rate/batch limits (A/skills-governance.md:112–118; A/requirements.md:201–204). No invented quota or Nesberry operational policy closes these gaps. A disabled optional hook cannot be the only monitor or the reason to fabricate an absence/pass claim.

One source-governance question needs explicit disposition: the source-binding endpoint passes only `request.userId` to an owner-role writer (B/behavior-graph/graph-change-review.controller.ts:24–35; management.ts:72–126). A write token owned by a project owner is not excluded by the current REST control-plane suffix list. The server checkout allowlist prevents arbitrary client filesystem roots, but who may change the selected repository/ref/prefix scope must be deliberately authorized before calling resulting observations independent. This is **not** classified as a demonstrated violation of an unstated universal human-only binding policy; the existing skills-specific human rule must not be silently generalized to all native operations.

## 7. Verdict at this stage

**Formal post-design Security verdict: NOT RUN / WITHHELD pending White pre-gate and corrected snapshot handoff.** This preparatory receipt is not a `NO-FINDINGS`, release approval or certification. The two High source prerequisites remain open; the driver should not claim that reusing the existing approval or MCP boundary already meets the canonical contract. Proposed mitigations and falsifiers are concrete, but no regression or accepted residual-risk receipt closes them.

## 8. False-positive log / controls actually found

These are **source counterexamples**, not runtime attack attempts:

- Mixed cookie/bearer escalation is explicitly prevented by the mutually exclusive middleware branches (B/auth/auth.middleware.ts:16–39).
- Omitted token scopes default to read only; empty, malformed and unknown scopes are rejected. The historical broad-default claim is not true of this head (B/auth/api-token.service.ts:38–49).
- Both MCP routes require a bearer; foreign-project tokens and removed issuers fail in the shared handler (B/mcp/mcp.controller.ts:111–117; service.ts:142–159). This blocks an anonymous/cross-project formulation of SEC-P02.
- REST token calls cannot mint more credentials, edit membership or administer the project root; method-based read/write gates and current membership are explicit (B/auth/api-token-rest.guard.ts:30–54). The narrower approval bypass remains because the approval suffix is not protected there.
- Approved-run admission does reject an ordinary same-project Draft case atomically, locks rows and repeats the same-project Approved predicate in insertion (B/legacy/approved-run-cases.policy.ts:22–41; service.ts:7253–7295). The finding concerns how Approved is acquired or retained, not an absent run gate.
- Exact-revision approval rejects a stale definition and inserts its receipt in the same transaction (B/legacy/legacy.service.ts:1825–1853). Revision integrity and approver authority are distinct requirements.
- Source binding rejects arbitrary fields, absolute/traversing selection prefixes and unapproved checkout/repository pairs; owner membership is rechecked at the write (B/behavior-graph/project-source-binding.management.ts:80–126; S/behavior-graph/project-source-binding.ts:39–42). No arbitrary-server-path or SSRF exploit is established.
- Approved skill text explicitly does not enable executable scripts or network/tool privileges, and semantic maliciousness is not promised to be perfectly detectable (A/skills-governance.md:123–130,160–176). These are positive design constraints, not executed controls.

## 9. Side findings, coverage and limits

No unrelated defect was repaired or promoted to an ASER blocker. The living baseline's historical run/UI/integrity observations were treated as dated evidence, not retested defects or current deployment truth. Its last substantive update is 28 October 2026 and last authenticated Run read 20 October; deployed SHA remains unverified (`input/Tesbo_Existing_Application_Baseline_v1.0.md:4–12,69–76,757–767`). Its separation of Approved case design, actual execution and reference-versus-evidence bytes is retained.

**Read coverage:** all six ASER files and target policies in full; orchestration and Security charter in full, and applicable privacy/security guard with portability boundaries. Across the eight canonical documents, this was an explicit security-focused primary-section sample: foundational principles/actors/auth/publication, default-pack/skills/history, MCP consent and contracts, source/graph trust, workflow/UX denial states, agent invariants, implementation dependencies and QA-177–183/424–447. Relevant whole primary sections were read rather than relying only on matching search lines; this is not a claim that every paragraph of the eight documents or 26k-line legacy service was audited. The complete controller/service/guard token chain and the cited native write/read paths were inspected; adjacent implementation, database triggers beyond the inspected owners, deployment configuration and operational controls remain outside verified coverage.

**No live mutations, code fixes, migrations, GitHub writes, messages to third parties, or method changes.** The only created review artifacts are this receipt and the pinned supplemental source cache/manifest. Later design/source corrections require a separate delta receipt; this initial account must remain unchanged.

**Reviewer:** `/root/white`, independently dispatched analytical gate reviewer, not the specification author or an appointed operational/policy authority.  
**Review area:** Tesbo ASER integration, R-ASE-01–48, SK-E2E-01–28 and ASE-FLOW-01–16, including affected existing native and canonical workflows.  
**Gate:** PRE-ARCHITECTURE.  
**Verdict:** **BLOCKED — DUPLICATE/UNCLEAR OWNERSHIP.** Additional incomplete-archaeology and formal-quorum conditions are recorded below.  
**Bounded remediation:** **In-place specification corrections may proceed under the existing user authorization, as proposed remediation for independent re-review.** This is not PASS TO DESIGN, implementation admission, canonical-policy ratification, or any final-freeze verdict. Architecture/package freeze and unconditional UI freeze remain withheld.

## 1. Context, revision and gate authority

The examined proposal is the six original ASER files in `input/Tesbooboo/docs/specs/agent-synchronized-engineering/` at `1alextest/Tesbooboo@0c54b8ed28c9dec51ed0ca3bbb3cd3b7b8ab12d8` (PR149 input), with development baseline `11fdbe70e5b63c6049b1dc0331be88daae0c62dc`. The governing primary package is the exact eight-file Tesbo v1.4.27 original archive, SHA-256 `6e6c3c08efcf91615146925a6d96c6d91069875f51014a2f62a7d5703e07f681`, identified in `canonical-manifest.json` and extracted under `input/canonical/`. Its opening status is NOT FROZEN; later proposed sections do not automatically ratify their open decisions.

Methodology is the unchanged canonical orchestration and White charter v1.6 at `NesberryEnterprise/Nesberry@d83549c85a4dcd047fb40dc4fba4ec62737ab0fc`; respective Git blobs `86f3489471f30f6cf1b3a7b1e87088979aa8b3ce` and `5eca7bd27074cabe1553a9ec61ac7e28ce412fb1`. Both were read fully. Applicable Blue/Red membership clauses and Grey FJCC, evidence-tier and Major-finding membership clauses were also inspected. This receipt applies the method; it does not amend it.

**Development stage and evidence boundary:** specification/design adequacy and integration with an existing application. Evidence is primary-document inspection, selected pinned implementation inspection, receipt reconciliation and explicitly conceptual reasoning. No application test, database request, runtime MCP call, browser operation, graphical comparison, target execution, penetration test or external-client interoperability test was performed by White. Current runtime/build/schema identity, authentic current graphical baseline and applicable human operational approvals have not been supplied. Their absence is not proof that the proposed implementation is defective or that every reusable producer is absent.

White's authority is to inspect prerequisites and withhold a gate. It cannot select a product policy, invent a schema, substitute for a specialist, or grant a human approval. Root's neutral dispatch and progress/handoff messages supplied locations, scope and timing; their suggestions or intended corrections are not findings. `working/`, prior standalone review reports and later corrections were not read. Historical review narratives embedded within the required primary specifications were treated as dated records, not inherited current verdicts.

## 3. Portability, target policy and scope

**Methodology transfer receipt:** NOT REQUIRED for a changed canonical obligation, because none was proposed or changed. Application of the unchanged method to this evidence is not a proof of framework-wide or runtime portability. No transferable-effectiveness percentage is claimed.

| Instruction kind | White disposition for Tesbo |
|---|---|
| Core principle | Single accountable owner; prerequisites before approval; exact provenance; independent acceptance; truthful unknowns; problem-space adequacy and evidence at the claim boundary apply unchanged. |
| Capability/risk condition | Persistence, multiple writers/principals, asynchronous callbacks/retries, confidential records, existing UI and history are evidenced here, so corresponding checks apply. Absence of runtime access does not make them N/A. |
| Application policy | Tesbo P-01, P-05, P-07, P-14, P-17, P-18 and P-20, its repository instructions and dated approved decisions control. Nesberry marketplace axes, payment/relationship vocabulary, founder identity and local authority hierarchy are not imported. |
| Technical binding | Tesbo's pinned native services, canonical FR/AC/T/QA/UI contracts and actual test/environment rules supply the bindings. Nesberry commands or an advertised future checker do not become executed Tesbo proof. |
| Example/origin evidence | Vendor hooks, named coding agents, the DMIS pilot and Nesberry incidents are examples or optional integrations. They cannot force a new platform dependency or make sampled results universal. |

The target's axes include project/workspace, artifact kind, audience, confidentiality, origin/authority, actor/principal, draft/revision, approval/assignment/effectivity, source/build/deployment, evidence tier and obligation/completion. The receipt package uses these instead of imposing Nesberry's taxonomy. Brand deletion leaves real jobs and native domain facts; no independent ASER authority earns existence solely from its label.

The canonical v1.4.19–21 additions explicitly retain owner-review status and unresolved decisions. Existing protected publication remains human-controlled under P-07; installing or reading a pack does not approve artifacts or enable MCP. Original external testing/report/retest and human review remain in scope alongside code-first change synchronization. The draft may not replace that job with a cycle-linked verification bundle and call the integration complete. These are target-source obligations, not new White requirements. [Canonical 00 P-07/P-14/P-17/P-18, v1.4.19–21; 06 goal/invariants/complete demonstration; Grey §3/GI-01.]

White's own source checks corroborate that the proposed reuse needs precise qualification: a status-only run gate is narrower than exact reviewed-definition eligibility; coalesced draft history is narrower than immutable submission identity; a project-keyed mutable source binding does not by itself prove multi-source historical identity; a legitimate Bug association can invoke a separate native result mutation. This receipt retains these as open source/design integration evidence, not executed exploits or independently certified production failures. Existing locks, exact approval receipts, scope checks, source capture and sealed-run protections are counterevidence against claims of wholly absent protection.

## 5. White's independent FJCC check

The handed-off inventory is derived from actors/jobs, surfaces, requirements/lifecycle and sampled actual implementation. It retains native and external entry paths, zero-case exploration/reporting, seven ASER domains, review/publication, source/impact, release/aftercare, model/trace, sharing/history, migration and access changes. Persona labels do not grant permissions. White found no basis in this bounded review to replace that inventory with the 16 ASE-FLOW examples or a requirement count.

White mechanically reconciled the sealed row IDs and counts against preparation: **49 distinct unchanged IDs, 43 high-risk and 6 medium-risk; 37 G0_SIMULATED and 12 G1_CODE_GROUNDED; 24 HOLDS, 21 INCONCLUSIVE and 4 FAILS; zero G2/G3 observations.** Each row has a separate declared status and outcome. These numbers validate ledger accounting only. G1 here is code-grounded reasoning, not execution.

The four-source inventory and explicit branch limitations are sufficient to expose material pre-gate questions. They are **not sufficient to mark full journey assurance complete**. QA's independent oracle challenge, Blue/Security all-entrance reconciliation, Purple's navigation/accessibility/current-UI evidence and Graph's lineage challenge are not supplied at this stage. Every high-risk row has a bounded trace, but the universal writer/read, race, recovery and visual obligations in Grey §5 remain unexecuted or incomplete.

Two reconciliations must survive remediation:

- Grey GJ-28 `HOLDS` confirms the stated bounded intent of partial recovery. It does not close EG-02's objection that receipt retrieval versus controlled resume, aggregate terminal conditions and changed dependencies are not yet a complete deterministic contract. Keep EG-02 open for an exact corrected-revision review; do not convert a narrative branch into a specified request/state protocol.
- Grey GT-02 adds a concrete side-effect route to the protected-writer evidence. White read the same primary code, but this is not the independent QA/journey-review disposition required to confirm a Major finding. Its canonical direct/side-result conflict remains a gate to any claim that existing Bug/result reuse is sufficient unchanged. Root is not authorized by this receipt to perform a code fix.

## 6. Blocking reasons at this pre-architecture gate

### W-PRE-01 — Single canonical ownership is not yet determined for material proposed facts

ASER design §§3–4 and 10 supplies useful candidate owner labels and explicitly illustrative logical entities. It does not settle their identity/cardinality/lifecycle against T-422/FR-076–077 and FR-106–114, common proposal/review/apply FR-027–037, FR-115 changesets, FR-119 QA impacts, FR-081/T-426 links, native tasks/Bugs and FR-117/C-12 expected-model authority. “Native graph/link services,” “unified task service” or a view name cannot decide which authored fact wins if records disagree.

Canonical D-14 and the v1.4.19 UI instruction explicitly extend the existing Settings/QA Standards family. A Skills label or separate permissioned view is not inherently a duplicate store; equally, “reuse” wording does not establish compliance with that placement. Source observations/captures must remain distinct from accepted expected-model revisions. The primary contract already defines both. [ASER design §§3–4,7,10; canonical 01 FR-027–037/076–077/106–124; 03 663–824/C-12; 07 D-14/v1.4.19; B01/R01/EG-01; Grey W-C2.]

**Gate consequence:** no freeze of new persistent concepts, aggregate authority, schemas or conflicting navigation. A reviewable clause-to-canonical-owner/identity/lifecycle/producer/consumer allocation and the applicable negative design proof are required. White does not choose that allocation here. An unresolved owner is not an acceptable “named gate” hidden inside a passing verdict.

### W-PRE-02 — Material lifecycle and semantic contracts remain nonunique

Packet status and per-domain dispositions are not yet bound to the canonical terminal/rejected/withdrawn/partial operation model. The original proposal's same-key replay versus resumable partial work needs an exact disposition under changed permission, skill or source context. R-ASE-40 and design §14 use substantively different discrepancy vocabularies without a mapping. The source/decision scope for one change in different releases and a reconfigured source binding also remains open. These affect what a downstream reviewer may rely on, not only prose style. [ASER R-ASE-02/13/37/39/40/42; design §§4/6/13–15; canonical 01 FR-030/032/035, 02 §4.3, 04 §4.5; EG-02/04; R04; Grey GJ25/26/28/35/36.]

**Gate consequence:** corrective draft work must make the existing owner contracts and remaining decisions explicit, with discriminating rejected-only, partially accepted, lost-acknowledgment, dependency-drift and independently scoped acceptance outcomes. No new aggregate state machine is approved by this gate. The SK-E2E range mismatch is a bounded reference correction; fixing it alone closes none of these material questions.

### W-PRE-03 — Necessary current-owner evidence and migration boundaries remain incomplete

Blue/Red and the checked source demonstrate concrete differences between current case/KB/source behavior and the proposed invariants. Complete affected writer/reader inventories, general-task fit, file/report-byte paths, guide/workspace provisioning and current consumer effects remain partly uninspected. Source absence is not established repository-wide. Reusing current code unchanged cannot be presumed sufficient; building a new subsystem because proof is missing is equally unsupported. [B02–B05, R02–R04, EG-03; Grey GT-01–03/W-C3–4; direct primary checks in §2.]

**Gate consequence:** carry exact discovery/extension/migration prerequisites and unexposed/deferred boundaries into the corrected candidate. The architectural claim needs an evidenced owner and feasible enforcement/migration contract; future implementation admission needs actual denied-and-authorized-success tests. White does not demand implementation of an intentionally proposed feature merely to review a draft, and does not count source-only counterexamples as runtime certification.

### W-PRE-04 — Formal prerequisites and authority decisions cannot be supplied by inference

Blue/Red formal quorum is unmet; Grey's Major findings lack independent second review. The canonical package also leaves requirement/AC authority, provisional oracle, human model/asset publication, source attestor, QA assignment/closure, proof thresholds, retention/build policy and relevant operational ownership unratified. Skills bootstrap/recovery, no-active-skill behavior and composition decisions remain explicit in the ASER proposal. Analysts' role names do not appoint those authorities. [Blue/Red Membership; Grey Membership; canonical 00 v1.4.21, 03 844–863, 05 T-454; ASER skills §11/12.5.]

**Gate consequence:** obtain actual member dispositions under the unchanged membership rules and preserve product decisions with their accountable human owner and status. A recommendation may be drafted and challenged; it cannot become decided ground or authorize an operational effect merely through reviewer agreement. Missing runtime permission is not a reason to stop this authorized document remediation.

These are dependencies on stronger claims, not waivers of the pre-gate blocks. The driver may add evidence and request dispositions; it may not silently mark a specialist gate closed.

| **W-C1 — Original external QA loop** | Preserve and later demonstrate the canonical zero-case/no-native-AI-key task→assigned executor/own driver→original report bytes→analysis/retest→exact human decisions and selected apply. Existing coding-change and cycle-linked bundles do not establish this. Implementation/client completion withheld. |
| **W-C2 — Canonical owner/status convergence** | Resolve W-PRE-01/02 at exact revisions, including candidate versus impact, approval versus assignment/effectivity, authored versus observed, independent source/release scope and native requirement authority. Schema/architecture freeze withheld. |
| **W-C3 — Protected writers/readers and independent challenge** | Blue/Security/QA reconcile every material entry/consumer and independently disposition GT-01–03 with discriminating oracles and permitted positive cases. Candidate readability must remain distinct from activation. No claim of complete enforcement or Major closure from one new facade. |
| **W-C4 — Runtime, storage and operations** | Authorized tests at a known build/schema prove the applicable native DB/API/MCP/queue/byte/history/retry/concurrency/provider/client outcomes, with exact selection and unexecuted scope. All 49 journeys currently lack live observation. A simulation or docs check does not satisfy this gate; no universal requirement for an irrelevant browser/adapter is invented. |
| **W-C5 — Authentic current UI and usable completion** | For every materially impacted existing surface, record source SHA/owner/API/permissions, current build, role, fixture, viewport/browser/theme, authentic Before, approved changed/unchanged scope, and real After interaction/visual/keyboard/responsive comparison. Current status: SOURCE-INSPECTED / VISUAL-BASELINE BLOCKED. Source-inspired mocks and historic screenshots cannot close it. Architecture exploration may continue; unconditional UI freeze cannot. |
| **W-C6 — Quorum, coverage and delta control** | Satisfy actual Blue/Red membership and Grey independent Major review; reconcile inventory, all high-risk branches and QA oracles with the applicable specialists; retain omitted/not-run scope. Every changed artifact gets a separate delta review before its evidence can support freeze. Full-assurance completion withheld. |

**Graph status:** NOT RUN / NOT HANDED OFF at this pre-gate. Graph closeout is required after applicable candidate reviews. Its eventual receipt must state verifier, revision, mode (manual assessment versus implemented mechanical execution), evidence and remaining limitations. A planned Graph checker, a link count or an existing product graph is not a clean assurance closeout.

**Final White status:** NOT ISSUED in this receipt. The later final gate must reconcile exact corrected hashes, Green/Blue/Red/Security/Purple/QA/Grey and required pre-freeze EG dispositions, Graph closeout, legacy treatment and mutually traceable requirements/design/tasks. Specialist binding/advisory/not-run status must be explicit. No current receipt supplies runtime, visual, release or human operational approval by implication.

## 8. Permitted next step and stop boundary

The already authorized driver may make **bounded, reviewable, in-place corrections to the existing specification/proposal owners**: clarify mappings and evidence limits, remove contradictions, define the unresolved contract sufficiently for reviewers to assess it, preserve canonical IDs/history, and state proposed decisions and their gates. This is remediation of the evidence under review, not passage of Gate D. It does not authorize changing approved P-07/P-14/P-18 policy, implementing a new primitive, modifying product code/live records or replacing the canonical eight-file package with an ASER authority.

Any promotion of a new proposal obligation into the authoritative product contract must follow the original eight-file ownership/approval boundary. Existing user authorization for specification editing does not itself answer an open product decision. Corrected files must carry new hashes and receive the required independent delta and final-stage reviews; original receipts remain immutable. A false positive may be disposed with evidence in a separate receipt rather than rewriting the original finding.

**Why this is blocked rather than PASS WITH EXPLICIT GATES:** there is enough evidence to preserve the problem and prepare concrete corrective text, but material identities, lifecycle convergence and formal prerequisites are still unresolved. The White charter permits named choices to remain during design only when they are explicit dependencies rather than competing sources of truth. The current evidence does not satisfy that distinction for the complete proposed increment.

**Final pre-gate disposition:** **BLOCKED — DUPLICATE/UNCLEAR OWNERSHIP; bounded specification remediation may proceed; freeze and implementation admission remain withheld.** White has not designed the remedy, waived another role or converted a conceptual test into executed proof.

**End of immutable initial pre-gate receipt.**

# ASER/Skills Governance — Source-anchored Spec Challenge, 18 November 2026

**Status:** Preliminary reviewer-operated, **charter-guided DESK ANALYSIS**, not an independently delegated assurance team run; no code change, authenticated MCP, browser execution, live service, or E2E test. **No formal team verdicts or White sign-off are claimed.**
**Target:** `1alextest/Tesbooboo` PR #146, branch `spec/agent-synchronized-engineering-20261118`, specification as revised 2026-11-18. This review records issues against the **specification**, not defects in the assurance teams or a proof that implementations are faulty.
**Sources actually read:** five ASER documents plus `skills-governance.md`; Tesbo `development` MCP tool source and `docs/reviews/governed-asset-evolution-reconciliation.md`; Nesberry canonical `.kiro/steering/teams/README.md`, `review-orchestration.md`, Red/Blue/Green/White/QA/Grey/Elephant-Gold charters, Purple mandate and Graph Assurance requirements; Security charter identified in `.claude/agents/security-team.md`. Application profile is Tesbo, not Nesberry. No copied Nesberry policy is assumed binding to Tesbo.

## Evidence and execution integrity

The request is to run the full assurance teams independently. This session exposes GitHub and read-only document access, but **no actual agent-team dispatch runner**. Browser Use independent cold-review attempt failed for insufficient credits; external research/reviewer invocation was declined by the tool approval flow and was not retried. Therefore per-team rows below are **charter-mapped inspection observations by this reviewer**, not delegated executions by independent Red/Blue/White/etc. No verdict count, agent quorum, Graph harness output, fresh-eyes independence, test execution or cross-application proof is fabricated.

## Run context / known grounding

- Actor contexts: coding agent, optionally delegated specialist agent, QA/BA/document/policy/skills reviewers, interactive human skill-governance administrator, project users, external integration/provider. Machine identity and token-owning human identity are distinct for governance.
- Existing owners in Tesbo: `mcp.tools.ts` exposes native case CRUD and KB document CRUD, external Jira/Linear/Notion testcase link; original `docs/FEATURE_DOCUMENTATION.md` catalog and Zyra task/KB/graph routes are reuse candidates, not evidence of implemented Skills Library. A prior governed-asset evolution proposal explicitly includes skills with native versioning; it is **not canonical implementation**. The original eight Tesbo 1.4.18 specs remain authoritative until reviewed reconciliation.
- Evidence tier: SOURCE/DESK only. UI source available but no authenticated actual interface reconstruction. Deployed MCP client still may have 50 rather than the 58 tools seen in source; deployment identity UNKNOWN.

| ID | Risk / source section | Counterexample / falsifier | Challenge role(s) | Severity | Disposition |

If no draft iteration/abandon mechanism, it must create duplicate stable skill IDs or become stuck. | QA, Grey | HIGH | **Addressed**: explicit immutable candidate *iterations* and withdrawal; unique logical identity, no in-place rewrite, candidate base/version preconditions. |
| ASER-RV-03 | Skills §§3,6 and ASER design §6: human-only approval promised, but no proof of a distinct **machine vs interactive session** enforcement boundary in existing Tesbo services. | Project owner mints API token; agent calls same REST route as web approval with a bearer principal and a forged `humanApproved:true`. | Security, Red, QA | CRITICAL | **Guard specified; implementation unproven**. Forbid approval via all machine principals; require interactive trusted server auth + independent transition test. No claim current system has this protection. |
